A line of standard input shorter than 80 characters is padded with spaces to the record; a longer line is CONSECUTIVE records, each padded — never one oversized transfer. The receiving size GR3/GR4 compare against is the receiver's character positions: the PICTURE length for a character receiver, the slice length for a reference-modified one, the digit count plus a SIGN SEPARATE position for a fixed-point numeric one, the group's character positions for a group, the MAXIMUM size for a dynamic-length one (D-DL2), the ARGUMENT's length for an ANY LENGTH one (§13.18.2.4 GR1 b); kb/Work PB1013), and exactly one record for a floating-point one (item 1) — one reader, `ReceivingStore.CharacterPositions`. A larger receiver requests further records until filled (GR4a, left-aligned); a smaller one keeps the leftmost characters and ignores the rest of the record AND the line's remaining records (GR4b); end-of-input leaves the unfilled remainder as spaces; a zero-length receiver consumes one record and ignores all of it (GR4b's closing sentence). | `conformance-test:AcceptDifferentialTests.Device_LongLine_SplitsAtRecordBoundary`; `conformance-test:AcceptDifferentialTests.Device_WideReceiver_ReadsAdditionalRecords`; `conformance-test:AcceptDifferentialTests.Device_AnyLengthReceiver_TransfersTheArgumentsSize`; `unit:AcceptSourceTests.ZeroLengthReceiver_ConsumesItsRecord` |
| DOC-A.1-18 | **CANCEL statement — result of canceling an active program when EC-PROGRAM-CANCEL-ACTIVE is not enabled**, §14.9.5 GR5, required + documented | The program is **not canceled and the run unit terminates** with the fatal `EC-PROGRAM-CANCEL-ACTIVE` raise (kb/Work PB154): `ProgramTable.CancelNode` throws before any state mutation regardless of the checking state — GR5's implementor-defined not-enabled arm is the SAME disposition as the enabled arm, so a program cannot observe a half-canceled active target either way. With checking enabled a USE declarative can take the condition and RESUME (pinned by `2023/pb154_cancel_active`). | `conformance:2023/pb154_cancel_active` |
| DOC-A.1-19 | **CANCEL statement — result of canceling a non-COBOL program**, §14.9.5, required + documented | **No effect** (kb/Work PB154). A name that does not resolve to a registered WiseOwl COBOL unit is the GR7 no-op, and CANCEL never probes sibling modules (the probe is a CALL-side locate step; running it on CANCEL loaded assemblies and cached the miss). A foreign (non-COBOL) DLL on disk is therefore never touched by CANCEL. There is no CALL-CONVENTION directive (§7.3.9) — no non-COBOL program can be declared, so no richer effect is definable yet. ⚠ **This determination does not, and today cannot, answer §14.9.5.4 GR10's own question**, which governs a program the runtime system LOCATED per GR2: `ProgramTable.Cancel` resolves every target with `probe: false` and returns on a null node, so no locate step runs and the named module's language is never consulted — `CANCEL "Cobol.Net.Runtime"` and `CANCEL "L1NOSUCH"` take the byte-identical path. The branch is therefore UNREACHABLE from COBOL source, which is why the inventory row records CONFORMS with an empty `test-ref` and stays a GAP: a golden written against it would be invariant under the determination it claimed to measure (one was drafted in golden-lane round 2 and deleted for exactly that reason). Closing it needs either a `unit:` harness that deploys a companion non-COBOL module and drives `ProgramTable.Cancel` directly, or the unobservable-rule closure path kb/Work PB386 puts to the owner. | — |
| DOC-A.1-56 | **DISPLAY statement — conversion of data**, §14.9.11.4 GR1, required + documented | Each operand converts to its character image (kb/Work PB148 completed the register): a **PICTURE-bearing numeric** item renders its PICTURE-digit image (digits the description declares, the decimal point and sign per the description; a BinaryCapacity item's beyond-PICTURE value renders the PICTURE-digit image, the value modulo 10^digits (owner decision R13: follow GnuCOBOL; kb/Work PB825 retired against it), pinned by `comp5_display_beyond_picture`); a **signed item of a non-DISPLAY usage** (COMP/COMP-3/COMP-5/BINARY-*) has no zoned overpunch to render, so it prints a **leading `-` when negative and no sign character otherwise** — a VARIABLE-width form (digits, or `-`+digits), pinned by `pb148_display_forms`; a **PICTURE-less float** (COMP-1/COMP-2/FLOAT-*) renders the invariant-culture shortest-round-trip image (`CobolFloat.Display` — the same image a function result uses, item 92); **alphanumeric/edited/national/boolean** items transfer their character content verbatim (the UTF-16 repertoire is the device repertoire; a boolean prints its '0'/'1' characters); a **numeric literal**, and the constant-name that substitutes one (§13.10.4 GR1), displays **as written** (kb/Work PB1643): its sign as written, its trailing zeros and its decimal separator the PROGRAM's, so `DISPLAY 1,5` under DECIMAL-POINT IS COMMA prints `1,5` (§12.3.7.4 GR14 a: the comma is "the character written in numeric literals to represent the decimal separator"; a contained program inherits the clause, §12.3.4 GR1), and a floating-point literal prints its significand the same way (`1,5E3`). The §14.9.11.4 GR1 latitude is settled GnuCOBOL's way (precedence tiebreaker): GnuCOBOL 3.2.0 prints the literal as written, with the program's separator, in its `default`, `cobol85`, `cobol2002`, `cobol2014`, `xopen` and `mf` configurations, and only the vendor configurations `ibm`, `mvs`, `bs2000` and `acu` (`pretty-display: no`) print digits only; WiseOwl COBOL follows the first group. The value the numeric decoders read stays the canonical dot-decimal text (`BoundNumericLiteral.Text`); the image is `BoundNumericLiteral.Image`. Class **object, pointer and index** operands (and the word NULL) are compile-time rejections (`COBOLNET1694` — §14.9.11.3 SR1 / §13.18.60.3 SR10), never converted. The variable-length-group format is A.1 item 57. ⛔ **A strongly-typed group with a class pointer/object leaf** (the only conforming spelling of such a group, §13.18.60.3 SR14) is a legal identifier-1 — §14.9.11.3 SR1 bars only an item OF class message-tag, object or pointer, and a strongly-typed group's class is its type-name (§8.5.2.1) — and **displays its STORAGE image: each pointer leaf contributes its 8 positions holding the pointer's storage image of A.1 item 216 (NULL as eight zero positions, an address as its binary token — kb/Work PB1071; they were 8 reserved spaces before), each object leaf its 8 reserved placeholder positions as SPACES, never the managed reference** (kb/Work PB244). This is the byte extent D-SLOT reserves for the leaf in a byte-addressed storage area, so the group shows the same characters from a record struct as from a BASED/ADDRESS-OF cell, and `FUNCTION LENGTH` equals the displayed width. GnuCOBOL (the precedence tiebreaker) displays a group's raw storage, including a pointer's address bytes, and so now does WiseOwl COBOL, with the token of item 216 standing for the address. The same one-way image is a MOVE's sending group (§14.9.25.4 GR4) and an intrinsic function's string argument (§15.3 item 2 treats a strongly-typed group as class and category alphanumeric unless a function prohibits it, as MAX, MIN, ORD-MAX and ORD-MIN do; `FUNCTION UPPER-CASE(G)` reads these same characters — kb/Work PB1901, `conformance:2002/pb1901_strong_group_pointer_leaf_string_argument`). Comparison of two such groups and every read-back still refuse it, because the image is neither injective (an object leaf's placeholder is the same for every object) nor invertible (a pointer's token decodes to no reference). | `conformance:2002/pb148_display_forms`; `conformance:2023/comp5_display_beyond_picture`; `conformance:2002/pb244_pointer_group_transfer`; `conformance:85/pb1643_display_literal_decimal_comma`; `conformance:2002/pb1643_display_literal_comma_constant_invoke` |
| DOC-A.1-58 | **DISPLAY statement — size of data transfer**, §14.9.11 GR2, required + documented | **Unbounded.** The standard display device (a byte stream) imposes no transfer size: every DISPLAY statement's operands are joined into ONE sending item and written in a single stream operation (GR4's device-accommodates arm is always taken; no operand splitting, no line folding). Implemented in `AcceptDisplayEmitter.EmitDisplay`. | `conformance:2023/l1_display_transfer_size` |
| DOC-A.1-59 | **DISPLAY statement — standard display device**, §14.9.11 GR8, required + documented | When the UPON phrase is omitted the standard display device is the process **standard output** stream. The output-capable SPECIAL-NAMES device-names are **CONSOLE** and **SYSOUT** (→ standard output) and **SYSERR** (→ standard error); a mnemonic bound to an input-only device (e.g. SYSIN) fails §14.9.11.3 SR2 (`COBOLNET0817`). The device-names and their capabilities are ONE table, `ImplementorNames` (item **189**). Implemented in `AcceptDisplayBinder` (`BindDisplayUpon` / `MnemonicDevice`) + `AcceptDisplayEmitter.EmitDisplay`. | `conformance:2023/l1_display_standard_device`; `conformance:negative/l1-display-upon-input-only-device` |
| DOC-A.1-62 | **Dynamic-length elementary items — maximum length**, §8.5.1.10.1 / §13.18.19.4 GR2, required + documented | **1,073,741,791 characters** (`0x3FFFFFDF`) for every dynamic-length elementary item, alphanumeric or national. §8.5.1.10.1 makes the maximum size "the smallest of" three candidates and this is the third of them, "the maximum permitted by the implementor"; §13.18.19.4 GR2 makes it the whole answer when no LIMIT phrase is written. The value is the CARRIER's own ceiling and nothing else: a dynamic-length item IS a native .NET `System.String`, whose maximum length is 0x3FFFFFDF characters. A dynamic-capacity table's maximum is NOT this rule: item 60's constant (`CobolDynTable<T>.MaxOccurrences`) is about half the .NET array-length limit, chosen so its doubling growth never overflows a 32-bit length, and its runtime resource bound is kb/Work PB1410. A LIMIT phrase BELOW it is the maximum size; a LIMIT phrase above it cannot raise the maximum and is reported (COBOLNET2027, a warning — §13.18.19.3 states no rule bounding integer-1 unless a dynamic-length-structure-name is written too, SR4, so the entry is legal). The second candidate, "the largest integer that can be stored in an item of the usage specified in the PREFIXED phrase", applies when the item names a dynamic-length-structure-name whose clause has a PREFIXED phrase (kb/Work PB829; determination D-DL3 in §3): SHORT PREFIXED 65535, SIGNED SHORT PREFIXED 32767, and the 32-bit fields, both above this maximum, change nothing. ONE producer — `CobolDynString.MaxSizeOf` — computes it, and `DataItem.DynMaxSize` carries it to every emitter, so no site can see "no maximum". ⚠ A request ABOVE the maximum takes §14.9.39.4 GR38's clamp with EC-STORAGE-NOT-AVAIL, and the maximum is a ceiling on the SIZE, not a promise the host can allocate it — GR38's third leg covers that and is implemented (§4 above). | `unit:CobolDynStringTests.MaxSizeOf_NoLimitPhrase_IsTheImplementorMaximum`; `conformance-test:DynamicLengthTests.ImplementorMaximum_IsTheNumberPublishedInConformanceSection7`; `conformance:2023/pb463_dyn_implementor_maximum` |
| DOC-A.1-64 | **ENTRY-CONVENTION clause (entry-convention-names, their meanings and the default when not specified)**, §11.9.7.4 GR2/GR3/GR4, optional (documented if provided) | **Not provided.** WiseOwl COBOL defines NO entry-convention-name-1: the one entry convention is COBOL, the convention §11.9.7.4 GR4 c makes the default when the clause is not specified and GR2 names when it is. Every runtime element - program, contained program, function, method - is activated through the one uniform entry ABI (`ICobolProgram.Call(CobolArg[] args, ManagedPointer? returning)`, reached through `ProgramTable.CallProgram`), with the §8.3.2.2 program-name mapping (case-insensitive, `ExternalizedNames.Same`, A.1 item 68). `ENTRY-CONVENTION IS COBOL` is accepted where §11.9.7.3 SR1 permits the clause; `ENTRY-CONVENTION IS <any other name>` is **COBOLNET2385** (GR3 - a name this implementation gives no meaning is refused by name, never silently activated with the COBOL convention), and so is the clause in a contained program, a method, or a FACTORY / OBJECT paragraph (SR1) - kb/Work PB232. | `conformance:negative/pb232-entry-convention-name`; `conformance:2014/pb232_entry_convention_cobol` |
| DOC-A.1-158 | **Reference format — rightmost character position of the program-text area (margin R)**, §6.3, required + documented | **Margin R is immediately to the right of character position 72**, i.e. the fixed-form program-text area is columns **8–72**. Characters beyond it are not part of the program text and are ignored — they are not an error (§6.3.4; comment-text likewise runs only "up to margin R"). Note §6.3.1 makes this position *implementor-defined*: the standard does **not** mandate 72, and ISO 2023 has no "identification area" (that was a COBOL-85 card-image convention). Columns 1–6, the sequence number area, are **optional** and may hold any character (§6.3.2) — a blank sequence area is ordinary fixed-form source. Free-form reference format (§6.2) is not column-bounded and is unaffected. **The initial reference format is FIXED form** — §7.3.24.3 2) "The default reference format of a compilation group is fixed form" — at every entry point (the `cobol` CLI, `CompilerDriver.Options.SourceFormat`, `Frontend.InitialFormat`), and a `>>SOURCE FORMAT` directive switches it (§7.3.24.3 1)); a SOURCE FORMAT directive on the FIRST line may be written in either format (4)). The compiler option **`--source-format`** (`fixed`, `free` or `auto`) is the implementor-defined mechanism §4.2.10 3) requires for selecting a nonstandard behavior of this required element: `free` starts the text in free form, and `auto` is the nonstandard extension that DETECTS the initial format from the text's layout (`ReferenceFormatProcessor.IsFixedForm` — a heuristic that reads conforming fixed-form source with a non-numeric or blank sequence area and text past margin R as free form, which is why it is never the default; kb/Work PB1362). GnuCOBOL, the rule-1 precedent, likewise defaults to fixed form and selects free form by a flag. A compiler directive is recognized in the program-text area OF THE FORMAT IN EFFECT — positions 8 through margin R of a source line in fixed form, whatever the sequence area holds, and the whole line in free form (§7.3.3 SR2/SR3; kb/Work PB1361). The in-repo test corpus was authored against detection and its harnesses select `auto` explicitly; the NIST CCVS programs compile under the fixed default. **Library text** is logically converted by the same walker and starts in the reference format in effect for its COPY statement (§7.3.24.3 3)); its own `>>SOURCE FORMAT` directives switch it, and the COPY statement's text is back in its own format after it (5)). Where the COPY statement's format was itself DETECTED (under `--source-format auto`) rather than stated by a directive or the option, a text detected fixed hands fixed form on, while a text detected free — which proves only that it is not column-bound, seven-space indentation being legal in both — hands nothing on, and the library text is detected by its own structure exactly as a compilation group is (`ReferenceFormatMap.LibraryTextDefaultAt`; kb/Work PB1067, witnessed by `conformance:2002/pb1067_library_text_source_format`). | `conformance:2023/l1_margin_r_program_text_area`; `conformance:85/pb1362_fixed_form_default`; `conformance:2002/pb1361_directive_in_format_in_effect`; `unit:CliParserTests.SourceFormat_DefaultsToFixed_SelectsFreeOrAuto_AndRejectsAnythingElse` |
| DOC-A.1-48 | **Default endianness specifications (for standard floating-point usages)**, §11.9.8 / §11.9.9, required + documented | When no OPTIONS **FLOAT-BINARY** clause is specified, the implied endianness-phrase for the standard binary floating-point usages (FLOAT-BINARY-32/-64) is **HIGH-ORDER-LEFT — big-endian** (§11.9.8.3 SR1 makes this the implementor's choice; it matches the big-endian pin of every other pinned byte representation, items 205/207, so records interchange). A specified `FLOAT-BINARY DEFAULT IS HIGH-ORDER-RIGHT` is **honored**: the implied phrase makes each FLOAT-BINARY-32/-64 item's IEEE interchange image **little-endian** (§13.18.60.4 GR19b), carried per item on its emitted numeric profile — the implementor-defined float usages (COMP-1/COMP-2/FLOAT-SHORT/-LONG/-EXTENDED) are unaffected (GR19c scopes the clause to the standard usages; GR13/GR21 pin ours big-endian). A per-item endianness-phrase on the USAGE clause itself (§13.18.60.2) is **parsed and honored**, and it **wins** over the OPTIONS-implied phrase — not by preference but by §11.9.8.3 SR2/SR3's own wording, which implies the clause only "for … any data item described with a standard binary floating-point usage in which an endianness-phrase is not specified". So the documented default above is the FALLBACK of a two-level derivation: the item's own phrase, else the OPTIONS clause, else HIGH-ORDER-LEFT. The phrase is rejected on a usage the general format does not print it on (COBOLNET1716), an encoding-phrase outside FLOAT-DECIMAL-16/-34 is rejected (COBOLNET1717), and a repeated phrase is rejected (COBOLNET1718, §5.2.6.4). **FLOAT-DECIMAL** (§11.9.9): the standard decimal floating-point usages are rejected as processor-dependent non-support (COBOLNET1564, item 207's family), so no default encoding/endianness determination arises; the clause binds and is inert. | `conformance:2023/pb174_float_item_endianness`; `conformance:2023/pb174_float_item_endianness_default`; `conformance:negative/pb174-float-decimal-encoding-nonsupport` |
| DOC-A.1-57 | **DISPLAY statement — format for display of a variable-length group**, §14.9.11.4 GR7, required + documented | A variable-length group (§8.5.1.12 — a dynamic-length elementary item or dynamic-capacity table is subordinate to it) displays as **its members' images concatenated in declaration order, each dynamic member at its CURRENT extent**: a fixed member contributes exactly the character image it occupies in a record (the item 205–215 representations — zoned digits, radix-2, BCD, IEEE interchange bytes), a DYNAMIC LENGTH item its current content at its current length, a dynamic-capacity table every occurrence at its current capacity, and a nested **scalar** variable-length group the same format recursively. The geometry follows the §15.50.4 r7 `FUNCTION LENGTH` sum: `LENGTH(G)` **equals** the displayed width, with no exception — a NATIONAL member contributes the TWO BYTES it occupies (§13.18.60.4 GR8 leaves the size to the implementor; D-N1 pins two, UTF-16BE, high-order first), exactly as LENGTH and BYTE-LENGTH have always counted it. ⚠ This row used to record a "one sanctioned width divergence" — a national member displaying ONE character per position while LENGTH counted two bytes — and kb/Work PB327 dissolved it: the group image became the group's STORAGE, so a national leaf could ride a file record at all, and the same change made the displayed width and LENGTH agree. §13.18.29.4 GR2's own NOTE states the consequence ("Without the GROUP-USAGE NATIONAL clause, the content of such a group item would be treated as category alphanumeric, possibly leading to corruption or invalid handling of data") and names the remedy: a GROUP-USAGE NATIONAL group is category national and displays its national CHARACTERS, through the generated `AsNat()` face. **Two variable-length mechanisms in one group compose (kb/Work PB244 shape (b))**: a group that holds an OCCURS DEPENDING table beside a dynamic-length item or dynamic-capacity table displays the table at its **current count** (§13.18.38.4 GR8; §13.18.38.3 SR22 makes the table the record's trailing storage, so the current image is a character prefix of the maximum one), the count arriving as the `__odo` parameter of the generated `CurrentImage` / `AsVarImage` / `CurrentExtents` from the operand's access path (`PlaceRenderer.PeelOdo`) — the current count for a statement's operand, the **maximum** at an activation boundary (§14.8.2.2, "the maximum length is used"). A dynamic-length item inside a **fixed OCCURS** table element is its own dynamic-length member **per occurrence** (§8.5.1.12.1 does not exempt a table above it; §8.5.1.12.2 puts each at its own relative byte position), so the table contributes `OCCURS` times the element's components — a compile-time multiplicity (`GroupImageCodec` `VarPartKind.NestedTable`, `VariableLengthCompatibility.Atoms`, the `FUNCTION LENGTH` per-occurrence walk). A CELL-BACKED group (an EXTERNAL or ADDRESS-OF-taken record) composes the same format from its cell, a dynamic-capacity table and a per-occurrence dynamic-length item included (kb/Work PB1042: each is a component slot of the cell, a table's occurrences element cells — DATA_MODEL D-SLOT "cell components"), and a cell-backed group that holds both a dynamic-length item and an OCCURS DEPENDING table shows the table at its **current count** exactly as a declared group does (kb/Work PB244: the window reserves the table at its maximum as the trailing storage of the fixed run, §13.18.38.3 SR22, and the operand's count cuts that run to the current extent, §13.18.38.4 GR8 — `StorageCell`'s `CellOdoTail`). **A table of variable-length ELEMENTS** — an OCCURS DEPENDING or dynamic-capacity table each of whose occurrences holds a dynamic-length item (§8.5.1.12.1 does not exempt a table element, so every occurrence is a variable-length group of its own) — displays **each occurrence's own current image in occurrence order**, an OCCURS DEPENDING table the first N occurrences its DEPENDING item names (§13.18.38.4 GR8) and a dynamic-capacity table every occurrence at its current capacity (§8.5.1.9.1) - in a declared group and in a CELL-BACKED one alike (the cell composes each element with the element's own window shape, `CellGroupShape`, and an OCCURS DEPENDING table's occurrences beyond the count drop their components with their fixed run, `CellOdoTail.Comps`; kb/Work PB244); `FUNCTION LENGTH` / `BYTE-LENGTH` of the group equal that displayed width (§15.50.4 rule 7, §15.14.4 rule 6; `DataItem.CurrentImageCapable`, `BoundIntrinsicCall.OverCurrentImage`). **DISPLAY has no excluded shape.** The OTHER statements that carry a variable-length group whole consume the same composer as a component carrier rather than a one-way image: the §14.8.2.2 / §14.8.3.2 activation-boundary crossing (kb/Work PB204) and - since kb/Work PB393 - the §14.9.25.4 GR9 MOVE, the §8.8.4.2.17 comparison, §14.9.20.4 GR8/GR10 INITIALIZE and the CORRESPONDING verbs carry a variable-length group in full, **including one whose OCCURS DEPENDING table has variable-length elements** (kb/Work PB244; `GroupImageCodec` `VarPartKind.OdoTable`: the first N occurrences' carriers at a statement - N the operand's count - and all of them at a boundary, where "the maximum length is used" (§14.8.2.2); a receiver takes every occurrence it has from whatever was carried, and the ones past the carried components are the §14.9.25.4 GR9b excess part; `VariableLengthCompatibility.Atoms` unrolls the table at its maximum, as it does a plain OCCURS DEPENDING table). A MOVE INTO a group that holds an OCCURS DEPENDING table uses the table only to the count the standard names (§13.18.38.4 GR8 a: a DEPENDING item outside the group - its value at the start of the statement; b: inside it and the group receiving - the maximum), and the occurrences past it keep their content (`FromVarImage`'s `__odo`, `StorageCell.StoreVarGroupAt`'s count - the law `WriteGroupImage` already applies to a fixed-length receiver; kb/Work PB244). **Rejected loudly rather than shown at a wrong extent**: a DYNAMIC-CAPACITY table of variable-length elements as a whole-group MOVE / comparison / CALL operand (its carrier component is one string, which cannot be split back into elements that each hold components of their own), and a file or sort RECORD that holds an OCCURS DEPENDING table of variable-length elements (a record read back cannot say how many occurrences it holds: the DEPENDING item is data, not layout; `DataItem.RecordImageCapable`). Since kb/Work PB981 a WRITE / REWRITE / RELEASE of any other variable-length RECORD sends this same composition (§3 determination D-FRA). (A USAGE INDEX leaf no longer excludes a group - the R40 pin, item 211.) | `conformance:2023/pb164_vlg_display`; `conformance:2014/pb1042_external_dynamic_table`; `conformance:2014/pb1042_cell_variable_components`; `conformance:2014/pb244_vlg_cell_odo_and_dynamic`; `conformance:2014/pb244_vlg_display_element_tables`; `conformance:2014/pb244_vlg_display_cell_element_tables`; `conformance:2014/pb244_vlg_carrier_odo_element_tables`; `conformance:2014/pb244_vlg_receiving_odo_count` |
| DOC-A.1-205 | **USAGE BINARY clause — computer storage allocation, alignment and representation of data**, §13.18.60.4 GR4, required + documented | A BINARY item is a **two's-complement integer of the item's UNSCALED value, MOST SIGNIFICANT BYTE FIRST (big-endian)**, in a width pinned by the PICTURE's digit count: **1 byte** for 1–2 digits · **2** for 3–4 · **4** for 5–9 · **8** for 10–18 · **16** for 19–38. The implied decimal point occupies no storage (§13.18.40.4) and the sign is the two's-complement sign — there is no separate sign byte. **Alignment: none** — WiseOwl COBOL performs no SYNCHRONIZED physical padding, so an item begins at the next byte and a group carries no implicit FILLER. The 16-byte tier exists because GR4's closing sentence requires storage "sufficient … to contain the maximum range of values implied by the associated decimal picture character-string" and a signed 19-digit picture (10^19−1) exceeds 2^63−1. Widths are sign-INDEPENDENT (GR12's precedent for the fixed-width usages). Worked example: `PIC 9(4) COMP VALUE 1234` occupies 2 bytes `04 D2`; `PIC S9(4) COMP VALUE -1234` occupies `FB 2E` (65536−1234). An UNSIGNED item holds the absolute value (§14.9.25.4 GR6d2b). `FUNCTION BYTE-LENGTH` reports exactly this width, and it is the width the item occupies in a group image, a record, a SORT key and a REDEFINES view — one representation at every byte boundary. | `conformance:2023/l1_usage_binary_storage` |
| DOC-A.1-206 | **USAGE BINARY-CHAR / -SHORT / -LONG / -DOUBLE — wider range than the minimum**, §13.18.60.4 GR12, optional | **Provided, the native range.** GR12's MINIMUM bands are asymmetric, and the asymmetry is the whole content of the rule: every SIGNED row of its table prints a STRICT `<` on both sides (`-2**(w-1) < n < 2**(w-1)`) while every UNSIGNED row prints `<=` on the left (`0 <= n < 2**w`) — re-rendered from the canonical PDF, printed page 507. The signed exclusion of `-2**(w-1)` is deliberate — it lets a sign-magnitude or ones'-complement machine conform — and §15.58.4's NOTE concedes it in terms: “BINARY-CHAR SIGNED \| -128 (assuming an 8-bit twos-complement representation)”. WiseOwl COBOL provides the full NATIVE TWO'S-COMPLEMENT band of each usage's byte width: **BINARY-CHAR** SIGNED -128..+127, UNSIGNED 0..255; **BINARY-SHORT** SIGNED -32768..+32767, UNSIGNED 0..65535; **BINARY-LONG** SIGNED -2147483648..+2147483647, UNSIGNED 0..4294967295; **BINARY-DOUBLE** SIGNED -9223372036854775808..+9223372036854775807, UNSIGNED 0..18446744073709551615. Each SIGNED usage therefore expresses exactly ONE value beyond the GR12 minimum — `-2**(w-1)` — and the four UNSIGNED usages are exactly the minimum. A value outside the band raises the size-error condition (§14.7.5), never a silent widening; GR12's nesting sentence still holds at the wider end, LOWEST-ALGEBRAIC of each narrower signed usage being expressible in the next. The band is written down ONCE — the byte width per usage (1/2/4/8) in `PicInfo.StorageWidth` under the `BinaryCapacity` discipline (`PicInfo.Truncation`) — and read by the runtime store bound (`CobolNum.InBinaryRange` / `CobolNum.TryStoreU`) and by the compile-time HIGHEST/LOWEST-ALGEBRAIC fold (`IntrinsicBinder.BindAlgebraicFold`), which `AlgebraicFoldContainerAgreementTests` holds in agreement. Owner decision Q30, 2026-09-09 (kb/Work PB592): this cell previously read “Not provided. Each usage holds exactly the GR12 minimum range”, which was false for all four SIGNED usages and, written as `±128`, was not readable as a range at all. | `conformance:2002/pb592_binary_usage_range_bounds` |
| DOC-A.1-207 | **USAGE BINARY-SHORT/-LONG/-DOUBLE and FLOAT-SHORT/-LONG/-EXTENDED — representation and length**, §13.18.60.4 GR13 + GR21, required + documented | BINARY-CHAR **1** byte, BINARY-SHORT **2**, BINARY-LONG **4**, BINARY-DOUBLE **8** — two's complement, big-endian, SIGNED and UNSIGNED the same width (GR21). FLOAT-SHORT is **IEEE 754 binary32 (4 bytes)**; FLOAT-LONG and FLOAT-EXTENDED are both **IEEE 754 binary64 (8 bytes)** — the standard's subset nesting (GR13) is satisfied since every binary64 value is expressible in binary64. **A floating-point item participates in group images, records and CALL/MOVE byte transfers as exactly those big-endian IEEE interchange bytes** (kb/Work PB164 wave 2 — bit reinterpretation, never a numeric conversion; the byte order matches USAGE BINARY so records interchange). **A REDEFINES view over one reads and writes those same bytes** — the item's window IS its storage (§13.18.44.4 GR1), so a windowed float DISPLAYs, CONVERTs and INITIALIZEs exactly as its natively-carried twin does: one representation, whichever description names it. | `conformance:2023/l1_usage_fixed_width_lengths` |
| DOC-A.1-208 | **USAGE COMPUTATIONAL clause — alignment and representation of data**, §13.18.60.4 GR6, required + documented | COMPUTATIONAL (and its COMP / COMP-4 synonyms) is **identical to USAGE BINARY** — radix 2, the item 205 width ladder and byte order, the same PICTURE-digit-count truncation discipline. **COMP-5 shares the representation but OWNS ITS FULL CONTAINER RANGE** (owner decision 2026-08-07, kb/Work R10): the value is bounded by the native two's-complement range of the byte width, never the picture's digit count. **It participates in group images, records, CALL/MOVE byte transfers and a REDEFINES view on exactly that representation** (kb/Work PB164 — a full-container value rides the image bit-identically; the earlier exclusion was predicate drift, not a documented determination). A REDEFINES view over a COMP/COMP-5 item reads and writes those same bytes: the storage association is the item's own storage (§13.18.44.4 GR1) and either data-name may reference it (GR2), so a windowed item behaves exactly as its natively-carried twin does — pinned byte-level by `stepd_mixed_usage_redefines` (an unsigned `9(4) COMP-5` window reads `FF FF`, and bytes written through the alphanumeric view read back as the value they encode). The in-memory CARRIER follows the container so the whole range is representable: a signed item rides `long` (≤ 8-byte container) or `Int128` (16-byte); an **unsigned 8-byte container rides `ulong`** ([0, 2^64) exceeds `long`) and an **unsigned 16-byte container rides `UInt128`** ([0, 2^128) exceeds `Int128`). `FUNCTION HIGHEST-ALGEBRAIC` / `LOWEST-ALGEBRAIC` of such an item fold exactly the container's ends (§15.43.4 r2 / §15.58.4 r2 — the §15.43.4 NOTE's `BINARY-CHAR UNSIGNED → +255` row is the standard's own container-range illustration), and the folded maximum stores back into the item losslessly. Pinned by the golden `highest_algebraic_comp5_unsigned` and `AlgebraicFoldContainerAgreementTests`. | `conformance:2023/stepd_mixed_usage_redefines`; `conformance:2023/highest_algebraic_comp5_unsigned`; `unit:AlgebraicFoldContainerAgreementTests.SixteenByteUnsigned_StoresFullRange_AsContainerBits` |
| DOC-A.1-211 | **USAGE INDEX clause — alignment and representation of data**, §13.18.60.4 GR10, required + documented | An index item is a **64-bit managed integer occurrence number** (8 bytes as reported by `FUNCTION BYTE-LENGTH`), never a byte offset. **Its character image is that occurrence number as an 8-byte BIG-ENDIAN two's-complement binary** (owner decision R40, 2026-08-30) — the same byte order as every other pinned representation (items 205/207/208), so records interchange — and an INDEX-leaf group therefore **participates in group images, records, CALL/MOVE byte transfers and DISPLAY** on exactly those bytes. **Alignment: none** (the item 205 rule). §13.18.60.3 SR10's reference restriction is untouched: an index item may be referenced explicitly only in a SEARCH or SET statement, a relation condition, an intrinsic function argument, an inline method invocation argument, or the USING phrase of a procedure division header / CALL / INVOKE. **A REDEFINES view over one reads and writes those same 8 bytes** — the item's window IS its storage (§13.18.44.4 GR1), so a windowed INDEX item behaves exactly as its natively-carried twin does. Naming one as a SORT key remains outside SR10's reference set. | `conformance:2023/pb164_index_group_image` |
| DOC-A.1-216 | **USAGE POINTER clause — alignment, size, representation, and range of values**, §13.18.60.4 GR23, required + documented | A data-pointer item is **one managed `ManagedPointer` reference — 8 bytes as reported by `FUNCTION BYTE-LENGTH`, naturally aligned on the 8-byte boundary the .NET runtime gives a reference, with no PICTURE; its VALUE is the reference, and its 8 STORAGE positions hold the image `PointerImage` defines**. ⛔ **STORAGE IMAGE (kb/Work PB970 arm 2; CLAUDE.md rule 1's ISO → GnuCOBOL → IBM/Micro Focus precedence):** GnuCOBOL stores a pointer as the machine address in the byte order of the platform's native binary integers and NULL as the zero address (IBM's and Micro Focus's NULL too), so the image read as an 8-byte binary integer IS the address. This implementation has no machine address, so its image is an ADDRESS TOKEN of the same shape, in this implementation's binary byte order — **big-endian** (A.1 items 205 and 207): NULL of every category = eight X"00"; a data-pointer = its storage area's base + its displacement, the k-th area imaged receiving base k × 2^32 (so `SET P UP BY n` moves the image by n and two areas never meet while a displacement stays within ±2^31); a program-pointer / function-pointer = a base from the same allocator per (category, case-folded externalized name). The token is stable for the run unit and one-way (never decoded back into a reference — no conforming pairing needs it). ONE codec (`src/Cobol.Net.Runtime/Control/PointerImage.cs`) defines the image; its consumer is the CALL boundary's BY CONTENT record into a same-length non-pointer formal (§14.8.2.3.3 rule 1; §14.2.3 GR9 "That argument is moved to this allocated record without conversion" — `CobolArgAdapt.Text`/`Num` via `WithPointerContent`); a formal of any other length stays EC-PROGRAM-ARG-MISMATCH. ⛔ **A pointer MEMBER of a shared storage area (a BASED, EXTERNAL, ADDRESS-OF or ALLOCATEd cell, or a record struct's one-way group image) shows the SAME image in its 8 positions** (kb/Work PB1071): the VALUE stays in the area's managed slot, and `CobolPtr.SlotWrite` writes the codec's image of every stored value into the member's positions beside it, ALLOCATE seeds the NULL image at each pointer member (§14.9.3.4 GR9 — "data items of class object or class pointer in the allocated storage are initialized to null"), the cell seeds start them at the NULL image, and a record struct's `AsImage` renders the live value, so a byte window, a group MOVE or DISPLAY, and the CALL boundary's BY CONTENT record all read what the codec defines (a class-OBJECT member has no image and keeps its reserved space positions). This OVERTURNS the earlier pin of kb/Work PB231's reserved space fill; `conformance:2002/pb231_based_pointer_leaf` and `conformance:2002/pb244_pointer_group_transfer` are re-derived, and `conformance:2002/pb1071_pointer_member_image` pins the relations the standard fixes (NULL is the zero address; equal addresses have equal images; §14.9.39.4 GR20 "incremented … by the number of bytes specified" moves the image by n). BY REFERENCE (§14.8.2.3.2) and a rule-2 activation (§14.8.2.3.3 rule 2d → §14.9.25.3 SR1) still refuse the pairing, at run time and at compile time respectively. Its VALUE is either the predefined address NULL or a (storage area, character-position displacement) pair over the one `StorageCell` shape (`CobolPtr`; COBOLNET_DATA_MODEL_DESIGN). **The RANGE of values is the signed 64-bit interval of displacements, −9 223 372 036 854 775 808 through +9 223 372 036 854 775 807** — the carrier's own range, `CobolPtr.MinAddress` / `CobolPtr.MaxAddress`, and the range §14.9.39.4 GR20 means by "the range of values allowed by the implementor for a data-pointer data item". A SET pointer UP/DOWN BY whose new address leaves that interval sets **EC-RANGE-PTR** and leaves identifier-9 unchanged (GR20), and the displacement is formed in `Int128` so the boundary is a value the guard can see rather than a wrap it cannot (kb/Work PB465 — `SET P UP BY 18446744073709551618` used to move the pointer by 2). ⛔ **An address inside the range but outside the addressed STORAGE is not out of range**: it is a legal pointer VALUE, and §13.18.5.4 GR4 raises EC-BOUND-PTR when it is DEREFERENCED, not when it is formed — a storage-bounds test at SET time would reject conforming programs. That deferral is why this range is the carrier's and not the cell's. ⛔ **GR20's range is not GR19's rule**: GR19 asks only whether arithmetic-expression-3 "evaluates to an integer", so an integral amount of 1.0E19 does NOT satisfy it and must not set EC-SIZE-ADDRESS. | `conformance:2002/pb465_set_pointer_range_ec`; `conformance:negative/pb465-set-pointer-up-by-at-85`; `unit:CobolPtrTests.UpByAmountReal_AnIntegralAmountPastTheCarrier_IsGr20_NotGr19`; `conformance:2002/pb970_pointer_by_content_storage_image`; `conformance:negative/pb970-pointer-by-content-prototype-alnum-formal`; `unit:PointerImageTests.DataPointer_IsAreaBasePlusDisplacement_BigEndian`; `unit:PointerImageTests.Null_OfEveryCategory_IsTheZeroAddress`; `conformance:2002/pb1071_pointer_member_image`; `conformance:negative/pb1071-pointer-member-image-85`; `unit:PointerImageTests.SlotWrite_StoresTheValueAndItsImage_AndOnlyItsOwnPositions` |
| DOC-A.1-214 | **USAGE OBJECT REFERENCE clause — amount of storage allocated**, §13.18.60.4 GR22 a), required + documented | **8 bytes — one .NET managed reference on the 64-bit runtime this compiler targets — and the SAME amount for every one of the §13.18.60.2 general format's eight writable shapes.** GR22 a) expressly permits an implementation to differ per shape (“not necessarily the same for every object reference”) and WiseOwl COBOL does not take that latitude: the descriptor (kind · name · FACTORY · ONLY) is a COMPILE-TIME fact that selects the emitted C# reference type — `CobolObject?`, the class's type, its `__FACTORY` sibling, or the interface — and every one of those is a single managed reference at run time. **Alignment: none** (the item 205 rule). **Where the 8 bytes are observable:** `FUNCTION BYTE-LENGTH` of the item is 8 (§15.14.4 rule 1); and because §13.18.60.3 SR14 admits the item as an elementary item subordinate to a type declaration with the STRONG phrase, a strongly-typed group COUNTS those 8 positions in its size (`PIC X(3)` + one reference = 11). In that group's character image the 8 positions are the D-SLOT placeholder — **eight SPACES, never the reference, whether the reference is NULL or not** — the group-image determination of item 56 (kb/Work PB244), and it is the one image every consumer of the group's characters reads: DISPLAY, an intrinsic function's string argument (§15.3 item 2; kb/Work PB1901), and a MOVE to a receiver that is not strongly typed (§14.9.25.4 GR4, “no conversion of data”). A MOVE between two strongly-typed groups of the same type (§14.9.25.3 SR2) transfers the reference itself, so the receiving group's reference identifies the same object. §13.18.60.3 SR15 keeps the item out of the file section, so the placeholder never reaches a file record. A STRING or UNSTRING statement naming such a group (as a sending item, a delimiter or a receiver) is REFUSED AT COMPILE TIME, never run: ISO §14.9.43.3 SR1 needs every identifier to be usage display or national, and §8.5.2.1 gives the usage of display only to an alphanumeric group — a strongly-typed group's class and category are its type-name, so its usage is read off its elementary items and an object-reference leaf is neither (COBOLNET1626, UNSTRING SR2 / SR4 COBOLNET1651 / COBOLNET1626); the object reference is legal only under a STRONG type (SR14), so no other group can hold one (kb/Work PB1527, PB244). | `conformance:2002/pb496_object_reference_storage`; `conformance:2023/pb61_length_byte_length_rule_branches`; `conformance:2002/pb1527_object_reference_group_image`; `conformance:negative/pb1527-string-object-reference-group` |
| DOC-A.1-135 | **MODULE-NAME — the form of a module name, and the composition of the STACK list**, §15.65.4 r4 + r9, required + documented | **Names.** A program element is reported by its PROGRAM-ID / FUNCTION-ID — the DECLARED program-name-1 / user-function-name-1, never the `AS literal-1` externalized name, even when the paragraph writes one (r4 offers the AS form as one of four and this determination does not take it; kb/Work PB303, `2023/pb303_as_module_name_and_invoke`); a METHOD by its METHOD-ID; a method's CURRENT (r7) is its class, the outermost element of its compilation unit. r4 permits any of these forms and the method-id form is chosen because it is the more informative. **STACK (r9) lists RUNTIME ELEMENTS, not compilation units** — entry 1 is CURRENT, every entry after it is what ACTIVATING would return within the previous one, the penultimate is TOP-LEVEL and the last is a single space, exactly as r9 composes it. ⚠ **Consequence, deliberate:** a CONTAINED (nested) program yields its outermost name TWICE (`MAIN;MAIN; `), because r9's first entry is outermost-granularity (r7) while the chain is element-granularity (r5), so for a nested program they coincide. The alternative — collapsing consecutive frames of one compilation unit, which §15.65.1's looser "a list of all the module names" wording would allow — was REJECTED (owner decision 2026-08-05) because it cannot represent RECURSION: three nested activations of one RECURSIVE program collapsed to a single entry while ACTIVATING in the same frame named the program, so STACK and ACTIVATING contradicted each other. A visible repeat can be read; missing frames cannot. | `conformance:2023/pb63_module_name_lengths` |
| DOC-A.1-134 | **MODULE-NAME — what is returned if the indicated module is not COBOL**, §15.65.4 r3, required + documented | **A single space.** The only non-COBOL runtime elements WiseOwl COBOL can designate are the operating environment itself and a .NET host that drives a COBOL element without entering through `ProgramTable.RunMain`: with no COBOL element running every keyword returns one space; TOP-LEVEL (r10) returns one space when frame 0 was not pushed as the main (the host, not the environment, activated it — `ModuleStack.Name` case 4, kb/Work PB63 / RV-15.65.4-10); ACTIVATING and STACK's final entry are one space for the environment (r5 / r9). | `unit:ModuleStackInvariantTests.TopLevel_WithoutAMainFrame_IsTheDocumentedNonCobolValue` |
| DOC-A.1-136 | **MODULE-NAME — how it is determined whether the program is a main program**, §15.65.4 r5, required + documented | **The main program is the element the run unit was started with** — the program `ProgramTable.RunMain` activates (the CLI's `--run` entry, a host's `RunMain` call): it is pushed as the run unit's `IsMain` frame; nothing else ever is. A program CALLed, INVOKEd or referenced as a function is never main, whatever its PROGRAM-ID; a RECURSIVE re-activation of the main is a CALLed frame. So ACTIVATING in the main is a single space (r5) and TOP-LEVEL is its name (r10). | `conformance:2023/pb63_module_name_lengths`; `unit:ModuleStackInvariantTests.ActivatingInTheMain_IsASingleSpace_TheOneR1Exception` |
| DOC-A.1-137 | **MODULE-NAME — the ACTIVATING value when the activation was in a nested program**, §15.65.4 r6, required + documented | **The nested (contained) program's OWN name**, i.e. the runtime element that executed the CALL / INVOKE / function reference — never its outermost containing program. r6 grants either; the element form is chosen because r9 defines every STACK entry after the first as "what ACTIVATING would return within the previous module", so ACTIVATING and the STACK chain agree by construction (`ModuleStack.Name` builds STACK from the same frame field): a separately-compiled program CALLed from contained `INR` inside `TOP` reports `ACTIVATING = INR` and `STACK = EXT;INR;TOP; ` (pinned by `2023/pb63_module_name_lengths`). | `conformance:2023/pb63_module_name_lengths` |
| DOC-A.1-140 | **POP directive — the warning mechanism for an unsuccessful POP directive**, §7.3.20.4 GR2, required + documented | **A compile-time WARNING, COBOLNET2297, on the POP line; the compilation continues and nothing is restored.** A `>>POP directive-name` is unsuccessful when no state of that directive was saved by a PUSH in the compilation group, or every saved state was already restored by an earlier POP (GR2's two antecedents). A `>>PUSH ALL` saves every directive §7.3.22.4 GR2 names — including those whose state this compiler does not hold (LISTING, DISPLAY, CALL-CONVENTION, PROPAGATE) — so a later `>>POP LISTING` after a `>>PUSH ALL` is SUCCESSFUL. ⚠ GR2 speaks of directive-name only, so a `>>POP ALL` with nothing stored restores nothing and draws no warning. The warning is issued once, by the post-COPY stage that sees every PUSH/POP of the final text (`DirectiveSiteProcessor`); a PUSH/POP in an omitted `>>IF` branch is not processed and cannot make a later POP successful (kb/Work PB941). | `unit:DirectiveStateStackTests.ANamedPopWithNothingStored_Warns_AndPopAllDoesNot` |
| DOC-A.1-133 | **MODULE-NAME — the length of the returned value item and whether it may have trailing spaces**, §15.65.4 r1/r2, conditionally required (documented voluntarily) | **A dynamic-length alphanumeric item with no trailing spaces**, r1's stated exception included (a single space for ACTIVATING in a main program, and STACK's final single-space entry — content, not padding). The name is delivered as its exact string; there is no fixed width between the module stack and the expression, so §15.65.4 r2's "does not fit" antecedent (EC-BOUND-FUNC-RET-VALUE) is structurally unreachable — pinned by `ModuleStackInvariantTests` (kb/Work PB63 / RV-15.65.4-2), which also fixes the r10 host-boundary arm above. | `unit:ModuleStackInvariantTests.NoTrailingSpaces_AndNoTruncation_AtEveryDepth` |
| DOC-A.1-209 | **USAGE DISPLAY — size and representation of characters**, §13.18.60.4 GR7, required + documented | **One byte per character position, uniform at every length.** GR7 states three obligations and this determination answers the third: the item “is aligned on a character boundary” (every byte is one, so a DISPLAY item never causes alignment filler — `X(1) X(3) X(1)` occupies 5); characters are “of uniform size” (`X(1)`/`X(5)`/`X(9)` occupy 1/5/9 — the same one byte per position at every extent, so no variable-width external form is used); and that size is “equal to or less than the size of characters in the computer’s national character set” — the national determination is **2** bytes per position (row 213, UTF-16 code units), and 1 ≤ 2, which is what makes the DISPLAY size a CONSTRAINED choice rather than a free one. `FUNCTION LENGTH` (character positions, §15.50.4 r3) and `FUNCTION BYTE-LENGTH` (bytes, §15.14.1) therefore agree on every alphanumeric item. A **numeric** item with no USAGE clause is USAGE DISPLAY by implication (§13.18.60.3 SR13 b) at one byte per DIGIT position: `9(5)` and `S9(5)` are both 5 — §13.18.52.4 GR5 a associates an unseparate operational sign with an EXISTING digit position — and `S9(5) SIGN IS TRAILING SEPARATE` is 6, GR6 a making the sign its own character position that “is not a digit position”. ⚠ A.1 item 209 permits an implementor option to vary the size or representation per compilation unit; **WiseOwl COBOL provides none** — the width law is `PicInfo.ByteWidth` alone, so one program cannot see two answers. | `conformance:2023/l1_usage_display_character_size` |
| DOC-A.1-215 | **USAGE PACKED-DECIMAL clause — computer storage allocation, alignment and representation of data**, §13.18.60.4 GR11, required + documented | A PACKED-DECIMAL item is **binary-coded decimal, two digits per byte, most significant first**, with a **trailing sign nibble** in the low half of the last byte: `C` positive, `D` negative, `F` for an item with no operational sign (the convention IBM, Micro Focus and GnuCOBOL all write, so a data file interchanges). The digit run is padded on the left with a zero nibble when needed, giving **`digits / 2 + 1` bytes**; the implied decimal point occupies no nibble. Under the COBOL-2023 **WITH NO SIGN** phrase the sign nibble is not reserved at all — every nibble is a digit and the width is **`ceil(digits / 2)`**. ⚠ The two forms can occupy the SAME number of bytes: 3 digits is 2 bytes either way, laid out `12 3C` signed and `01 23` unsigned-no-sign. **Alignment: none** (no SYNCHRONIZED padding). Worked example: `PIC 9(4) COMP-3 VALUE 1234` occupies 3 bytes `01 23 4F`; `PIC S9(4) COMP-3 VALUE -1234` occupies `01 23 4D`. Decoding accepts the universal readings — `B` and `D` negative, everything else positive — so a file written by another COBOL system reads correctly. | `conformance:2023/l1_usage_packed_decimal_storage` |
| DOC-A.1-82 | **Floating-point numeric literals — the maximum and minimum permitted value of the exponent**, §8.3.3.3.3 r3, required + documented | The FORM is the standard's (SR2/SR3: a 1–36-digit significand with a decimal point, an exponent of at most four digits — COBOLNET1661 otherwise, kb/Work PB99). **The permitted VALUE is the decimal128 range — about 1E-6176 to 9.99E+6144 — in EVERY position and EVERY arithmetic mode** (owner decision D-B, 2026-08-30; kb/Work PB156 + PB195). The literal IS its exact §8.3.3.3.3 rule-5 value wherever it appears (significand × 10^exponent), carried on the SDIDI lane, so the implementor-defined range r3 asks for is that carrier's; `1.0E+400` evaluates, `1.0E+9999` and `1.0E-9999` are COBOLNET1661. ⛔ This REPLACED a per-position split in which an arithmetic expression, a relation or a MOVE source screened the literal against IEEE binary64 (about 4.9E-324 ≤ \|v\| ≤ 1.8E+308): §14.9.2.4 GR4 and its §14.9.44.4 GR4 twin exclude only operands "described with usage" binary-*/float-*, never a literal, and §14.7.7 rule 2 names a floating-point literal as its OWN bullet where it means to include one — so `ADD 1.0E+0` to a PIC 9(20) holding 12345678901234567890 owed …891 and a binary64 operand delivered …168, and `IF 1.0E+400 > X` was refused under native arithmetic while the identical program compiled under ARITHMETIC IS STANDARD-DECIMAL. A literal's notation now never changes its VALUE. ⚠ It does still select the intermediate LANE under native arithmetic, and that is a published §8.8.1.3 determination, not an invariant: a float literal operand routes a native statement onto the decimal128 lane (34 significant digits) where a fixed-point literal keeps the Int128 one (~38), so `A * B + 0.0E+0` and `A * B + 0.0` differ for 18-digit A and B, and INTERMEDIATE ROUNDING (§11.9.11) reaches the first and not the second — pinned by `pb156_float_literal_exact_native`'s LANE/LANEF legs (kb/Work PB274). Separately, the exact-integer `**` arm is keyed on the exponent's VALUE and not its spelling, so `10 ** 30`, `10 ** 30.0` and `10 ** 3.0E+1` are one expression (kb/Work PB272). **The VALUE-clause range is a different rule and keeps the carrier's bound:** §13.18.63.3 SR2 wants "permissible values within the range indicated by the PICTURE clause or the USAGE clause" (verbatim, cite.py-verified) and a FLOAT-* subject has no PICTURE, so a VALUE on FLOAT-SHORT / FLOAT-BINARY-32 shall lie in binary32's range and on FLOAT-LONG / FLOAT-BINARY-64 / FLOAT-EXTENDED in binary64's, while a VALUE on a fixed-point numeric item or a floating-point numeric-edited item keeps the literal's EXACT value and its range is the receiver's PICTURE (`PIC 9E+9999 VALUE 1.0E+9999` seeds `1E+9999`; `PIC 9(5)V99 VALUE 1.5E+3` seeds 1500.00). **Residual, stated honestly:** SR2 admits a 36-digit significand and the SDIDI carrier holds 34, so the 35th and 36th significand digits of such a literal round at `CobolDec.FromParsed` under the INTERMEDIATE ROUNDING mode; the form is accepted (SR2 is satisfied) and the two trailing digits are not preserved. Pinned by `2023/pb99_floating_literal_extremes`, `pb99_floating_literal_operand_exact`, `pb99_floating_literal_standard_decimal`, `pb156_float_literal_exact_native`; negatives `pb99-floating-literal-form` / `-range`. | `conformance:2023/pb99_floating_literal_extremes`; `conformance:2023/pb156_float_literal_exact_native`; `conformance:negative/pb99-floating-literal-range` |
| DOC-A.1-87 | **FORMATTED-CURRENT-DATE function — accuracy of the returned time portion**, §15.38.4 r2 (Annex A.1 lists it under "Returned value rule 1"; the obligation's text is r2 — do not "correct" it the other way), required + documented | **The run unit's clock TICK — 100 ns, i.e. 7 significant fraction digits**, exactly SECONDS-PAST-MIDNIGHT's precision (item 171) and the same injectable `RunUnit.Clock` seam (`COBOLNET_CLOCK`-deterministic; the code once read `DateTimeOffset.Now` directly and no longer does). The value is carried at 9 fraction digits as `ticks × 100` — integer arithmetic, no binary64 conversion on the way (kb/Work PB65, 2026-08-18) — so a format's `s` fraction field renders the first 7 digits from the clock and ZEROS beyond them, up to the §15.3.3.2 maximum of 18; nothing is truncated or diagnosed. The date portion is the clock's local calendar date; the UTC-offset portion (`+hh:mm`) is the clock's offset. ⚠ **This determination is CONFORMS-but-UNTESTED, and says so** (2026-09-02): the only method in the tests tree that reaches `FORMATTED-CURRENT-DATE` through the clock seam, `NowFunctions_OnePinnedClock_OneInstant`, renders at `ss.ss` — **two** fraction digits (`.81` of a pinned `.8124791`) — while this row claims the clock tick, seven significant digits and then zeros. **A test that does not verify the determination cannot close its row**, so `Pinned by` is deliberately EMPTY, the inventory row carries no `test-ref`, and item 87 remains a GAP until a golden renders a long fraction field from a pinned clock. (Until 2026-09-02 this row's inventory evidence was `SecondsPastMidnight_PinnedClock_ExactTicks`, which is **item 171's** test and never calls this function — the A11 misfiling one layer down, in the evidence rather than in the determination; re-referencing it onto the function's own test fixed the misfiling but did not make the row tested.) | `conformance:2023/l1_formatted_current_date_accuracy`; `unit:CobolDateAccuracyTests.FormattedCurrentDate_PinnedClock_ClockTickThenZeros` |
| DOC-A.1-144 | **RANDOM function — seed value when no argument on first reference**, §15.75.3 r4, required (the seed VALUE need not be documented — this row is VOLUNTARY, the item-92 precedent, kept because the determination is load-bearing for users) | **Per-process OS entropy — sequences are deliberately NOT reproducible across runs without an explicit seed argument.** The unseeded generator is .NET's parameterless `Random` (xoshiro256** seeded from OS entropy); there is no fixed seed value, which is itself the determination §15.75.3 r4 requires. ⚖ Decided 2026-08-09 under the owner's standing latitude rule (follow GnuCOBOL where the standard leaves latitude): **surveyed, not assumed** — GnuCOBOL 3.x `cob_intr_random` seeds its first unseeded reference from `get_seconds_past_midnight() * (module-pointer bits)` (libcob/intrinsic.c, read 2026-08-09), i.e. per-process entropy, the SAME choice — and IBM documents the unseeded seed as unpredictable, so the vendors do not even split. A program needing a reproducible sequence writes `FUNCTION RANDOM(seed)` on first reference, which fully determines the sequence per r3/r5. | `conformance:2023/l1_random_seed_determination` |
| DOC-A.1-145 | **RANDOM function — the subset of the domain of argument-1 that yields distinct sequences**, §15.75.4 r3 ("shall include the values from 0 through at least 32767"), required + documented | **Every seed 0 through 2,147,483,647 selects its own generator state; the seeds 0 through 65,535 are MEASURED pairwise distinct** — `RandomSeedSubsetTests` compares the first three draws of every seed in that range and finds no two alike, which discharges the required floor 0..32,767 with a margin (only what is measured is claimed: distinctness above 65,535 is the generator's design, `System.Random(int)` seeding injectively on the value, not a measurement). **The DOMAIN is every non-negative integer — the seed is a TOTAL argument** (kb/Work PB636): §15.75.3 r2 constrains the SIGN alone and r3 makes this subset a FLOOR, not a domain, so a seed of any magnitude is legal and §15.3 has no incorrect value to raise on. A seed at or above 2³¹ ALIASES to `seed AND 0x7FFFFFFF` (2,147,483,648 → 0, 2,147,483,649 → 1 — pinned), and that same low-31-bit reduction is what a 19-digit or 31-digit seed takes; a negative seed is EC-ARGUMENT-FUNCTION (§15.75.3 r2, item 144's sibling row). The generator is `System.Random(int)` (the .NET Knuth subtractive sequence, `CobolIntrinsics.Random(Int128 seed)` — the Int128 carrier IS the totality claim the renderer's intake pairs against); the same seed reproduces the same sequence within and across processes on a given .NET runtime (§15.75.4 r2). | `unit:RandomSeedSubsetTests.Seeds_0_Through_65535_YieldPairwiseDistinctSequences`; `unit:RandomSeedSubsetTests.SeedsAtOrAbove2Pow31_AliasToTheirMaskedValue`; `unit:RandomSeedSubsetTests.SeedsPastLong_AnswerInRange_AndRaiseNothingUnderChecking`; `conformance:2002/pb636_random_total_seed` |
| DOC-A.1-151 | **RECORD DELIMITER clause — the method for determining the length of a variable-length record when the clause is NOT specified**, §12.4.5.11.4 GR5, required + documented | **Two methods, chosen by the file’s ORGANIZATION — and this determination governs EVERY variable-length file WiseOwl COBOL writes, including one that DOES write a RECORD DELIMITER clause, because that clause is declined whole (§2 row 26, COBOLNET1778).** **(a) Record sequential, relative and indexed:** each record is framed on the medium by a **4-byte little-endian binary length prefix** giving the record’s byte length, immediately followed by that many payload bytes; the prefix value `0xFFFFFFFF` is the relative store’s empty-slot tag and carries no payload. ONE implementation serves every organization — `RecordFraming` (store-level `WriteStore`/`ReadStore`, stream-level `WriteFrameHead`/`PrefixLength`), streamed by `SequentialConnector` — so a file written by one organization’s connector is readable by another’s. A frame whose length word has bit 31 set carries the record’s EXTENT TABLE between the word and the payload (§3 D-FRA (v); kb/Work PB1053) — framing like the prefix, never part of the record. **(b) LINE SEQUENTIAL:** no prefix is written; the length is the number of characters between line delimiters, which is §9.1.7.2’s own rule (“In line sequential files the length of each record is determined by the number of characters between the preceding line delimiter and the following line delimiter or the end of file if no line delimiter is present”), so a line-sequential file stays plain text. **The licence to add a header at all is §9.1.7.2**, not §9.1.13.6 — “In record sequential files the length of each record is determined by any information the implementor may add to the record on the physical storage medium (such as record length headers)”; §9.1.13.6’s “externally-defined boundaries” is the I-O status 34 rule about writing past a FILE’s end and answers a different question (the miscitation it replaced lived in `RecordFraming`’s own header until 2026-09-05, kb/Work PB292). **§12.4.5.11.4 GR1 is honoured in both arms** — “Any method used shall not be reflected in the record area or the record size used within the function, method, or program”: the prefix is stripped before the record area is filled and never enters a record size a program reads, and the line delimiter likewise. Witness `conformance:2023/pb292_record_delimiter_witness`. | `conformance:2023/pb292_record_delimiter_witness` |
| DOC-A.1-115 | **Line sequential character set**, §14.9.30.4 GR16 + §9.1.13.2 item 7 (the '09' side) and §14.9.51.4 GR23 + §14.9.35.4 GR17 d) + §9.1.13.10 item 1 (the '71' side), required + documented | **A line sequential file with no CODE-SET clause is UTF-8 text (owner decision kb/Work R51; kb/Work PB1760), and the members of its character set are the Unicode scalar values from U+0020 (SPACE) up, in an alphanumeric and a national record area alike — a surrogate pair is one character, HIGH-VALUE (U+FFFF) is a member; the characters BELOW U+0020 — the C0 controls U+0000–U+001F, among them LF U+000A (the line delimiter, with CR U+000D only as part of a CR LF pair — item 114) and the tab U+0009 — are outside it, and so is an unpaired surrogate, which is not a character and has no UTF-8 form.** **Encoding determinations** (§12.4.5.10.3 GR2 leaves "the range of allowable characters in a line sequential file" to the implementor, §8.1.2 the encoding; `LineSequentialEncoding` is their one executable copy): (a) a record area is written as the UTF-8 of its CHARACTERS — a national area's UTF-16BE pairs are decoded first, so a national space is one byte; (b) a UTF-8 byte-order mark opening the file is accepted and is not record data, and a WRITE never writes one; (c) a byte that does not begin a well-formed UTF-8 sequence (a legacy 8-bit file's `é` = X'E9', a torn sequence) decodes to the lone surrogate U+DC80+byte — not a character, so the READ is successful with '09' by the ONE predicate below, the record area keeps the byte's identity (GnuCOBOL, the precedence rule's next authority, transfers a line sequential file's bytes unchanged), and a WRITE or REWRITE of that record area is '71'; (d) §14.9.30.4 GR14/GR15's sizes and §14.9.35.4 GR17's NOTE 4 ("sufficient characters to fill the record area") are in the record area's own units — a UTF-8 line of ten `é` fills a `PIC X(10)` record — while §14.9.35.4 GR17 b)/c) compare BYTES and replace the line IN PLACE, so a REWRITE whose UTF-8 is longer than the line it replaces is '44' and a shorter one is space-filled to that line's byte length. DEL (U+007F) through U+00FF, the whole Latin-1 supplement included, are members, so an ordinary 8-bit text file is read without '09' (pinned: the golden's `B2ORD4=128` / `B2ORD5=256`, FUNCTION ORD's 1-based ordinals for 0x7F and 0xFF). **ONE set, three rules** (kb/Work PB329): a SUCCESSFUL READ whose record area holds a non-member sets '09' (§14.9.30.4 GR16 / §9.1.13.2 item 7 — the record is still delivered, and '0' as a first digit raises only the continuable EC-I-O-WARNING); a WRITE (§14.9.51.4 GR23) or a REWRITE (§14.9.35.4 GR17 d) of such a record area is UNSUCCESSFUL with '71', and §9.1.13.10 item 1's "the record area remains unchanged" means nothing reaches the medium — the golden re-reads the file and finds only the records the successful writes released. `LineSequentialCharacterSet` is the set's one executable copy and `SequentialConnector.RecordAreaOutsideLineCharacterSet` its one call shape, so the read arm and the three write entry points cannot disagree about what a line sequential character is. **THE DERIVATION, IN FIVE STEPS.** (1) The delimiters are FORCED out, not chosen: §9.1.7.2 fixes a line sequential record's extent as "the number of characters between the preceding line delimiter and the following line delimiter", so a record area holding CR or LF cannot round-trip as one record (the delimiter is LF, with CR only as part of a CR LF pair — item 114; a lone CR read into a record area is record data and answers '09'). (2) The set cannot be ONLY "everything but the delimiters": the reader consumes CR/LF as the framing, so a record area could never hold one and GR16's '09' would be unreachable by construction — a REQUIRED A.1 determination that makes its own rule vacuous is not a determination. (3) The remaining C0 controls are the stream/device repertoire rather than text — NUL terminates a host string, SUB (0x1A) marks end-of-file on DOS-descended hosts, VT/FF are page controls, ESC introduces an escape sequence — and a line sequential file exists to BE plain text and to interchange with non-COBOL tools, the same property that keeps the §9.1.6 fixed file attributes of a SEQUENTIAL file out of item 129's validated set entirely — a line sequential file records no attribute because recording one would stop it being plain text. (4) ⚖ **Surveyed, not assumed** (the standing latitude rule): GnuCOBOL's `COB_LS_VALIDATE` defaults to TRUE "per COBOL 2022" and "defines for LINE SEQUENTIAL files that the data should be validated as it is read (status 09) / written (status 71)", treating data below SPACE as invalid — the same boundary and the same two statuses. (5) With no CODE-SET clause there is no ceiling below the Unicode scalar values: the file is UTF-8 text, so every character a record area can hold has an image (owner decision kb/Work R51, which retired R47's U+00FF ceiling for this organization; kb/Work PB1760). With a CODE-SET clause the ceiling is the CODE-SET alphabet's coded character set instead (§13.18.13.4 GR6): under STANDARD-1, STANDARD-2 or `ASCII` an alphanumeric character above U+007F is outside the set — '71' on the WRITE or REWRITE, '09' on the READ of a medium byte X'80'–X'FF' — and under `EBCDIC` every character U+0020–U+00FF is inside (kb/Work PB1542; `Contains` takes the connector's `CodeSetConversion`, whose `Represents` is the same membership every other organization's '91' asks). **CHARACTERS, NOT BYTES.** §14.9.30.4 GR15 is explicit that a record area is specified implicitly or explicitly as alphanumeric OR as national, and this connector channel carries one char per BYTE, so a NATIONAL record area is tested two bytes at a time as UTF-16BE (§13.18.60.4 GR8 / determination D-N1): `N"CD"` occupies the bytes `00 43 00 44`, and a byte-level test would read the 0x00 halves as control characters and refuse every national line sequential record (it would have broken the standing golden `2023/pb327_national_line_sequential_fill`). A trailing ODD byte is half a national position, whose content §14.9.30.4 GR14/GR15 leave undefined; it forms no character and is not tested. **'09' WINS OVER '06'.** GR16 is stated after GR15 and its only condition is "the execution of the READ statement is successful", so it lands on a truncated over-length read too. **ONE EDITION.** ORGANIZATION LINE SEQUENTIAL is a COBOL-2023 introduction (§12.4.5.10.3 GR2; the Foreword lists “Line Sequential file organization” among the main changes over ISO/IEC 1989:2014), so this determination and every rule that consumes it exist at 2023 alone — the 2002 and 2014 copies of the golden were deleted as programs no edition below 2023 can express (kb/Work PB688). | `conformance:2023/pb329_line_sequential_charset_2023`; `conformance:2023/pb690_line_sequential_byte_image_2023`; `conformance:2023/pb1542_code_set_line_sequential`; `unit:CodeSetConversionTests.LineSequentialCharacterSet_CeilingIsTheCodeSetAlphabet` |
| DOC-A.1-150 | **RECORD DELIMITER clause — feature-name-1 and the method associated with it**, §12.4.5.11.3 SR2 + §12.4.5.11.4 GR4, **optional** + documented-if-provided | **Not provided.** No name is available for use as feature-name-1. §12.4.5.11.3 SR2 — “The implementor shall specify the names available for use as feature-name-1” — leaves the set to this implementation, and the set specified here is **empty**, which Annex A.1’s preamble licenses in terms: “Optional: The element may be provided at the implementor’s option.” A `RECORD DELIMITER IS feature-name-1` written anyway is ACCEPTED and diagnosed by name (COBOLNET1778, §2 row 26): §12.4.5.11.4 GR4 has no method to associate with the spelling, so GR5’s implementor method (item 151) frames the record instead and §12.4.5.11.4 GR1 keeps that invisible to the program. ⛔ **This is a §4.2.7-shaped decline on an A.1-OPTIONAL item, not a §4.2.6 one** — A.3 item 26 reaches only the STANDARD-1 phrase, so the clause’s two arms are declined on two different licences and the row that records THIS one is here rather than in §2. Closes as DOCUMENTED-NON-SUPPORT under the `a1-optional-not-provided` derived selector (kb/Work PB280 Q1, owner decision 2026-09-02). Witness `conformance:2023/pb292_record_delimiter_witness` + `conformance-test:CobolNet.Tests.Conformance.DocumentedNonSupportWitnessTests.RecordDelimiterFeatureName_NamedNonSupportWarning_PinnedToSpec`. | `conformance:2023/pb292_record_delimiter_witness`; `conformance-test:DocumentedNonSupportWitnessTests.RecordDelimiterFeatureName_NamedNonSupportWarning_PinnedToSpec` |
| DOC-A.1-112 | **LEAP-SECOND directive — whether standard numeric time form values greater than or equal to 86,400 may be reported**, §7.3.17 GR4 + §15.80.3 r4 + §15.3.3.3, required + documented | **NO — never.** The determination §15.80.3 r4 requires ("The implementor defines whether a value greater than or equal to 86,400 may be returned from the SECONDS-PAST-MIDNIGHT intrinsic function") is answered in the negative: .NET's `DateTime` cannot represent a leap second, so the day's tick count is always < 86,400 seconds and the returned value satisfies §7.3.17.4 GR4's ON-mode bound ("greater than or equal to zero and less than 86,401") on BOTH directive branches. `>>LEAP-SECOND ON` is recognized (`LeapSecondDirectiveProcessor`, kb/Work PB65) and governs the ARGUMENT side of the directive as §15.3.3.3 and §7.3.17.4 GR4 require: a formatted-time argument may carry 60 in its seconds subfield (`SECONDS-FROM-FORMATTED-TIME("hhmmss", "235960")` = 86,400; TEST-FORMATTED-DATETIME accepts it), a standard numeric time form value is bounded at 86,401 (FORMATTED-TIME / FORMATTED-DATETIME and LOCALE-TIME-FROM-SECONDS present 86,400 as 23:59:60 — §15.54.4 r2 returns "a character-string containing hours, minutes, and seconds of the time specified by argument-1" and §15.3.3.3 lets the seconds subfield be 60 under ON, so the leap second is 23:59:60, read through the ONE `CobolDate.TimeOfDay`; it was `24:00:00` — COMBINED-DATETIME accepts it). The FLOOR is enforced on every carrier (kb/Work PB1379): a floating-point or SDIDI seconds argument lands rounding toward negative infinity, so a COMP-2 `-1.0E-10` takes the §15.3 out-of-range path under ON and OFF alike instead of being truncated to midnight. The REPORTED side stays this negative determination on both directive branches. Golden pair `pb65_leap_second_on` / `pb65_leap_second_off`. Range endpoints pinned by `CobolDateWindowingTests.SecondsPastMidnight_Range_StandardNumericTimeForm`; see item 171 for the precision half of the same function. | `conformance:2023/pb65_leap_second_on`; `conformance:2023/pb65_leap_second_off`; `unit:CobolDateWindowingTests.SecondsPastMidnight_Range_StandardNumericTimeForm`; `conformance:2023/pb1379_time_form_floor_float_carriers_off`; `conformance:2023/pb1379_time_form_floor_float_carriers_on`; `unit:CobolDateSecondsLandingTests` |
| DOC-A.1-171 | **SECONDS-PAST-MIDNIGHT function returned value — precision**, §15.80.3 r3, required + documented | **100 nanoseconds — 7 fraction digits**, the .NET `DateTime` tick resolution: the function returns the day's TICK count as the unscaled value at scale 7 (the renderer's documented contract). Range **[0, 86 400)** — LEAP-SECOND's §7.3.17 default OFF is the only supported mode (§15.5.5), so a value ≥ 86 400 is unreachable. Pinned by `CobolDateWindowingTests` (05:14:27.8124791 → 188 678 124 791 ticks, plus both range endpoints) and read through the ONE injectable `RunUnit.Clock` seam, so the precision and the clock's determinism are the same mechanism. | `unit:CobolDateWindowingTests.SecondsPastMidnight_PinnedClock_ExactTicks`; `unit:CobolDateWindowingTests.SecondsPastMidnight_Range_StandardNumericTimeForm` |
| DOC-A.1-176 | **SET statement — the value in a NaN PAYLOAD**, §14.9.39.4 GR34 and GR35, required + documented | **Quiet NaN: payload ZERO. Signaling NaN: payload ONE.** GR34/GR35 set the content of identifier-14 to "a canonical representation of a quiet NaN / of a signaling NaN, with the payload set to an implementor-defined value, as described in ISO/IEC 60559:2020, Clause 3, for the basic interchange format corresponding to the usage of identifier-14", and A.1 item 176 makes the choice documentable. §14.9.39.3 SR32 confines the receiver to a STANDARD floating-point usage (§3.166 / §3.167), and of those WiseOwl COBOL provides FLOAT-BINARY-32 = IEEE binary32 and FLOAT-BINARY-64 = IEEE binary64 EXACTLY — the other three are Annex A.3 items 17/19 documented non-support (§2 rows 17 and 19), refused at declaration — so the interchange format the rules name IS the .NET carrier and nothing is approximated. **The full encodings, sign bit from GR34/GR35's own last sentence (the SIGN phrase, positive when absent):** binary32 quiet `0x7FC00000` (`0xFFC00000` negative), binary32 signaling `0x7F800001` (`0xFF800001`); binary64 quiet `0x7FF8000000000000` (`0xFFF8000000000000`), binary64 signaling `0x7FF0000000000001` (`0xFFF0000000000001`). The QUIET payload of zero is the canonical NaN every IEEE implementation produces — leading significand bit set, every other significand bit clear; the SIGNALING payload of one is the SMALLEST value that keeps the significand nonzero once ISO/IEC 60559:2020 §6.2.1's quiet bit is cleared, which a signaling NaN requires. **⛔ Written as BIT PATTERNS, never as a CLR constant:** `double.NaN` is `0xFFF8000000000000` — its sign bit is SET — so GR34's "otherwise the sign is positive" would have to be spelled `-double.NaN` and would depend on how a constant negation folds; and the CLR has no signaling-NaN constant at all, because every managed arithmetic path quiets one. **⛔ And delivered to the item with no binary64 anywhere on the way (kb/Work PB961):** a binary32 → binary64 widening QUIETS a signaling NaN, so the value is materialized by an opaque runtime call over the bits (`CobolFloat.FromBinary32Bits` / `FromBinary64Bits` — an inline `BitConverter` reinterpretation of a constant is a JIT floating constant, which the JIT carries as a double, and it stored the binary32 signaling NaN as the QUIET `0x7FC00001`), and a windowed or record-image FLOAT-BINARY-32 encodes and decodes through the binary32 carrier's own lanes (`CobolNum.FormatImageSingle` / `ParseImageSingle`). A same-usage MOVE of the item copies it on its own carrier, as §14.9.25.4 GR6 c) requires ("transferred to the receiving data item without change"), so a copy keeps the payload too. ONE table, `IeeeSpecials.Bits` (`src/Cobol.Net.Compiler/Binding/IeeeSpecials.cs`), is both the emitted encoding and the documented one; `RuntimeApi.FloatFromBits` spells it. The quiet-versus-signaling distinction is COBOL-observable through §8.8.4.4.4 GR3 j)/k)'s FLOAT-NOT-A-NUMBER-QUIET / -SIGNALING class conditions (kb/Work PB225), decided on the carrier's raw bits by `CobolFloatClass`; `CobolFloatClassTests` pins that every value this table writes classifies as what it was written as. | `unit:IeeeSpecialsTests.EveryCanonicalValue_DecodesToWhatItsGeneralRuleRequires`; `unit:IeeeSpecialsTests.TheStoredImage_CarriesTheDocumentedBits`; `conformance:2014/pb452_set_content_float`; `conformance:2014/pb961_signaling_nan_binary32`; `unit:CobolFloatClassTests.EverySetFormat15Value_ClassifiesAsWhatItWasWrittenAs` |
| DOC-A.1-174 | **SET statement — the effect of SET on the FUNCTION whose address is being stored in a function-pointer**, §14.9.39.4 GR15, required + documented | **NONE. Storing a function's address does nothing whatever to the function.** GR15 says only that the effect "is implementor-defined", so the whole obligation is to say what this implementation does, and what it does is defined by what a function-pointer IS here: the externalized function-name (§8.4.3.12.4 GR2 — "For a COBOL function, the address is that of the function identified by the externalized function-name in its FUNCTION-ID paragraph"), carried in the managed `FunctionPointer` identity struct. **Taking that address therefore performs no activation, no loading, no linking, no allocation and no initial-state establishment**: §14.6.2.3.2's initial state is established "the first time the function … is activated in a run unit", and a SET is not an activation (§8.4.3.2.4 GR6 is the only thing that activates a function). Concretely: a function's WORKING-STORAGE is untouched, a previously activated function keeps its §13.5.4 GR1 static data exactly as it stood, and a never-activated function stays never-activated — `ProgramTable.FunctionAddressOf` performs a registry LOOKUP only, and its one side effect (the separately-compiled sibling-module probe) is the same module load a CALL would perform and changes no COBOL-visible state. Consequently a function's RESULT cannot depend on whether its address was taken, which is what the witness measures. ⚠ The empty answer is the substantive one: an implementation that (say) pinned or eagerly initialized the function would have to say so here, and a program relying on either would be relying on an implementor definition. **The witness is an OBSERVATION, not a restatement:** `conformance:2014/pb452_set_function_pointer` activates `FUNCTION PBF8DBL(21)` before the first SET and again after the last, and both activations print `000000042` — so the function's result cannot depend on whether its address was taken. | `conformance:2014/pb452_set_function_pointer`; `conformance:2023/pb452_function_address_runtime` |
| DOC-A.1-175 | **SET statement — the effect of SET on the PROGRAM whose address is being stored in a program-pointer**, §14.9.39.4 GR17, required + documented | **NONE.
