Storing a program's address does nothing whatever to the program.** GR17 says only that the effect "is implementor-defined", so the obligation is to say what this implementation does, and it is fixed by what a program-pointer IS here: the externalized program-name of an OUTERMOST program (§8.4.3.13.4 GR2), carried in the managed `ProgramPointer` identity struct. `SET … TO ENTRY` performs a registry LOOKUP only (`ProgramTable.EntryOf` — its one side effect, the separately-compiled sibling-module probe, is the same module load a CALL would perform and changes no COBOL-visible state), and `SET pp TO pp2` / `TO NULL` is a carrier copy (`SetEmitter.EmitSetProgramPointer`). **So the SET performs no activation, no CANCEL, and no initial-state establishment**: a program already activated keeps its WORKING-STORAGE in its last-used state (§8.6.4), a never-activated program stays never-activated, and a later CALL through the pointer is an ordinary CALL of that program (§14.9.4). ⚠ The empty answer is the substantive one, exactly as for item 174: an implementation that (say) loaded, initialized or cancelled the program would have to say so here. **The witness is an OBSERVATION:** `conformance:2002/pb460_set_program_pointer_effect` calls a counting subprogram once (`COUNT=1`), stores its address three times, and calls it through the pointer — `COUNT=2`, where a SET that re-initialized it would print `COUNT=1`; a third program whose address is stored but which is never called prints nothing. | `conformance:2002/pb460_set_program_pointer_effect`; `conformance:2002/program_pointer` |
| DOC-A.1-210 | **USAGE FUNCTION-POINTER clause — alignment, size and representation of data, and allowable languages**, §13.18.60.4 GR26, required + documented | GR26 leaves four things to the implementor and this determination answers all four. **ALIGNMENT: none** (the item 205 rule). §13.18.60.3 SR14 confines a function-pointer to a level-1/77 elementary item or an elementary item of a STRONG type declaration. Its value rides a MANAGED SLOT (`StorageForm.FunctionPointerRef`), and inside a strongly-typed group it occupies 8 character positions whose character image is the D-SLOT placeholder — eight SPACES, whether the pointer is NULL or not — exactly as a `POINTER` or `PROGRAM-POINTER` leaf does (item 56): `01 G TYPEDEF STRONG` of `PIC X(3)`, a `FUNCTION-POINTER TO` leaf and `PIC X(2)` has `FUNCTION LENGTH` 13 and displays `abc` + eight spaces + `zz` before and after `SET … TO ADDRESS OF FUNCTION`. **SIZE: 8 character positions** wherever a size is asked for (`FUNCTION BYTE-LENGTH`, the `ElementaryByteWidth` pin) — the 64-bit managed reference width this implementation reserves for every class-pointer leaf, shared with POINTER / PROGRAM-POINTER / OBJECT REFERENCE / INDEX so the four cannot drift. **REPRESENTATION: the function's EXTERNALIZED FUNCTION-NAME**, held in the readonly record struct `FunctionPointer(string? Name)` (`src/Cobol.Net.Runtime/Control/FunctionPointer.cs`) and resolved through the ONE run-unit `ProgramTable`; the predefined address NULL is the null name, which §8.4.3.10.4 GR2's "guaranteed not to represent the address of any function" satisfies because no FUNCTION-ID paragraph can name a function null. It is deliberately a SEPARATE type from `ProgramPointer`, whose representation is the same shape: §8.4.3.10.4 keeps the predefined NULLs apart by category and §14.9.39.3 SR20/SR21 forbid mixing the categories in one SET, so one shared struct would have made the two assignment-compatible in the GENERATED C#, the one place a binder screen cannot reach. **ALLOWABLE LANGUAGES: COBOL only.** GR26's second paragraph admits "another language for which the implementor has declared support for access by function pointers" and this implementation declares none: a non-COBOL function has no run-unit registration to resolve, so §8.4.3.12.4 GR4 — EC-FUNCTION-NOT-FOUND and the predefined address NULL — is the defined answer for one, never an undefined outcome — which the `NF-RAISED` / `B-NULL` pair of `conformance:2023/pb452_function_address_runtime` measures. | `conformance:2014/pb452_set_function_pointer`; `conformance:2023/pb452_function_address_runtime`; `conformance:negative/pb452-function-pointer-without-to` |
| DOC-A.1-177 | **SIGN clause — representation when the picture character-string contains the symbol 'S' and no optional SIGN clause applies**, §13.18.52.4 GR4 + §8.5.1.5, required + documented | **The operational sign is FUSED onto the TRAILING digit position, and WHICH characters it uses is the compile option `--sign-encoding`, default `ibm`** — owner decision kb/Work PB803, 2026-09-09: *"Keep both behind an option with the default being IBM and Micro Focus compatibility."* GR4 grants both halves at once (“Neither the representation nor the position of that operational sign is specified by the symbol 'S'. The implementor shall specify the position and representation of the operational sign”) and §8.5.1.5 repeats the obligation (“if it is not used, operational signs will be represented as defined by the implementor”), so this determination answers both. **POSITION — TRAILING**: an item with no SIGN clause is byte-identical to one that writes `SIGN IS TRAILING`, and different from one that writes `SIGN IS LEADING`. **REPRESENTATION — two conventions, one table pair each, chosen at COMPILE time:** `ibm` (**the default**) fuses positive 0–9 to `{ABCDEFGHI` and negative 0–9 to `}JKLMNOPQR`, so `PIC S9(3)` +123 is `12C`, −123 is `12L` and `SIGN IS LEADING` −123 is `J23`; `ascii` leaves a POSITIVE digit alone and makes a negative digit *digit + 0x40* (`pqrstuvwxy`), so `PIC S9(4)` −1234 is `123t` and `SIGN IS LEADING` −1234 is `q234`. Surveyed rather than invented: `ibm` is what IBM Enterprise COBOL, Micro Focus and GnuCOBOL's `-fsign=ebcdic` write, `ascii` is GnuCOBOL's `-fsign=ascii` (its default on a PC host) — read from that compiler's published test expectations, never its sources. The value is spelled **`ibm`, not “ebcdic”**, because nothing about those characters is EBCDIC-encoded on an ASCII host: they are what an EBCDIC-descended CONVENTION produces after translation, and the misnomer would make the option unreadable. **The fused sign occupies an EXISTING digit position and adds none** (§13.18.52.4 GR5 a, which is normative and not part of the latitude), so `PIC S9(3)` is 3 character positions under either convention. **⚠ THE INTERCHANGE CONSEQUENCE, stated rather than left implicit:** the convention is a property of the **COMPILED PROGRAM** — baked into every emitted `NumProfile` and into every emitted signed class condition, never read from the process environment — so two programs of one run unit compiled under different options each read storage by their OWN convention, exactly as two vendors' programs do. Nothing records the convention of a DATA FILE: it is whatever wrote the file, and a mismatch is undetectable (a record holding `123t` read through a program compiled `ibm` yields +1230, silently). A signed DISPLAY item that must cross a convention boundary should be declared `SIGN IS SEPARATE`, whose characters §13.18.52.4 GR6 b) pins to `+`/`-` with no implementor latitude at all. ONE table pair per convention lives in `ZonedSign` (`src/Cobol.Net.Runtime/Values/Numeric/ZonedSign.cs`), consulted by the encoder (`CobolNum.FormatDisplaySigned`), the decoder (`CobolNum.ParseDisplay`) and the class condition alike; the option reaches them through `PicInfo.ProfileInitializer(SignEncoding)`, the one place a `NumProfile` is written. | `conformance:2023/pb803_sign_default_representation`; `conformance:2023/pb803_sign_encoding_ascii`; `conformance:85/pb803_sign_default_representation_85` |
| DOC-A.1-178 | **SIGN clause — what constitutes a valid sign when the SEPARATE CHARACTER phrase is not present**, §13.18.52.4 GR5 b), required + documented | **At the SIGN POSITION: a plain digit `0`–`9`, plus both tables of the program's `--sign-encoding` convention. Every other character position must be a plain digit.** Under the default `ibm` the sign position admits `0`–`9`, `{`, `A`–`I`, `}`, `J`–`R`; under `ascii` it admits `0`–`9` and `p`–`y`. Any other character there makes the item **NOT NUMERIC**. GR5 b) — “The implementor defines what constitutes valid signs for data items” — is the whole of the latitude, and §8.8.4.4.4 GR3 n)1.a delegates the NUMERIC class condition to it by name (“Valid operational signs are defined in 13.18.52, SIGN clause”). **A PLAIN DIGIT IS VALID, and means POSITIVE**: an unseparated sign shares a digit position, so an item whose sign was never punched — what a group MOVE of raw characters deposits, and what the `ascii` convention writes for every positive value — is well formed. **A punched character carries a DIGIT as well as a sign**, so the valid set also fixes the VALUE: under `ibm`, `12C` is +123, `12L` is −123, `12{` is +120 and `12}` is −120, and at the LEADING position `C23` is +**3**23 (the punch contributes digit 3, exactly as it does at the trailing position of `12C`) — a sign-only reading would answer +123 and be wrong. The refused near-misses are worth naming: `+` and `-` are §13.18.52.4 GR6 b)'s SEPARATE characters and are NOT valid here, and neither are a space, a lower-case `l`, or a letter past the end of the run such as `Z`. **The set is DERIVED from the same `ZonedSign` tables the encoder punches with** — `ZonedSign.IsSignCharacter`, reached from `CobolClass.IsNumericZoned` (the class condition, emitted by `ConditionRenderer.RenderClass`) and from `CobolNum.IsNumericImage` (§14.6.13.2 rule 2's checked sending read, which the standard defines BY REFERENCE to the class condition) — never written a second time; it previously stood as a literal character-range pattern beside the tables and could only ever have answered for one convention. **GR5 a) is documented with it because it is NOT latitude:** the sign is *presumed to be associated with* a DIGIT position, so `PIC S9(3) SIGN IS LEADING` and `PIC S9(3) SIGN IS TRAILING` are each **3** character positions, never 4. What a numeric operation makes of a character OUTSIDE the valid set is a different obligation (§14.6.13.2 / EC-DATA-INCOMPATIBLE) and is not filed here. | `conformance:2023/pb803_sign_valid_set`; `conformance:2023/pb803_sign_encoding_ascii`; `conformance:85/pb803_sign_default_representation_85` |
| DOC-A.1-22 | **CHAR-NATIONAL function — which one of the multiple characters is returned**, §15.16.4 r2 (second sentence), required + documented | §15.16.4 r2 has TWO branches and only the second is an implementor obligation. **Branch 1 — the order is DEFINED** (an `ALPHABET … FOR NATIONAL` literal phrase with `ALSO`): the standard itself answers — “the character returned is the first character defined for that character position”, and §12.3.7.4 GR7 k)6 fixes which one that is (“Literal-1 is the first character in the sequence of multiple characters defined at that ordinal position”). WiseOwl COBOL obeys it (`NationalCollation.CharAt` reads the emitted `__COLLATE_NAT` table in the alphabet’s own written order), but that is CONFORMANCE, not a determination. **Branch 2 — the order is UNDEFINED, and this is item 22:** an `ALPHABET … FOR NATIONAL IS LOCALE` sequence is an ALGORITHM, not a written order, so every set of characters the locale collates EQUALLY shares one ordinal position with no order among its members. **WiseOwl COBOL returns the LOWEST-CODED member of that rank** — `LocaleCollation.OrderVector.FirstOfRank`, a vector materialized once per collator, so the answer is the same for every invocation at a given implementation, collating sequence and ordinal position, exactly as r2’s third sentence requires. (Position 1 of a locale national sequence is the completely-ignorable rank; its lowest-coded member is U+0000.) Determination **L7** (§4 locale facility) states the position model this refines; `HIGH-VALUE`/`LOW-VALUE` read `LastOfRank`/`FirstOfRank` of the same vector. | `conformance:2002/l1_char_national_locale_tie`; `conformance:2002/l1_char_national_also_positions` |
| DOC-A.1-180 | **SMALLEST-ALGEBRAIC function — usage of the argument when native arithmetic is in effect**, §15.83.3 r4, required + documented | **No restriction beyond the standard's own rules 2 and 3** (kb/Work PB122, 2026-08-29 — supersedes the earlier refusal of floating-point usages, whose rationale — that a smallest increment for an IEEE float is exponent-dependent — confused the ULP of a stored VALUE with the format minimum §15.83.1 actually asks for; r4's latitude also could not license the refusal under the STANDARD modes, where it was an over-rejection). Every numeric usage is accepted. A fixed-point argument returns **10^(−scale)** (§15.83.4 RVR2 — `S999` → +1, `99V9(3)` → 0.001, `S9PP` → +100, `BINARY-CHAR` → +1). A floating-point argument returns **the smallest positive (subnormal) value of the item's CARRIER** — the smallest algebraic difference between two representable values (§15.83.1), a property of the format exactly as 10^(−scale) is of a PICTURE: binary32 carriers (COMP-1 / FLOAT / FLOAT-SHORT / FLOAT-BINARY-32) → 2^−149 = 1.401298464324817070923729583289916E−45, binary64 carriers (COMP-2 / FLOAT-LONG / FLOAT-EXTENDED / FLOAT-BINARY-64) → 2^−1074 = 4.940656458412465441765687928682214E−324 (34 correctly-rounded digits — full decimal128 precision; the HIGHEST/LOWEST max folds keep R10's round-trip grain), the decimal carrier → 1E−28. The §15.83.4 r1 IN-ARITHMETIC-RANGE screen on the entry is enforced beside the r2/r3 usage bars (every carrier declarable today passes under both reachable modes; the guard fires automatically if a wider carrier ever lands). Pinned by `2023/pb122_smallest_algebraic_float` and `Algebraic_SmallestFloat_FoldsToCarrierSubnormal`. | `conformance:2023/pb122_smallest_algebraic_float` |
| DOC-A.1-183 | **SPECIAL-NAMES paragraph, ALPHABET clause, code-name-1 - the alphanumeric coded character set and collating sequence, the ordinal number of characters, and the correspondence with the native alphanumeric character set**, ISO 12.3.7.4 GR7 i, **conditionally required** ("conditioned upon the existence of a code-name") + documented | **Two code-names exist, so this item is LIVE** (it was vacuous until owner decision kb/Work PB793 filled the SR15 set; see A.1 item 184). GR7 i asks for three things per name, and Table 6 gives code-name-1 a Y in both the coded-character-set and the collating-sequence column, so each name answers all three.<br>**`ASCII` - the ISO/IEC 646 International Reference Version.** *Coded character set:* the 128 characters of ISO/IEC 646 IRV. *Ordinal number of characters:* 128; ordinal n is the character at ordinal position n of that set. *Correspondence with the native alphanumeric character set:* the IDENTITY on U+0000-U+007F - ordinal n corresponds to native character U+(n-1) - because the first 128 positions of the native alphanumeric character set ARE ISO/IEC 646 IRV (item 188 / determination D-N1). *Collating position of each character:* its native collating position, i.e. the sequence IS the native order, which is what 12.3.7.4 GR7 c already says of the STANDARD-1 keyword ("The collating sequence referenced by both the STANDARD-1 and STANDARD-2 phrases shall be as defined by the order in which characters appear in ISO/IEC 646"). `ASCII` is therefore a supported SPELLING of a set the standard also names; it is provided because other implementations spell it that way and a program that does is not otherwise portable. No runtime carrier is emitted for it.<br>**`EBCDIC` - IBM CCSID 37 (code page 037, "EBCDIC US/Canada").** *Coded character set:* the 256 characters of CCSID 37. *Ordinal number of characters:* 256; ordinal n is the character CCSID 37 assigns to code unit n-1. *Correspondence with the native alphanumeric character set:* the CCSID 37 code page itself - the whole table, derived at compile time from .NET's `Encoding.GetEncoding(37)` and never transcribed (`ImplementorCodeNames.SingleByteCodePage`). It is a bijection of the 256 code units onto exactly the 256 native characters U+0000-U+00FF. *Collating position of each character:* a character the page spells takes the position equal to its CCSID 37 code unit (so X'81' `a` &lt; X'C1' `A` &lt; X'F0' `0`, the reverse of the native order on both pairs). **Every OTHER native character** - the 65,280 UTF-16 code units above U+00FF that CCSID 37 does not spell - **takes a position greater than every specified one, in unchanged native relative order**: this implementation adopts 12.3.7.4 GR7 k3's own rule ("Any characters of the native collating sequence that are not specified in the literal phrase shall assume a position in the collating sequence that is greater than that of the highest character specified in this literal phrase. The relative order within the set of these unspecified characters is unchanged from the native collating sequence") as its GR7 i specification, so every native character has a position and none shares one. Those characters are NOT members of the coded character set: they have no ordinal and no medium representation, so no SYMBOLIC CHARACTERS or CLASS ordinal reaches them and a CODE-SET record containing one cannot be written (13.18.13.4 GR6 b). **HIGH-VALUE / LOW-VALUE under this sequence** (12.3.7.4 GR8/GR9): LOW-VALUE is the native character CCSID 37 puts at code unit X'00'; HIGH-VALUE is U+FFFF, the last character of the unspecified tail the GR7 k3 rule above places after the page's 256 (owner decision kb/Work R52 — the whole native repertoire, not the Latin-1 block, kb/Work PB1093). On a `CODE-SET IS EBCDIC` medium HIGH-VALUE is written as X'FF', the coded character set's highest code unit (owner decision kb/Work R51 item 4; `CodeSetConversion.HighUnit`), and X'FF' reads back as HIGH-VALUE. **Which EBCDIC**, surveyed rather than assumed: CCSID 37 is the canonical registered complete single-byte EBCDIC page and the z/OS default, i.e. the one the compiler whose users write `ALPHABET ... IS EBCDIC` means; GnuCOBOL's own default translation table corresponds to no registered CCSID and its named alternates are CCSID 500 variants, so following its LATITUDE (which names are accepted) does not mean adopting its table - none of its tables or sources are used here. CCSID 500 differs from 37 in five punctuation positions and is the obvious second row for whoever needs it. | `conformance:2023/pb793_code_name_alphabets`; `conformance:2023/pb793_code_set_ebcdic`; `conformance:85/pb793_code_name_alphabets_85` |
| DOC-A.1-184 | **SPECIAL-NAMES paragraph, ALPHABET clause, code-name-1 and code-name-2 - the names supported for code-name-1 and code-name-2**, ISO 12.3.7.3 SR15, **optional** + documented-if-provided | **code-name-1: `ASCII` and `EBCDIC`. code-name-2: NONE.** SR15 - "The implementor shall specify the names supported for code-name-1 and code-name-2 in the ALPHABET clause, if any" - leaves the set to this implementation, and "if any" licenses an empty one in either class. The ALPHANUMERIC class is not empty (owner decision kb/Work PB793, following the surveyed model implementations where the standard leaves a point open and the vendors split); what each name MEANS is item **183**. The NATIONAL class IS empty and item **185** is therefore vacuous: the native national character set is the UTF-16 repertoire (determination D-N1) and the standard's own `UCS-4` / `UTF-8` / `UTF-16` keywords already name the ISO/IEC 10646 forms, so no national code-name would name a set that is not already reachable. The OTHER words that may stand alone in an alphabet definition are the general format's own keywords - `NATIVE`, `STANDARD-1`, `STANDARD-2` in the ALPHANUMERIC branch; `NATIVE`, `UCS-4`, `UTF-8`, `UTF-16` in the NATIONAL one; `LOCALE` in either - and the 12.3.7.4 GR10 figurative constants of a one-operand literal phrase. Any OTHER bare word is a source error, **COBOLNET1907** `alphabet-code-name`, whose message names the supported set from the table itself. &#x26D4; That refusal closes an under-rejection that was also a WRONG ANSWER: an unrecognized code-name used to be reinterpreted as a LITERAL PHRASE SPELLING OUT ITS OWN LETTERS, so `ALPHABET A-ASC IS ASCII` built an alphabet whose first four positions were A, S, C, I and every downstream reference (PROGRAM COLLATING SEQUENCE, SORT, SYMBOLIC CHARACTERS ... IN, CLASS ... IN, CODE-SET) read that (kb/Work PB770 leg e). The supported set is ONE table (`ImplementorCodeNames`), read by the binder, by the coded-character-set reference sites and by the diagnostic alike, so the names, their meanings and the message can never disagree. Witnesses, ONE PER CLASS plus the acceptance: `conformance:negative/pb770-alphabet-unsupported-code-name` refuses a code-name-1 outside the set, `conformance-test:AlphabetLiteralPhraseTests.NationalUnsupportedCodeName_IsSR15` refuses code-name-2 on the empty national class, and `conformance:2023/pb793_code_name_alphabets` accepts both supported spellings. | `conformance:negative/pb770-alphabet-unsupported-code-name`; `conformance-test:AlphabetLiteralPhraseTests.NationalUnsupportedCodeName_IsSR15`; `conformance:2023/pb793_code_name_alphabets`; `conformance:85/pb793_code_name_alphabets_85` |
| DOC-A.1-185 | **SPECIAL-NAMES paragraph, ALPHABET clause, code-name-2 - the national coded character set and collating sequence, the ordinal number of characters, and the correspondence with the native national character set**, ISO 12.3.7.4 GR7 j, **conditionally required** ("conditioned upon the existence of a code-name") + documented | **VACUOUS - there is no code-name-2.** A.1 conditions this item "upon the existence of a code-name", and A.1 item 184 records the national half of the SR15 set as empty, so there is no national coded character set, no ordinal numbering and no native correspondence for this item to describe. It is stated rather than omitted because the condition is a DECISION (kb/Work PB793) that could change: the code-name table is keyed by class and the binder asks it for both, so a national row would wake this item without a code change. The refusal that proves the class is empty is the witness. | `conformance-test:AlphabetLiteralPhraseTests.NationalUnsupportedCodeName_IsSR15` |
| DOC-A.1-188 | **SPECIAL-NAMES ALPHABET clause, UCS-4, UTF-8 and UTF-16 phrases — correspondence with the native character set**, §12.3.7.4 GR7, required + documented | The native alphanumeric and national repertoires are **UTF-16, one code unit per character position** (the D-N1 substrate). The UTF-16 phrase's correspondence with the native set is therefore the **BMP identity** — code point = code unit, and §8.5.1.4's denial of surrogate-pair recognition makes the supplementary-plane codepoint/code-unit divergence unreachable. **UTF-8 and UTF-16 name coded character sets ONLY** — Table 6 leaves their collating-sequence column empty, and GR7 g/h give each only a correspondence — so naming one where a collating sequence is required (PROGRAM COLLATING SEQUENCE, SORT/MERGE, a file COLLATING SEQUENCE) is a syntax-rule violation. **UCS-4 names BOTH** (Table 6 row UCS-4, and GR7 f: “The collating sequence referenced by the UCS-4 phrase shall be defined by the order in which characters appear in ISO/IEC 10646”); on the one-code-unit-per-position D-N1 substrate that ISO/IEC 10646 appearance order over the 65,536 single-position characters **is code-unit order**, i.e. the NATIVE national sequence, and §8.5.1.4’s denial of surrogate-pair recognition puts the only place the two could diverge out of reach. UTF-8’s external form is converted to/from the native UTF-16 form at a codec boundary. `CollatingModel` carries this split (`HasCollatingSequence` false for exactly UTF-8 and UTF-16). **A supplementary character named by a NUMERIC ordinal under a CLASS clause's `IN` phrase** (`CLASS C FOR NATIONAL IS n IN alphabet-name-4`, alphabet-name-4 UCS-4 or UTF-8) is a legal operand (SR17 c2: the ordinal exists in the IN set), it is one character (SR17 c4 speaks of national LITERALS only), and outside a THROUGH phrase its two UTF-16 code units are members of the class; but under a THROUGH phrase GR12 expands "the contiguous characters in the native character set" between the bounds, and a character that needs two code units has no position in the native set's run — the specification says nothing for that pair, so this implementation REFUSES it, under its own message naming GR12 (never under c4), rather than invent a run over code points the national substrate cannot hold (implementor-defined, kb/Work PB1091). | `conformance:2023/l1_alphabet_ucs4_utf8_utf16_correspondence` |
| DOC-A.1-189 | **SPECIAL-NAMES paragraph, device-name (names available, restrictions on use)**, ISO 12.3.7.3 SR7 and SR8, **optional** + documented-if-provided | **Provided. The device-names are `CONSOLE`, `SYSIN`, `SYSOUT` and `SYSERR`**: `CONSOLE` = the process standard input (ACCEPT) and standard output (DISPLAY); `SYSIN` = standard input only; `SYSOUT` = standard output only; `SYSERR` = standard error, output only. SR8 - "The implementor shall specify the names that are available for switch-name-1, feature-name-1, and device-name-1" - is answered by ONE table, `ImplementorNames` (`src/Cobol.Net.Compiler/Binding/ImplementorNames.cs`), which is also the answer to items **190** and **191**; a SPECIAL-NAMES entry naming anything else is **COBOLNET2241** (kb/Work PB862), whose message lists the available names FROM the table. ⛔ Before that table `SPECIAL-NAMES. WIBBLE WOBBLE.` compiled and registered a mnemonic naming nothing, and a misspelled `CONSOEL IS PRT` surfaced only at the DISPLAY that used it, as a device 'CONSOEL' that "is not capable of receiving data". **Restrictions on use** (SR7's "additional restrictions"): a device's mnemonic-name-3 may be the FROM operand of ACCEPT only when the device has input capability and the UPON operand of DISPLAY only when it has output capability (`COBOLNET0817`, §14.9.1.3 SR2 / §14.9.11.3 SR2); by SR7 itself it may appear in no other statement - `SET` refuses it (`COBOLNET1757`, §14.9.39.3 SR5) and `WRITE ... ADVANCING` refuses it (`COBOLNET2243`, §14.9.51.3 SR16). Item **2** and item **59** state the default devices. | `conformance:85/pb862_implementor_names`; `conformance:negative/pb862-unavailable-device-name`; `unit:ImplementorNamesDriftTests.EveryTableName_IsDocumented_AndNamedByTheDiagnostic` |
| DOC-A.1-190 | **SPECIAL-NAMES paragraph, feature-name (names available, any positioning rules, any restrictions on use)**, ISO 12.3.7.3 SR6 and SR8, **optional** + documented-if-provided | **Provided. The feature-names are `C01` and `CSP`**, and their only use is as the ADVANCING operand of a WRITE statement (SR6; §14.9.51.3 SR16). **Positioning rules** (§14.9.51.4 GR25 d - item **222**): `C01` - skip to channel 1, the top of the next page, which is exactly the `ADVANCING PAGE` advance of the same statement; `CSP` - suppress spacing, a ZERO-line advance: exactly ADVANCING 0's overprint of the same statement, written as a bare carriage return (§3 "DETERMINATION — ADVANCING 0 is an overprint"). ⚖ Surveyed (owner-decision protocol, `follow_gnucobol_on_split_latitude`): IBM Enterprise COBOL, Micro Focus and GnuCOBOL all spell both names with these meanings. Channels 2-12 (`C02`-`C12`) are **not** provided: each names a stop on a physical carriage-control tape, and a line-sequential print file has no forms-control model to position against. **Restrictions on use**: none beyond SR6 - a feature's mnemonic in ACCEPT or DISPLAY is `COBOLNET0817`, in SET `COBOLNET1757`; a WRITE whose file has a LINAGE clause may not name one (§14.9.51.3 SR13). A name outside the table is **COBOLNET2241** (item **189** carries the one-table rule). The NIST SQ207M placeholder `XXXXX073` is supplied as `CSP` by the NIST preprocessor, preserving the zero-line advance its golden encodes. | `conformance-test:FileIoDifferentialTests.WriteAdvancingC01_IsThePageAdvance`; `conformance-test:FileIoDifferentialTests.WriteAdvancingMnemonic_ZeroLineAdvance_RecordAlwaysReleased`; `conformance:negative/pb862-write-advancing-device-mnemonic` |
| DOC-A.1-191 | **SPECIAL-NAMES paragraph, switch-name (names available, which switches may be referenced by the SET statement, scope of, and external facility for modification)**, ISO 8.3.2.3.11 and 12.3.7.3 SR8, 12.3.7.4 GR2-GR4, **optional** + documented-if-provided | **Provided. The switch-names are `SWITCH-0` through `SWITCH-36` (GnuCOBOL's set) and `UPSI-0` through `UPSI-7` (IBM's User Program Status Indicators).** Every one may be referenced by the SET statement (GR3). The **scope** of every switch is the RUN UNIT - one switch shared by every runtime element (GR4 and its NOTE 1). The **external facility** that supplies a switch's initial status is the process environment variable `COBOL_<SWITCH-NAME>` with hyphens as underscores (`SWITCH-1` -> `COBOL_SWITCH_1`): `ON`, `1` or `TRUE` (any case) means on, anything else or absent means off; the value is read at the first interrogation and a later SET governs for the rest of the run unit (`SwitchStore`). Only a switch-name may carry ON STATUS / OFF STATUS condition-names, in either order, each at most once (§12.3.7.2 choice indicators, §5.2.6.4); a device-name or feature-name written with one is **COBOLNET2241**, because a system-name belongs to exactly one type (§8.3.2.3.1). A name outside the table is COBOLNET2241 too (item **189** carries the one-table rule). ⚖ Before kb/Work PB862 ANY word was accepted as a switch-name - a determination that could not stand beside a device-name set, since §8.3.2.3.1 forbids a name belonging to two types. | `conformance:85/pb862_implementor_names`; `conformance-test:AlterSwitchesDifferentialTests.Switch_EnvironmentVariableSuppliesInitialState`; `unit:ImplementorNamesDriftTests.EveryTableName_IsAccepted_InTheArmItsKindAllows` |
| DOC-A.1-221 | **WRITE statement (mnemonic-name-1)**, ISO 14.9.51.3 SR16, **optional** + documented-if-provided | **Provided.** A WRITE's ADVANCING operand may be a mnemonic-name associated with one of the feature-names of item **190** (`C01`, `CSP`); a mnemonic associated with a switch-name or a device-name is **COBOLNET2243** ("When mnemonic-name-1 is specified, the name is associated with a feature-name specified by the implementor"), and with a LINAGE file SR13 refuses any mnemonic. | `conformance:negative/pb862-write-advancing-device-mnemonic`; `conformance-test:FileIoDifferentialTests.WriteAdvancingC01_IsThePageAdvance` |
| DOC-A.1-222 | **WRITE statement (page advance when mnemonic-name-1 specified)**, ISO 14.9.51.4 GR25 d, **conditionally required** + documented | Item **221** provides mnemonic-name-1, so this item is LIVE. The page is advanced per the feature-name's row in `ImplementorNames`: `C01` advances exactly as `ADVANCING PAGE` does in the same position (BEFORE or AFTER); `CSP` advances zero lines. | `conformance-test:FileIoDifferentialTests.WriteAdvancingC01_IsThePageAdvance`; `conformance-test:FileIoDifferentialTests.WriteAdvancingMnemonic_ZeroLineAdvance_RecordAlwaysReleased` |
| DOC-A.1-123 | **Native arithmetic — techniques used, intermediate data item**, §8.8.1.3 / §11.9.5 / §14.7.7, required (the internal procedure need not be documented) | **The native intermediate data item is a scaled `Int128`** — a 128-bit two's-complement integer holding the unscaled value with a compile-time decimal scale (magnitude bound ≈ 1.7 × 10^38; every ≤ 31-digit operand and every aligned sum of them is exact). Any expression with a floating-point operand — or whose resultant identifiers are all floating-point — evaluates entirely in IEEE binary64 instead (that binary64 intermediate IS the arithmetic value §14.7.4.3 rounds into a floating-point resultant: a binary64 resultant holds it unchanged under every ROUNDED mode, a binary32 one takes the mode's neighbour, and an exact scaled or SDIDI value is rounded into either format ONCE — kb/Work PB1196); a nested quotient carries up to 14 guard fraction digits and rounds once, at the final transfer (§14.7 NOTE 1). **A floating-point operand means a floating-point ITEM, a floating-point carrier literal or a floating-point receiver** — a floating-math function's returned value and a non-integer native power are engine-produced approximations (item 92), not operands "described with" a float usage, so beside non-float operands they join the arithmetic through their shortest-round-trip decimal and the SDIDI lane (kb/Work PB1641; `pb1641_function_result_beside_decimal_operands`). **The scaled `Int128` is used for a product only where the operands' DIGIT BOUNDS prove it fits** (the PICTURE's digit count for an item — a COMP-5 item owns its whole container range — and the digit count of a literal; the exact sum, difference and product of bounded operands are bounded in turn): N unscaled digits times M unscaled digits is below 10^(N+M), so N+M ≤ 38 is a bare multiply, N+M > 38 forms the exact 256-bit product — **and when that product IS the final transfer to a resultant (a single-receiver COMPUTE, MULTIPLY BY, MULTIPLY GIVING, and each receiver of a several-receiver MULTIPLY GIVING or COMPUTE whose right-hand side is a product) it is rounded ONCE, at that resultant's scale with that resultant's own mode (`CobolDec.MulAtScale`)**, whatever width the resultant is: a 16-byte COMP-5 item owns a 38-digit container (§13.18.60.4 GR12), so its ROUNDED phrase — PROHIBITED included — must see every digit of a 38-digit product, and the SDIDI's 34 would hide them (kb/Work PB1143's review finding N1: `COMPUTE R5 = E * F` of two 20-digit operands into `PIC S9(31) COMP-5` stored …5370000 for …5361999). A product past the Int128 carrier at that scale is, checked, the size error EC-SIZE-TRUNCATION (§14.7.5 case 3; the receiver is unchanged) and, unchecked, keeps the low-order 38 digits a store can use — the same two dispositions as an SDIDI value's final transfer. **A product NESTED in a larger expression** (`A * B - C * D`, a relation operand) is not a transfer: its intermediate rounding is the implementor's (§8.8.1.3), so it is formed on the SDIDI (reduced to 34 significant digits by ROUND-TO-ODD — an inexact product keeps an odd last digit, so a receiver of at most 32 digit positions, which every PICTURE-limited one is (§13.18.40.3 SR14 caps them at 31), rounds it in every ROUNDED mode and PROHIBITED exactly as it would round the exact value; a 16-byte COMP-5 receiver of a compound expression holds more than that, and a compound expression whose intermediates exceed 34 digits is the determination of this item, never a claim of exactness), and an unknown bound (an intrinsic's value, a windowed view) uses the checked multiply in EVERY statement — a native product never wraps (kb/Work PB1143; `PIC 9V9(30)` times `PIC 9V9(30)` is legal because §14.7.7 r2 a) caps the COMPOSITE, not the unscaled product, and GnuCOBOL's arbitrary-precision decimal gives the exact product). The DIVIDE kernel's radix alignment never wraps either: a dividend that scales up past the carrier is formed on the exact 256-bit numerator and the quotient rounds ONCE by its true remainder (`CobolDec.QuotientAtScale`, so a 38-digit quotient reaches a 16-byte COMP-5 receiver whole — `pb1143_quotient_into_wide_receiver`); only a divisor scaled past the carrier, or an alignment past 10^38, takes the SDIDI (34 digits, round-to-odd); and a quotient past the carrier is EC-SIZE-OVERFLOW. The statistics functions SUM / RANGE / MEAN / MEDIAN / MIDRANGE align their arguments to a common scale; when the arguments' digit bounds show that alignment can leave the carrier the function is evaluated on the SDIDI instead (kb/Work PB621; `pb621_statistics_beyond_the_aligned_carrier`), so a value the receiver holds is never refused for the carrier's width — an argument of unknown bound keeps the escape-checked native arm (item 179), and MOD / REM keep it too (their value is a difference of nearly equal magnitudes that the 34-digit SDIDI cannot always carry, so they refuse loudly rather than answer an approximation). One value — the maximum of an unsigned 16-byte COMP-5 item, 2^128−1 — exceeds the intermediate range while being storable in an item; see item 179 for what an arithmetic reference to it does. | `conformance:2023/l1_native_arithmetic_intermediate`; `conformance:2023/l1_native_arith_lane_and_rounding`; `conformance:2002/pb125_factorial_native_size`; `conformance:2014/pb1196_float_resultant_rounded_mode`; `conformance:2023/pb1641_function_result_beside_decimal_operands`; `conformance:2023/pb1143_native_product_beyond_scaled_int128`; `conformance:2023/pb1143_product_into_wide_receiver`; `conformance:2023/pb1143_quotient_into_wide_receiver`; `conformance:2023/pb621_statistics_beyond_the_aligned_carrier`; `unit:CobolNumDivideAlignmentTests` |
| DOC-A.1-70 | **Fatal exception condition — whether execution continues, and how a numeric receiving operand is affected, when checking for the condition is NOT enabled: the arithmetic size error condition without a SIZE ERROR phrase (EC-SIZE-TRUNCATION, §14.7.5 no-phrase rule 4) and a non-finite value in a fixed-point landing (EC-DATA-NOT-FINITE)**, §14.6.13.1.3 item 8, required + documented (this row discharges the numeric-store family; other fatal conditions' dispositions are documented with their statements) | **Execution continues with the next statement, and the resultant identifier receives the LOW-ORDER digits of the result aligned at its scale** — the classic high-order truncation (`CobolNum.Store`), for EVERY value carrier: the standard-decimal intermediate (`CobolDec.ToUnscaled`, kb/Work PB74), the native scaled-`Int128` lane (`CobolNum.RescaleStoreCap` — the widening `CobolNum.Store` and `CobolEdit.Format` take, and the unchecked arm of the exact family's `CobolIntrinsics.Rescaled`, and the §14.2.3 GR9/GR10 CALL-argument landing `CobolArgAdapt.Land`; kb/Work PB288, whose crossing IS "a COMPUTE statement without the ROUNDED phrase" into a record of the formal's description and so takes this same no-phrase disposition, where it used to wrap the `Int128` intermediate in binary; and the same crossing into a FLOATING-POINT formal, which lands through `FloatResultant` — `CobolArgAdapt.LandFloat`, kb/Work PB1114 — under implied TRUNCATION, a value past the format's range landing at its largest finite value unchecked and raising EC-SIZE-TRUNCATION under checking), and a binary64 (`CobolFloat.ToScaledUnchecked` / `CobolIntrinsics.FromDouble`'s unchecked form — the exact decimal expansion past the Int128 carrier; kb/Work PB77, 2026-08-18). Before PB77 a value past the carrier landed the saturation SENTINEL and the store kept its low digits: `COMPUTE X5 = D40` (a COMP-2 holding 1.0E+40, PIC 9(5)) stored 03715 — the low digits of `Int128.MaxValue` — where the result's are 90752. A non-finite binary64 (NaN, ±Infinity) landing in a fixed-point receiver with checking off stores ZERO. "Low-order digits" is a DECIMAL statement for a DISPLAY / COMP / PACKED receiver and the CONTAINER's two's-complement residue for a BinaryCapacity one (COMP-5 / the BINARY-CHAR family, §13.18.60.4 GR12); it is never the Int128 carrier's own binary wrap taken modulo the picture. Until kb/Work PB639 (2026-09-05) the inline arithmetic store and the MOVE composed exactly those two — the alignment multiplied inside the carrier and only THEN met the decimal capacity — so `COMPUTE WC = 1000000000000000000000000000000` into `PIC S9(9)V9(9)` stored −123822295.304634368 where 10^39's low-order 18 digits are all zero; the determination and the code had disagreed on this row's own primary path since it was written. The other resultant identifiers of a multiple-receiver statement are stored normally. **An INTERMEDIATE that cannot be formed at all** — a standard-decimal intermediate past decimal128 (§14.7.5 case 7 / no-phrase rule 3, `10 ** 100000`), a native value past the Int128 escape boundary in a value-semantics position (case 5, item 179 (3)), an exponentiation-rule violation (case 1), a zero divisor (case 2) — **has no result to store: with checking not enabled execution does NOT continue; the run unit terminates abnormally** ("abnormal run-unit termination: EC-SIZE-… (fatal): …", exit 1 — kb/Work PB75, 2026-08-18; it used to be an unhandled .NET stack trace, exit 127), whether the raise came from an arithmetic statement without a phrase or from a condition / argument / subscript. ⛔ **The zero divisor is raised by EVERY value lane's quotient kernel, checked or not** (kb/Work PB1605): `CobolNum.Divide` (the scaled `Int128` carrier), `CobolFloat.Div` (binary64 — the unchecked arm of `NumericRenderer.CombineReal`) and `CobolDec.Div` (the standard-decimal intermediate) all throw the one `CobolSizeError.ZeroDivide()`, because §14.7.5 case 2 is the condition whether or not checking is enabled and checking decides only its disposition. Until PB1605 the first two answered with checking off — `COMPUTE A = B / Z` stored 000 and `COMPUTE FR = FL / FZ` stored +Infinity (NaN for 0 / 0), and the run went on — so the code contradicted this row on the scaled and binary64 lanes while the SDIDI lane already terminated. Survey under the owner's precedence (ISO → GnuCOBOL → IBM / Micro Focus; §14.6.13.1.3 item 8 is the latitude): GnuCOBOL 3.2 continues with the resultant unchanged (its decimal divide marks the quotient not-a-number and the store skips it), but its own source flags that handling as a known defect whose intended behaviour is to abort on the fatal condition — so that rung offers no settled model, and this row's family determination (termination, which the SDIDI zero divisor and the exponentiation-rule violation already took) stands for the zero divisor on every lane. Pinned by `SizeErrorDispositionTests.ZeroDivisor_CheckingOff_TerminatesNamingEcSizeZeroDivide` (both native lanes, COMPUTE / DIVIDE INTO / DIVIDE GIVING REMAINDER / a nested quotient / a condition, and the SDIDI lane), its phrase-path control, and `ZeroDivisorLaneTests`. With checking enabled the §14.6.13.1.3 #4/#5/#7 path applies to those statements exactly as to arithmetic ones (golden `pb75_sdidi_overflow_outside_arithmetic`, `SizeErrorDispositionTests`). With the phrase or with EC-SIZE checking enabled the disposition is §14.7.5's own — the receiver is left unchanged and the size error condition is processed — because the CHECKED landing saturates so the capacity check sees it (item 179, kb/Work PB13). ⛔ **The §14.2.3 GR9/GR10 ARGUMENT CROSSING takes that checked disposition IN THE ACTIVATING ELEMENT** (kb/Work PB640): GR9's second branch and GR10 allocate the linkage record "by the activating runtime element during the process of initiating the activation", so the crossing's COMPUTE runs under the ACTIVATING element's `>>TURN EC-SIZE` state — with checking on, a value further from zero than the formal's description permits raises EC-SIZE-TRUNCATION there (`CobolArgAdapt.LandForFormal` → `CobolNum.StoreOrRaise`), the activating element's USE declaratives run, and §14.9.4.4 GR3 g) does NOT transfer control, so the callee never executes. With checking OFF the low-order digits above stand. The same rule and the same two dispositions cover the INVOKE lane (§14.2.3 GR9's "a method"; §14.8.2.3.3 rule 2 a)), whose landing was already caller-side but unconditionally the unchecked one. ⛔ **ROUNDED MODE IS PROHIBITED on a float→fixed arithmetic transfer is raised by `CobolIntrinsics.FromDouble` itself** under the SIZE ERROR phrase or EC-SIZE checking (§14.7.4.3 rule 7 — EC-SIZE-TRUNCATION, the receiver left unchanged), because since kb/Work PB647 a final transfer lands AT the resultant identifier's scale and the store's rescale is the identity, so the store's own PROHIBITED test can no longer see the discarded fraction. Before the move the condition depended on the receiver's SCALE rather than on the transfer's exactness: the same statement raised into `PIC 9V9` and stored silently into `PIC 9V9(9)`. ⛔ **ROUNDED MODE IS PROHIBITED with checking NOT enabled and no SIZE ERROR phrase** (kb/Work PB1196): §14.7.4.3 rule 7 sets EC-SIZE-TRUNCATION, and with checking off §14.6.13.1.3 item 8 leaves "how any receiving operands are affected" to the implementor. WiseOwl COBOL's determination, for a fixed-point AND a floating-point resultant alike: execution continues and the resultant receives the TRUNCATED value — what ROUNDED MODE IS TRUNCATION would store (`COMPUTE N ROUNDED MODE IS PROHIBITED = -2.5` into `PIC S9` stores −2; into a FLOAT-SHORT, 0.1 stores 0.0999999940…). With the phrase or with checking enabled the receiver is left unchanged, as rule 7 says. **A floating-point resultant** (`FloatResultant`, kb/Work PB1196) has no low-order digits to keep: a value past the format's largest finite magnitude after the mode's rounding (§14.7.5 case 3 / no-phrase rule 4, EC-SIZE-TRUNCATION) stores, with checking off, ISO/IEC 60559's overflow result for the mode — the largest finite magnitude when the mode rounds toward zero (TRUNCATION, PROHIBITED, TOWARD-LESSER on a positive value, TOWARD-GREATER on a negative one), ±Infinity otherwise; a NaN or ±Infinity VALUE transfers unchanged and is never that condition. Pinned by `2023/pb77_move_past_the_carrier` (the `CMP-*` rows against the `SIZE-*` rows), `2023/pb288_call_argument_scale_landing` (the CALL crossing against its inline MOVE/COMPUTE equivalents) and `CarrierLandingFormTests`. | `conformance:2023/pb77_move_past_the_carrier`; `conformance:2023/pb75_sdidi_overflow_outside_arithmetic`; `conformance:2023/pb288_call_argument_scale_landing`; `conformance:2023/pb639_store_landing_low_order_digits`; `conformance:2014/pb639_store_landing_low_order_digits`; `conformance:2002/pb639_store_landing_low_order_digits`; `conformance:2023/pb640_call_argument_landing_checked`; `conformance:2014/pb640_call_argument_landing_checked`; `conformance:2002/pb640_call_argument_landing_checked`; `conformance:2023/pb640_invoke_argument_landing_checked`; `conformance:2014/pb640_invoke_argument_landing_checked`; `conformance:2002/pb640_invoke_argument_landing_checked`; `unit:CarrierLandingFormTests.Store_PastTheCarrier_KeepsTheLowOrderDigits`; `unit:CarrierLandingFormTests.EditedReceiver_FollowsTheSameTwoLandings`; `unit:CarrierLandingFormTests.LowOrderDigits_PastTheCarrier_AreTheExactExpansions`; `conformance:2014/pb1196_float_resultant_rounded_mode`; `unit:FloatResultantTests.Prohibited_IsAnExactnessTest`; `conformance-test:SizeErrorDispositionTests.ZeroDivisor_CheckingOff_TerminatesNamingEcSizeZeroDivide`; `unit:ZeroDivisorLaneTests.Binary64_BothKernels_RaiseAZeroDivisor` |
| DOC-A.1-127 | **OBJECT-COMPUTER paragraph — computer-name and implied equipment configuration**, §12.3.6 GR2, optional (documented if provided) | **Not provided.** `computer-name-1` (ISO §12.3.6.2 — optional since 2002; kb/Work PB78) is accepted with any spelling and identifies no equipment configuration: there is ONE object computer, the .NET runtime the program is compiled for, whether the name is present, absent (§12.3.6.4 GR3), or the paragraph is omitted (GR4). The clauses that follow it (PROGRAM COLLATING SEQUENCE; CHARACTER CLASSIFICATION — A.4.9 item 7, implemented, kb/Work PB64 T5) behave identically with or without a name. | `conformance:2023/pb373_object_computer_name_inert` |
| DOC-A.1-128 | **OCCURS clause — range of values allowed in the index**, §13.18.38.4 GR2, required + documented | **The full signed 64-bit interval, −9 223 372 036 854 775 808 through +9 223 372 036 854 775 807.** In this data model an index IS its 1-based occurrence number, held in a C# `long` (each INDEXED BY name is an `IndexDeclaration` owning its own `long` cell, minted by `DataBinder.RegisterIndexes`; COBOLNET_DATA_MODEL_DESIGN D3), so the allowed range is that carrier's range — `CobolIndex.MinIndex` / `CobolIndex.MaxIndex`. §13.18.38.4 GR2 requires the range to include at least occurrence numbers (1 − integer-2) through (2 × integer-2); integer-2 cannot exceed this implementation's maximum occurrence count (`CobolDynTable.MaxOccurrences`, 2^30−1), so the required interval is contained with more than thirty bits to spare. ⛔ **A value inside the range but outside the TABLE is not out of range**: §14.9.39.4 GR2 a) 1. c and GR4 b) each end "even if that occurrence is not a valid occurrence within this table", so an index pointing past its own table is legal and must not raise — a table-bounds test here would reject conforming programs. GR2's own three statements enforce the range (kb/Work PB459): a SET amount lands through `CobolIndex.TryAmount` and a SET / PERFORM VARYING augment through `CobolIndex.Augment`, each of which sets EC-RANGE-INDEX and leaves the index unchanged on a value or result outside it (GR2 b's "undefined unless the value of the index is specified by the rules of that statement" — §14.9.39.4 GR2 a) 1. b and GR4 a) specify it, so it is not undefined). A SEARCH cannot reach the boundary at all: §14.9.37.4 GR4's initial-index guard leaves the scan only when the index is within the table's occurrence count, so its `+= 1` is bounded by that count. | `conformance:2023/pb459_set_index_amount_guards`; `conformance:2023/pb459_set_index_amount_ec`; `conformance:2002/pb459_set_index_range_ec`; `conformance:85/pb459_set_index_overflow_unchanged` |
| DOC-A.1-179 | **Size error condition — whether the intermediate data item's value range is checked**, §14.7.5 item 5, required + documented | **Checked, in five places.** (1) Whenever size-error checking is ENABLED for the statement — an ON SIZE ERROR phrase, or EC-SIZE checking turned on by `>>TURN` / an exception-checking PERFORM (kb/Work PB91, 2026-08-18: this now includes every arithmetic expression the statement evaluates, in a condition, a function argument, a subscript, a SET amount, not only the arithmetic statement's own operands) — multiplication, addition and subtraction are overflow-checked at the `Int128` intermediate boundary (`MulChecked`/`AddChecked`/`SubChecked` → `CobolSizeError`, the size error condition, §14.7.5 case 5; a zero divisor in a divide operation → EC-SIZE-ZERO-DIVIDE, case 2), disposed per the phrase or per §14.6.13.1.3 (golden `pb91_native_overflow_outside_arithmetic`); with checking NOT enabled an addition and a subtraction wrap unchecked, exactly as §14.7.5's "if … the implementor defines that the range … is to be checked" conditions the case on the implementor's determination — **but a native PRODUCT never wraps in any statement** (kb/Work PB1143): a product whose operands' digit bounds prove it fits is a bare multiply, one that may leave the carrier is formed exactly on the SDIDI (item 123), and one of unknown width takes `MulChecked` whether or not checking is enabled. Reachable: `HIGHEST-ALGEBRAIC` of `PIC S9(19) COMP-5` is exactly `Int128.MaxValue`, where `ADD 1` overflows the intermediate itself. (2) An arithmetic OPERAND whose value exceeds the intermediate range — possible only for an unsigned 16-byte COMP-5 item holding a value above 2^127−1 — raises the size error condition on reference (`CobolNum.Widen`), never a silent wrap: with ON SIZE ERROR the phrase takes it, without it the run fails loudly naming the operand. The value-preserving paths (MOVE, DISPLAY, relation conditions) are NOT arithmetic and carry the full container range. Pinned by `highest_algebraic_comp5_unsigned` (the `S19-ADD1=SIZE` / `W19-ADD1=SIZE` legs). **(3) An SDIDI-carried value entering the Int128 carrier as an INTERMEDIATE** — an intrinsic argument, an aligned arithmetic operand, a subscript, an integer argument (kb/Work PB69, 2026-08-18) — whose magnitude the carrier cannot hold at the required scale raises **EC-SIZE-OVERFLOW** (`CobolDec.ToUnscaledIntermediate`), never the modular low-order digits; under native arithmetic such a value arises only from an integer power past the carrier (the documented double approximation — an intrinsic with such an argument computes on its SDIDI body instead, a relation compares it exactly, an arithmetic operation with it evaluates on the SDIDI; a store of it is the checked §14.7 transfer, SIZE ERROR). Pinned by `2023/pb69_native_power_past_the_carrier` and `CobolDecDivisionTests`. **(4) The receiver-bound radix-point ALIGNMENT of a result, under checking** (kb/Work PB639, 2026-09-05). This is §14.7.5 case 3, not case 5 — a RECEIVER overflow "after radix point alignment" — but it shares the carrier, and the alignment multiply wraps: a widening the `Int128` carrier cannot form was aligned first and capacity-checked afterwards, so a wrap that landed back inside the picture read as fitting. `CobolNum.WideningFits` is now consulted BEFORE the multiply by all three siblings (`TryStore`, `RescaleChecked`, `RescaleEscape`) and by `CobolEdit.TryFormat`: a value the carrier cannot align is further from zero than ANY fixed-point receiver permits, so it is the size error condition and storing rule 2 leaves the receiver unchanged. Before this, `COMPUTE WC = 340282366920938463463374607432 ON SIZE ERROR` into `PIC S9(9)V9(9)` — a legal 30-digit literal that is ceil(2^128 / 10^9) — ran NOT ON SIZE ERROR and stored +0.231788544, and the same statement under `>>TURN EC-SIZE CHECKING ON` raised nothing at all. **(5) The binary64 intermediate** (kb/Work PB1566, 2026-09-24; PB1581 + PB1147, 2026-09-26). Under native arithmetic an expression with a floating-point operand, or whose resultants are all floating-point, evaluates in binary64 (item 123), and the §15.4.1 float family's returned value IS a binary64 (item 92), so binary64's range is that intermediate's range and a result outside it is this case: **EC-SIZE-OVERFLOW** when farther from zero, **EC-SIZE-UNDERFLOW** when a nonzero exact result rounds to zero (§14.7.5's no-phrase rule 3 names both; a subnormal is in range) — raised by the ONE `CobolFloat.InBinary64Range` check as the same `CobolSizeError` FACTORIAL raises past the Int128 intermediate. It covers (a) the native binary64 OPERATORS where size-error checking is enabled — `+ - * /` (`CobolFloat.AddChecked` / `SubChecked` / `MulChecked` / `DivChecked`) and `**` (`CobolIntrinsics.PowNativeRealChecked`), the floating arm's twins of the scaled lane's `MulChecked` / `AddChecked` / `DivideOrThrow`, including §14.7.5 case 2's zero divisor (EC-SIZE-ZERO-DIVIDE) — and, like that lane, the bare IEEE operators where checking is not enabled; and (b) the float family's returned values unconditionally, as FACTORIAL's is: EXP / EXP10 on every carrier (e^x is never zero, so a finite argument's zero result is the underflow), every SDIDI-evaluated list body's narrowed result (VARIANCE / STANDARD-DEVIATION / PRESENT-VALUE over an exact list, kb/Work PB1565), and the binary64 list bodies over a floating argument (SUM, MEAN, RANGE, MIDRANGE, MEDIAN, VARIANCE, STANDARD-DEVIATION, PRESENT-VALUE — each operation of the equivalent arithmetic expression, so `MIDRANGE(1.5E308 1.5E308)`'s sum overflows). A NON-FINITE operand or argument is not an operation leaving the range and keeps item 70's disposition. Before it `DISPLAY FUNCTION EXP(1000)` printed `Infinity`, `COMPUTE F = C * C ON SIZE ERROR` over a COMP-2 holding 1.0E+300 took NOT ON SIZE ERROR and stored `Infinity`, and `COMPUTE FR = FL / FZ ON SIZE ERROR` with a FLOAT-LONG zero divisor did the same. Pinned by `2002/pb1566_exp_result_range`, `2002/pb1581_binary64_intermediate_range`, `WholeRangeBodiesDriftTests.AResultOutsideBinary64_IsTheSizeErrorCondition` and `FloatResultantTests`. | `conformance:2023/highest_algebraic_comp5_unsigned`; `conformance:2023/pb69_native_power_past_the_carrier`; `conformance:2023/pb91_native_overflow_outside_arithmetic`; `conformance:2023/pb639_store_landing_low_order_digits`; `unit:CarrierLandingFormTests.TryStore_AWideningThatWrapsBackIntoCapacity_IsStillASizeError`; `conformance:2002/pb1566_exp_result_range`; `unit:WholeRangeBodiesDriftTests.AResultOutsideBinary64_IsTheSizeErrorCondition`; `conformance:2002/pb1581_binary64_intermediate_range`; `unit:FloatResultantTests.CheckedOperations_RaiseTheTable13Condition`; `unit:FloatResultantTests.ListBodies_FormTheirExpressionInTheIntermediate` |
| DOC-A.1-202 | **Time formats and corresponding function values — maximum number of digit positions in the decimal fraction of the seconds subfield**, §15.3.3.2, required + documented | **18.** §15.3.3.2 requires the implementor to define this maximum and requires it to be **≥ 9**; 18 is chosen because it is exactly where the value stops being exactly representable — a fraction of 19 or more digits overflows the emitter's `(long)Pow10.AsWide(width)` and prints garbage, so the documented bound and the representable range are the SAME number rather than two constants that can drift apart. Enforced by `DateTimeFormatGrammar.MaxFractionDigits`, which the §15.3 format recogniser consults, so a format with a longer fraction is rejected at COMPILE time (`COBOLNET1631`) instead of fabricating a value. | `conformance:2023/l1_time_fraction_max_digits` |
| DOC-A.1-92 | **Characteristics and representation of a numeric intrinsic function's returned value, and its text image**, §15.4.1 + §14.9.11.4 GR1, required — documentation NOT required by A.1 (voluntary); partially addresses A.1-56 | §15.4 places a function's returned value in a **temporary elementary data item**, and under NATIVE arithmetic §15.4.1 makes "the characteristics and representation of the returned value … defined by the implementor"; §14.9.11.4 GR1 independently makes any conversion between a DISPLAY operand and the device implementor-defined. **WiseOwl COBOL's determination: a numeric function result used as TEXT renders in the LITERAL form of its value** — the significant digits with **no leading-zero padding**, a leading `-` when negative (never a zoned over-punch, because this is not a stored item), and a decimal point followed by exactly the result's scale in fraction digits when the value is scaled. A FLOAT-valued function (SQRT, trig, financial) renders through the same shortest-round-trip `CobolFloat.Display` a COMP-2 item does, so the function and an item holding its value agree. **The §15.4 temporary a statement materializes the value into** (the MOVE multi-receiver hoist of §14.9.25.4 GR1, an EVALUATE subject, an INVOKE argument) **has the same text image**: it is flagged as the function's returned value and described at scale 0 for an INTEGER function (§15.2 item 5), so `MOVE FUNCTION INTEGER(N) TO A B` stores in each character receiver what `MOVE FUNCTION INTEGER(N) TO A` does (kb/Work PB1007; a NUMERIC function's temporary keeps the implementor scale 9, which only `--permissive` can move to a character receiver). **AND ITS LANDING IN A FIXED-POINT ARITHMETIC RECEIVER TAKES THE STATEMENT'S ROUNDING MODE, ONCE, AT THAT RECEIVER'S SCALE (kb/Work PB647).** The quantizer used to round NEAREST-AWAY-FROM-ZERO at a working scale whatever the statement said, while the MOVE channel took the receiver's mode at the receiver's scale, so one returned value landed two ways in one receiver: with `S PIC 9V9(9)`, `MOVE FUNCTION SQRT(3) TO S` gave 1.732050807 and `COMPUTE S = FUNCTION SQRT(3)` gave 1.732050808, and `FUNCTION SQRT(0.9999999999)` into `PIC 9V9` split across a whole tenth. §15.4.1's identity sentence forbids that outright, and §8.8.1.3's native latitude reaches the INTERMEDIATE, not the transfer into a resultant identifier, which §14.7.4.1 puts "relative to the size provided for the resultant identifier" and §14.7.4.3 rule 2 makes TRUNCATION when no ROUNDED phrase is written. **The determination is therefore: the float family's returned value is a binary64, and its transfer into a fixed-point resultant identifier is the SAME transfer a MOVE of that value performs** — the receiver's scale, the statement's mode, one rounding (`ReceiverContext.FloatLanding`; the same rule governs native `**`). A nested float operand is not that transfer: it stays the same binary64 value (kb/Work PB653) and, as an OPERAND, it is an **engine-produced approximation, not a floating-point item** (kb/Work PB1641) — §14.9.2.4 GR4 / §14.9.44.4 GR4 (ADD and SUBTRACT; for the other statements and conditions the native lane is this compiler's determination under §8.8.1.3, item 123) require "enough places" except for operands "described with usage binary-char, binary-short, binary-long, binary-double, float-short, float-long, or float-extended", and §14.7.7 r2 lists an intrinsic function, those usages and a floating-point literal as three separate bullets. Beside operands that are NOT floating-point the approximation therefore enters the arithmetic through its shortest-round-trip decimal (the SDIDI, §8.8.1.5.1's conversion — the same VALUE) and the operation runs on the decimal lane, so `13.2 + FUNCTION SQRT(16)` is 17.2 (it stored 17.1 when 13.2 was dragged into binary64 as 13.199999999999999) and `0.1 + FUNCTION SQRT(0.04) = 0.3` is TRUE; beside a floating-point ITEM or into a floating-point receiver the expression stays in binary64 (item 123), exactly as before. The same holds for a native non-integer `**` and NUMVAL-F's receiver-less binary64. Where the ISO text is silent about the intermediate's representation, GnuCOBOL (which evaluates every intrinsic result as a decimal) is the precedent. Pinned by `pb1641_function_result_beside_decimal_operands`. The retired rationale — that rounding at the working scale is the better approximation of the returned VALUE — was true of the value and irrelevant to the transfer; the visible gain is that ROUNDED now does something on this family at all, where `COMPUTE S ROUNDED` and no-phrase `COMPUTE S` used to answer alike. A nested float operand carries no working scale at all, so the expression over a float FUNCTION and the same expression over a COMP-2 holding the identical value agree to the digit (`FUNCTION SQRT(3) * 2` into `PIC 9V9(9)` gives 3.464101615 either way). Pinned by `pb647_float_landing_mode` at 2002/2014/2023 and `FloatLandingModeDriftTests`. Examples: `DISPLAY FUNCTION ORD("A")` → `66` (§15.70.1 — ordinal position, lowest is 1); `DISPLAY FUNCTION MIN(3 -14 0 8 -3)` → `-14`; `MOVE FUNCTION ORD("A") TO PIC X(10)` → `66` left-justified, space-filled (§14.9.25.4 GR6). **⛔ The rule exists to make the compile-time FOLD unobservable.** An intrinsic over constant arguments folds to a numeric literal and a literal renders as its own text (`DISPLAY 42` → `42`); had a computed result been given a padded fixed width instead, `DISPLAY FUNCTION LENGTH(X)` and `DISPLAY FUNCTION ORD(C)` would print in visibly different formats for no reason a COBOL programmer could see. One rule, both paths — pinned by the golden `da2_function_as_text`, whose first four lines are two fold/compute pairs of the same value. **The Table-16 consequence (kb/Work PB73, adjudicated 2026-08-18):** the function's §15.2 TYPE is its §14.9.25.3 Table-16 row as a MOVE sender — an INTEGER function (ISO §15.2 item 5, "no digits to the right of the decimal point": LENGTH, ORD, INTEGER-OF-DATE, ABS/MAX/MIN over integers, …) is the Integer row and moves to an alphanumeric or national receiver in this text form (`MOVE FUNCTION ORD("A") TO PIC X(10)` → `66`); a NUMERIC function (item 4 — SQRT, NUMVAL, RANDOM, MAX over a non-integer, …) is the NONINTEGER row and moves only to a numeric or numeric-edited receiver, whatever a particular reference's value (the principle §8.4.3.2.3 SR11 states for the integer-operand positions) — COBOLNET0819. `--permissive` keeps the pre-adjudication admission with a warning, rendering the same literal text. Pinned by `2023/pb73_table16_function_type_and_boolean_view`, the negatives `pb73-move-numeric-function-to-alphanumeric` / `pb73-move-numval-function-to-national`, and `MoveTable16FunctionTypeTests`. Note this deliberately differs from GnuCOBOL, which zero-pads a computed `ORD` to its field width while printing a folded `MAX` minimally; the standard permits either, and internal consistency was preferred over matching a compiler that is not self-consistent here. **NUMVAL and NUMVAL-C HAVE NO NATIVE WORKING SCALE, BECAUSE §15.4.1'S LATITUDE DOES NOT REACH THEM (kb/Work PB251, rows RV-15.4.1-2 / RV-15.4.1-L2.2).** §15.4.1's closing sentence is a CONDITIONAL — "When a numeric or integer function does not have an equivalent arithmetic expression, its returned value is implementor-defined **unless otherwise specified in the function definition**" — and §15.67.4 r1 / §15.68.4 r1 are exactly such a specification, written once with no arithmetic-mode qualification: "The returned value is the numeric value represented by argument-1". Neither function has an equivalent arithmetic expression, so the latitude is withdrawn and **the native returned value is the parsed value EXACTLY**; only the *characteristics and representation* stay the implementor's. **WiseOwl COBOL's determination: that representation is the SDIDI, in every arithmetic mode** — the one representation that holds every conforming argument (§15.67.3 r3's 31 digits native, r4's 34 standard) with no working scale at all, since its exponent is carried at run time. No compile-time scale can do the job: a scaled `Int128` temporary needs *i* + *ws* digits for an argument with *i* integer digits and r3 permits *i* = 31, so the only *ws* safe for every conforming argument is 7 — while `NUMVAL("0.123456789")` needs 9. The former `max(context scale, 6)` rule made a function's value depend on the SHAPE of its receiver and truncated wherever no receiver scale was inherited: `DISPLAY FUNCTION NUMVAL("0.1234567")` printed 0.123456, and a receiver was no protection either — it bounds the STATEMENT's result, not an OPERAND's precision, so `COMPUTE R = FUNCTION NUMVAL("0.1234567") * 10000000` stored 1234560 into `PIC 9(9)`. Pinned by `pb251_numval_native_value_fixed`, whose native lines are the SAME lines `pb60_numval_standard_decimal` prints for the same references. **NUMVAL-F under native arithmetic is the FLOAT family's case (PB60 / RV-15.69.4-2):** §15.69.4 r2 makes its native returned value an approximation, and the approximation IS a binary64 wherever no fixed-point scale governs the render — a receiver-less context (a DISPLAY/STRING operand, a relation operand, an argument, a subscript) or a float receiver — rendered through the same shortest-round-trip `CobolFloat.Display` a COMP-2 item uses and compared natively (§8.8.4.2.4); a fixed-point ARITHMETIC receiver and a MOVE sender (the receiver's scale is known — `ReceiverContext.MoveSender`) keep the EXACT Int128 parse at the receiver-capped `max(context scale, 9)` working scale, so `MOVE FUNCTION NUMVAL-F("1.5E-8") TO PIC V9(9)` stores 0.000000015 digit-for-digit. Pinned by `pb60_numvalf_native_channels`. **NUMVAL-F's determination is the NATIVE-only one; NUMVAL's and NUMVAL-C's is not.** Under `ARITHMETIC IS STANDARD-DECIMAL` (and 2002's STANDARD) §15.69.4 r3 fixes NUMVAL-F's value too ("the numeric value represented by argument-1"), so its SDIDI temporary carries the parsed value exactly at its own scale in every channel (`NUMVAL-F("1.23456789012345E-3")` renders 0.00123456789012345; a 34-digit significand is exact) — pinned by `pb60_numval_standard_decimal`. NUMVAL and NUMVAL-C behave that way in EVERY mode, which is the whole content of the r2/r3 split: the standard states the standard-decimal value separately only for the function whose native value it left an approximation. **THE RETURNED VALUE'S CAPACITY — WHAT A CLASS CONDITION ASKS OF A FUNCTION-IDENTIFIER (kb/Work PB1401 / PB1466).** §8.5.2.12 items 6–7 make a numeric or integer function a category-numeric data item, so §8.8.4.4.4 GR3 g) / m) ("the numeric value farthest from zero" / "the nonzero numeric value nearest to zero that may be contained in that data item") need the temporary's extremes, and they follow the representation fixed above: a **binary64** result (the float family) has binary64's, asked on its own bits exactly as a COMP-2 item is; an **SDIDI** result (NUMVAL / NUMVAL-C in every mode, every function under a standard arithmetic mode) has the SDIDI's (±9.999…E+6144 at 34 digits, nearest 1E-6176); every other result has the §15.4 temporary's description above — **S9(30)** for an INTEGER function (nearest nonzero value 1, §15.2 item 5) and **S9(21)V9(9)** for a NUMERIC one. `FUNCTION ABS(-1) IS NEAREST-TO-ZERO` is therefore TRUE and `FUNCTION SQRT(4) IS NEAREST-TO-ZERO` FALSE. A function-identifier `IS NUMERIC` is GR3 n) 1. — the function is category numeric (§15.2 items 4–5), so its VALUE is tested, never its text: true for every fixed-point or SDIDI result and for a finite binary64 (`FUNCTION NUMVAL("1.5") IS NUMERIC` and `FUNCTION INTEGER(-5.5) IS NUMERIC` are TRUE; n) 2.'s all-digits test used to answer them FALSE on the text "1.5" / "-6"); `IS IN-ARITHMETIC-RANGE` compares the value's magnitude with the mode's intermediate extremes (GR3 l)). Every one of these tests evaluates the function ONCE (`CobolValueClass`), so RANDOM's seed advances once per written reference. Pinned by `conformance:2014/pb1401_computed_numeric_class` and `conformance:85/pb1401_computed_numeric_numeric`. **THE LENGTH OF A FUNCTION'S RETURNED-VALUE ITEM (kb/Work PB1398).** A function-identifier is a data item (§8.4.3.2.1), so `FUNCTION LENGTH` and `FUNCTION BYTE-LENGTH` (§15.50.3 r1 / §15.14.3 r1, "a data item of any class or category") measure the item its §15.2 type describes. **The determination: an INTEGER or NUMERIC function's returned value is the §15.4 temporary this entry already fixes — S9(30) DISPLAY for an integer function, S9(21)V9(9) DISPLAY for a numeric one — so its LENGTH is 30 alphanumeric positions (§15.50.4 r3) and its BYTE-LENGTH 30 bytes, whatever the arithmetic mode and whatever carrier (binary64, SDIDI, scaled integer) evaluates the value;** the item the value is STORED in and the item its length is ASKED OF are one description (`SendingValueTemp.NumericReturnedValuePic`). GnuCOBOL sizes each function's result field per function and per argument (an unstable contract the standard does not make), so it is not the precedent here. An INDEX function's item is the index carrier, 8 positions and 8 bytes. An ALPHANUMERIC, NATIONAL or BOOLEAN function has a run-time length: LENGTH counts its positions (§15.50.4 r1–r3); BYTE-LENGTH counts 1 byte per alphanumeric position, 2 per national position (D-N1) and — a boolean function having "an implicit usage bit" (§15.2 item 2) — 8 boolean positions to the byte rounded up (§15.14.4 r4: `BYTE-LENGTH(BOOLEAN-OF-INTEGER(5, 16))` is 2, with 9 positions 2, with 8 positions 1). Pinned by `conformance:2002/pb1398_length_of_function_result`, `conformance:2023/pb1398_length_of_index_function_result` and `conformance:negative/pb1398-length-of-arithmetic-expression`. | `conformance:2014/pb1401_computed_numeric_class`; `conformance:2023/da2_function_as_text`; `conformance:2023/pb73_table16_function_type_and_boolean_view`; `conformance:2023/pb60_numval_one_scan`; `conformance:2023/pb251_numval_native_value_fixed`; `conformance:2023/pb2_float_argument_exact_family`; `conformance:85/pb1007_function_temporary_character_receivers`; `conformance:2002/pb1398_length_of_function_result`; `conformance:2023/pb1398_length_of_index_function_result` |
| DOC-A.1-92 | **Evaluation of an exact-family intrinsic whose ARGUMENT is floating-point**, §15.4.1, required — documentation NOT required by A.1 (voluntary) | §15.4.1 makes the characteristics and representation of a returned value "defined by the implementor" under native arithmetic, and each of these functions is defined by an equivalent arithmetic expression over its own operands. **WiseOwl COBOL's determination: when ANY argument renders as floating-point (USAGE COMP-1 / COMP-2 / FLOAT-SHORT / FLOAT-LONG / FLOAT-EXTENDED), the function's equivalent arithmetic expression is evaluated in IEEE binary64** and the result is then delivered to the receiver by the ordinary rules — unquantized into a floating receiver, quantized through `FromDouble` into a fixed-point one — at the resultant identifier's own scale with the statement's ROUNDED mode when the value IS the transfer, at the working scale with truncation when it is a nested operand (kb/Work PB647). With an all-fixed-point argument list the function stays base-10 and takes the exact carrier its arguments need: scaled `Int128` while the aligned arguments and the sum of what the function adds fit it, and — for SUM, MEAN, RANGE, MEDIAN and MIDRANGE only (kb/Work PB621) — the SDIDI, whose 34 significant digits are NEAREST-rounded, once they could leave it. The latter is the "implementor-defined approximation of the value of that expression" §15.4.1 licenses under native arithmetic (cite.py-verified), not an exact value: the receiver's single rounding sees the 34-digit nearest value, so a 35th-or-later digit that decides a carry is already rounded (the double rounding A.1 item 123 states, and which a product avoids only because §14.9.26.4 owns its transfer, not a function's). The determination is forced rather than chosen: a floating-point argument is a legal class-numeric argument (§8.5.2.12 item 2 + §8.5.2.1 Table 2), and once an operand arrives as binary64 there is no exact value left for an exact evaluation to preserve. Sign discipline is the spec's, not the carrier's — MOD floors and REM truncates (§15.64.4 / §15.77.4), INTEGER floors and INTEGER-PART truncates (§15.44 / §15.49) — so `MOD(-7,3)` is 2 and `REM(-7,3)` is -1 in binary64 exactly as in `Int128`. Pinned by the golden `pb2_float_argument_exact_family`. Before this was implemented, such a program did not merely compute differently: it failed to compile, surfacing a raw Roslyn `CS1503` from the generated C#. | `conformance:2023/da2_function_as_text`; `conformance:2023/pb73_table16_function_type_and_boolean_view`; `conformance:2023/pb60_numval_one_scan`; `conformance:2023/pb2_float_argument_exact_family` |
| DOC-A.1-33 | **Computer's coded character set — correspondence between alphanumeric and national characters**, §14.9.25 MOVE GR6 · §8.8.4.2.11 locale-based comparison · §15.26.4 r1/r3 (DISPLAY-OF) · §15.66.4 r1/r3 (NATIONAL-OF), required + documented | Both native repertoires are **UTF-16, one code unit per character position** (item 188's substrate — note 188 itself answers a DIFFERENT question, §12.3.7.4's ALPHABET-phrase correspondence; THIS row is the character correspondence item 33 demands), so the correspondence is the **TOTAL IDENTITY, both directions**: every alphanumeric character's national correspondent is the same code unit, and vice versa. Consequences, each deliberate: **(1)** DISPLAY-OF/NATIONAL-OF **argument-2 is accepted and vacuous** — §15.26.4 r2 / §15.66.4 r2 substitute only for a character "that has no corresponding" representation, and under a total correspondence no such character exists (the §15.26.3 r2 / §15.66.3 r2 class and one-position screens still bind the argument); **(2)** the r3 **EC-DATA-CONVERSION is unreachable by declaration** from any character pathway — a conforming determination (r1 grants the correspondence to the implementor), not an accidental dead branch; **(3)** §14.9.25.4 GR6's alphanumeric→national MOVE conversion is the same identity (the reverse direction is not a MOVE at all — §14.9.25.3 SR10 / Table 16 forbid a national sender into an alphanumeric receiver, COBOLNET0819, and DISPLAY-OF is the sanctioned conversion); **(4)** CONVERT's NAT→ANUM and ANUM→NAT character legs (§15.19.4 r1/r3, whose NOTES name DISPLAY-OF/NATIONAL-OF as "the same facility") likewise never substitute. ⚠ The 8-bit BYTE serialization of usage DISPLAY (CONVERT's ANUM→HEX/BYTE legs) is a **separate** §8.1.2 NOTE 2 determination (item 209's one byte per DISPLAY position), and it is PARTIAL: a code unit above 0xFF has no one-byte image. **Decided disposition (2026-08-09, RV-15.19.4-2): the serialization substitutes `'?'` (0x3F) and sets EC-DATA-CONVERSION** — §15.19.4 r2 carries no substitution sentence because the standard's model assumes a total serialization; where ours is partial, the r1/r3-analogous substitution+EC is the smallest extension of the standard's own machinery, visible under checking and never silent (`CobolIntrinsics.ByteSub`; pinned by `Convert_ByteSerializationSetsDataConversion_WhenChecked_2023`). CONVERT's ANY source reads an item's RAW STORAGE bytes per the documented representations themselves (items 205/208/209/215, D-N1 UTF-16BE for national — `OperandText.AsStorageImage`, the one storage channel). Implemented in `CobolIntrinsics.Repertoire` (the identity, both directions); pinned by the golden `pb59_repertoire_identity` and the re-derived `national_intrinsics`. | `conformance:2002/pb59_repertoire_identity`; `conformance:2002/national_intrinsics` |
| DOC-A.1-93 | **Function returned values — returned value length exceeds implementor-defined limits**, §15.4 Returned values, required + documented (the value returned on excess shall be documented) | The maximum length of a returned value is **8,191 character positions** — the §8.3.3.4.3 SR1 / §8.8.3.2 SR2 literal-and-concatenation maximum, reused so the bound and the language’s own largest string constant are the SAME number (the BOOLEAN-OF-INTEGER row documented this per-function first; it is now the project-wide determination). When a function’s returned value would exceed it, **EC-ARGUMENT-FUNCTION is set to exist** exactly as §15.4’s own sentence prescribes, and **the value returned is the §15.3 checking-off default — a zero-length value** (with checking enabled the raise is fatal and no value is returned). Enforced where a result can actually outgrow it, each as its OWN arm carrying its own citation: `CobolIntrinsics.BaseConvert` (the unbounded base-2 re-expression — a PIC X(2000) ALL "F" base 16→2 input owes 8,000 digits and still converts; 8,192+ raises), `CobolIntrinsics.BooleanOfInteger` (argument-2 > 8,191 — a §15.4 rule, NOT §15.13.3 r2's argument rule, which 8,192 satisfies; conflating the two into one guard was kb/Work PB383). Both return through the one `ExceptionState.ArgumentErrorZeroLength` mechanism, so the two sites cannot answer the same determination differently. §15.12.3 r3’s input-side maximum has no separate A.1 number — it is bounded by this determination plus the 8,191-position item bound, and the row says so rather than inventing one. | `conformance:2023/l1_returned_value_length_limit`; `conformance:2023/l1_returned_value_limit_boolean_repro` |
| DOC-A.1-50 | **DELETE FILE statement — validation of fixed file attributes**, §14.9.10.4 GR19, required + documented | **The validated set is EMPTY.** GR19 requires the implementor to *define* which of the §9.1.6 fixed file attributes DELETE FILE validates; it nowhere requires the set to be non-empty, and an empty definition is as complete a definition as any other. WiseOwl COBOL validates **none** of them — not the organization, prime or alternate record keys, SUPPRESS WHEN attribute, code set, logical or physical record sizes, record type, key collating sequence, or record delimiter — **uniformly across every organization and storage medium** (GR19's second sentence permits the set to vary by organization or medium; ours does not, which is the simpler determination to state and to rely on). **The consequence, stated so nobody re-derives it as a defect:** GR18 sets '39' when the connector's attributes and the physical file's do not match, so with nothing validated there is nothing to mismatch and **'39' is unreachable from DELETE FILE BY DEFINITION, not by omission** — a DELETE FILE through a connector that contradicts the physical file succeeds with GR20 a)'s '00'. This is the determination the standard asks for, and it replaces the deferral ("documented non-support until a physical-attribute store exists") that stood in §3 until 2026-08-31; a deferral is not a definition.
