| DOC-A.1-147 | **RECORD clause — the implicit RECORD clause when no RECORD clause is specified**, §13.18.43.4 GR5, required + documented | **One rule for every file description entry, FD and SD alike** (§3 D-FRA (iv)). **(a) Implied format 2** when any record description of the entry is VARIABLE-LENGTH — a dynamic-length elementary item or a variable-length group (§8.5.1.12.1; `FileModel.IsVariableLengthRecord`): integer-2 is the smallest record description's byte size with every dynamic-length item at zero length, integer-3 the largest with each at its maximum size (GR5 b / GR8 b), and no DEPENDING phrase. **(b) Implied format 1 otherwise**: integer-1 is the byte size of the LARGEST record description entry (GR5 a), an OCCURS DEPENDING ON table counted at its maximum (GR3), so every record of the file is integer-1 bytes — a WRITE or RELEASE of a shorter record description sends the whole integer-1-byte record area (§14.9.51.4 GR13), as does a REWRITE of a relative or indexed file (§14.9.35.4 GR15, GR18), and an ODO record is always sent at its maximum. A REWRITE of a RECORD SEQUENTIAL file cannot: §14.9.35.4 GR16 requires record-name-1's own byte count to EQUAL the record being replaced, so a shorter record description's REWRITE is unsuccessful with '44' and the record is left as it was (kb/Work PB1168; `conformance:85/pb1168_rs_rewrite_size_gr16`). Sizes are in BYTES (GR1): a national character position counts two (D-N1). Example: `FD F.` or `SD S.` with `01 A PIC X(4)` and `01 B PIC X(10)` and no RECORD clause is fixed-length 10: `MOVE ALL "Z" TO B`, `MOVE "AAAA" TO A`, `WRITE A` (or `RELEASE A`) sends `AAAAZZZZZZ`. Same in every edition (a dynamic-length item exists from COBOL-2014, so arm (a) arises only there). An SD takes the same arm as an FD: `RELEASE A` releases the 10-byte record, and `SORT … GIVING` writes `AAAAZZZZZZ` (kb/Work PB322 F; D-WRT1). | `conformance:85/pb322_release_short_record_fixed_sd`; `conformance:85/pb1907_write_past_record`; `conformance:2014/pb981_fd_variable_length_records`; `conformance:2014/pb1276_varying_unstated_max_dynamic`; `conformance:2002/pb1277_record_size_in_bytes` |
| DOC-A.1-156 | **Reference format — the control characters that terminate a free-form line, and whether they may appear in comments and literals**, §6.1 3) b), required + documented | **Exactly one terminator: LINE FEED (U+000A).** A CARRIAGE RETURN (U+000D) immediately before a LINE FEED is part of that terminator and is not a character of the line, so LF and CR LF files read identically. **No other control character terminates a free-form line** — not a lone CR, not FORM FEED, VERTICAL TAB, NEL or any other C0/C1 control — and ONE set serves every stage that asks where a line ends, because ONE stage answers it — the line-entry stage (`PhysicalLines`, kb/Work PB1800), which every later stage reads: the reference-format and compiler-directive stage (where `>>` must begin a line), the floating-comment indicator `*>` (whose comment runs to the end of the line), and literal scanning; the lexer's line end is the same LINE FEED, so no stage ends a line at a lone CR. The end of the source text also ends its last line. **In comments and literals:** the terminator cannot appear in a comment or in the content of an alphanumeric or national literal — it ends the line (a literal that must span lines ends the first line with the floating literal continuation indicator, §6.2.3.1) — while every other control character, a lone CR included, MAY appear in comment-text and in literal content and is taken as that character: `"A<FF>B"` and `"A<CR>B"` each hold three characters, and a lone CR in a `*>` comment does not end it. The one exception is the horizontal tab, which is never a character of a comment or a literal because it is expanded to spaces at line entry (item 157; owner decision kb/Work R55). Free-form reference format exists from COBOL-2002 (`>>SOURCE FORMAT FREE`, and the auto-detection of item 158). This is GnuCOBOL's line model (rule 1 precedence; the standard leaves the set to the implementor). | `conformance:2023/pb1496_lone_cr_is_a_character`; `unit:PhysicalLinesTests.ALoneCrIsACharacter`; `unit:PhysicalLinesTests.ALoneCr_DoesNotStartADirectiveLine` |
| DOC-A.1-157 | **Reference format — the meaning of lines and character positions, free-form and fixed-form**, §6.1 1) c), required + documented | **A character position is ONE CHARACTER of the decoded source text — one Unicode code point** (each file decoded by its byte-order mark, UTF-8 without one, ISO/IEC 8859-1 when it is not well-formed UTF-8 — item 25, item 26): a character of the Basic Multilingual Plane is one position and so is a supplementary-plane character such as an Annex B extended letter of the Adlam or Linear B script, which is two UTF-16 code units of the in-memory string (a surrogate that is not half of a valid pair is a character of its own). The unit is the character because §6.1 1) a) describes reference format "in terms of character positions" and the standard counts the length of a word in characters, the base character and each combining character apart (§8.1.3.2 GR4 c)); a count of UTF-16 code units put margin R, the indicator area, every tab stop and the free-form 255 limit early by one position per supplementary character, so legal source whose text ends at position 72 was cut short (kb/Work PB1966; this row once read "a character outside the Basic Multilingual Plane is two"). One definition serves every site — `CharacterPositions` (count, index of a position, position of an index) and `FixedFormLine` (the sequence area, the indicator, the program-text area and margin R of a fixed-form line) — and `PhysicalLinesDriftTests.NoPipelineStage_ReadsAColumnAsAStringIndex` refuses a column read as a string index; a diagnostic's column is a character position too. The exception is a horizontal tab (U+0009), which advances to the next tab stop — positions 1, 9, 17, 25, … (a tab width of 8, GnuCOBOL's default `tab-width`; rule 1 precedence) — so it occupies one to eight positions. **A tab is expanded to those spaces EVERYWHERE, at line entry and before anything else reads the line** (the `PhysicalLines` stage, kb/Work PB1800) — in the sequence and indicator areas, in the program-text area, in a free-form line and inside an alphanumeric or national literal alike (owner decision kb/Work R55: the tab is an input-medium positioning control, not a character of the literal; `X"09"` is how a program puts a TAB character in data). So `<TAB><TAB>DISPLAY` in fixed form starts DISPLAY at position 17 with a space indicator; `000100<TAB>` fills positions 7–8, leaving the indicator area a space; and text a tab carries past margin R is truncated like any text past it. No tab reaches a later stage. **A line is the text of a source or library file between two line terminators** (item 156: LF, or CR LF), the terminator not included; the file's end also ends its last line. There is no record or block structure below the file. **Free-form:** a line has 0 to **255** character positions (§6.1 3) a)), counted on the expanded line; a longer line is diagnosed (COBOLNET2653, an error at every edition, asked wherever a line is read as free form — a `>>SOURCE FORMAT FREE` segment of a fixed-form file too), and program text may begin at any position. A fixed-form line has no such limit: §6.1 2) b) lets a logically converted line be longer, and only its program-text area counts. **Fixed-form:** positions 1–6 are the sequence number area, position 7 the indicator area and positions 8–72 the program-text area; margin R follows position 72 and anything beyond it is ignored (item 158). **A line shorter than 72 positions is read as if space-filled to margin R**, so its program-text area is always the 65 positions 8–72 — which is what an alphanumeric or boolean literal continued by the fixed continuation indicator carries to its continuation (§6.3.5: “any spaces at the end of the fixed-form continued line are part of the literal”), e.g. `000100 01 X PIC X(80) VALUE "AB` ending at position 29, continued by `000100-    "CD".`, gives `AB` + 43 spaces + `CD`. Fixed-form is available at every edition, free-form from COBOL-2002. This follows GnuCOBOL and IBM Enterprise COBOL, which both pad a short record to the margin (rule 1 precedence). The padding is applied by the ONE §6.5 logical conversion (`ReferenceFormatProcessor`), which also joins a continuation line to the latest LOGICAL line, so comment lines and blank lines interspersed among the parts of a literal leave it intact (kb/Work PB1491; golden `85/pb1491_continued_literal_logical_line`). | `conformance:85/pb1586_tab_stops_fixed_form`; `conformance:2023/pb1586_tab_in_literal`; `conformance:2023/pb1586_tab_free_form`; `conformance:2023/pb1496_free_form_line_255`; `conformance:negative/pb1496-free-form-line-256`; `conformance:2023/pb1966_supplementary_letters_margin_r`; `conformance:negative/pb1966-supplementary-letters-past-margin-r`; `unit:CharacterPositionsTests`; `unit:PhysicalLinesTests.ATabAdvancesToTheNextTabStop`; `unit:PhysicalLinesTests.FreeForm_LineOfMoreThan255Positions_IsDiagnosed` |
| DOC-A.1-159 | **Report file — the report writer logical record structure**, §13.4.5 FD entry, GR4 (§13.4.5.4), required + documented | **One print line is one record: the line's character positions written as plain text, one byte per column in ISO/IEC 8859-1, with no length prefix and no file header whatever the file's ORGANIZATION.** Each line is followed by CR LF (0x0D 0x0A, on every host), except that when the next line begins a new page the line is followed by a form feed (0x0C) instead. A report record is the CODE characters, when the RD has a CODE clause (§13.18.12.4 GR2: they "are included in the logical record size"), followed by the line, and the record is the size the FD's RECORD clause gives it: integer-1 bytes in Format 1, at most integer-3 in Format 2 or integer-5 in Format 3 (§13.18.43.4 GR6, GR7, GR18; kb/Work PB1934, PB2519). A column of the line past the record's size less the CODE is NOT recorded: the standard states no outcome for the cut (§13.4.5.4 GR4 leaves the structure to the implementor; this item is where it is documented), and the other choices contradict GR6, GnuCOBOL widening the record for a file without CODE and refusing the write of one with it. The cut is not a condition of its own and the exception conditions are untouched by it: the column occupancy EC-REPORT-COLUMN-OVERLAP (§13.18.14.4 GR4) reads reaches as far as the page width (§13.18.39.4 GR2 b)), so two items that overlap past the record are still reported. With no RECORD clause, or a Format 2 clause without its TO phrase, a line holds exactly the columns its report group description places (the widest item), national printable items included at one column per character position (item 160). The report file has no CODE-SET clause, so it uses the file coded character set of item 31 (owner decision kb/Work R47): a character U+0000–U+00FF is written as the byte of the same value, and a print line holding a character above U+00FF is NOT written — the write the report writer performs is unsuccessful with I-O status `'91'` in the report file's connector (`'71'` if the file is ORGANIZATION LINE SEQUENTIAL; items 31, 110, 115), never a `?`. GENERATE is not among the statements §9.1.13.1 lists as setting an I-O status, so the FILE STATUS item is not stored by it; the refused line leaves a BLANK line in the report file, because §14.9.51.4 GR15's unsuccessful write "does not take place" (its advance included) and the next line's advance then covers the refused line's slot — every later line of the page still lands on its own LINE-COUNTER line (`ReportWriter` moves its device line only when the write succeeded). Every edition. | `conformance:85/pb690_report_line_byte_image_85` |
| DOC-A.1-161 | **REPOSITORY paragraph — how the external repository and the class-specifier determine which class is used**, §12.3.8.4 GR6, required + documented | **The §8.13 external repository is the metadata of compiled WiseOwl COBOL assemblies** (owner decision kb/Work R44, 2026-09-24): a compiled class assembly carries the class's §8.13 information (externalized name, kind, methods, parameters, returning items, raised exceptions, entry convention, properties, type declarations, DECIMAL-POINT IS COMMA, currency symbols, locale identification), in a WiseOwl COBOL-owned metadata record wherever .NET metadata cannot express it natively; a compiler option names the repository assemblies a compilation references; a class-specifier resolves against the compilation group's definitions and that referenced repository, and a specifier neither answers is diagnosed (§12.3.8.3 SR6); two further options choose whether a compilation updates the repository (records the classes it defines) and whether differences from the repository are flagged (§8.13). **Within the compilation group** the specifier names the definition item 163 describes — by its declared word, or by its externalized name when `AS literal-1` is written — and otherwise the standard class BASE (§16.1), a group definition taking precedence. The precedence between the group and the referenced repository, how externalized names are compared across assemblies, and a name more than one referenced assembly answers are settled by the R44 design (kb/Work PB1086) and written here when it lands. ⚠ **Only the compilation group and BASE are searched today** (kb/Work PB1086): `OoNameResolution.Lookup` searches the per-group `OoClassTable` alone, the compiler offers none of the three options, a specifier no source answers compiles clean, and a class compiled separately cannot be named (COBOLNET0823). | `conformance:2002/pb974_repository_as_phrase` |
| DOC-A.1-162 | **REPOSITORY paragraph, INTERFACE phrase — how the interface-specifier and the external repository determine which interface is used**, §12.3.8.4 GR9, required + documented | **The item 161 determination, for interfaces** (owner decision kb/Work R44, 2026-09-24): the §8.13 external repository is the metadata of compiled WiseOwl COBOL assemblies, an interface assembly carries the interface's §8.13 information, an interface-specifier resolves against the compilation group's definitions and the referenced repository, and a specifier neither answers is diagnosed (§12.3.8.3 SR9); the reference, update and flag options are item 161's. **Within the compilation group** the specifier names the interface item 163 describes — by its declared word, or by its externalized name (its INTERFACE-ID `AS literal-1`, §11.6.4) when `AS literal-2` is written. §16 defines no standard interface, so there is no BASE step. The remaining resolution details are the R44 design's (kb/Work PB1086), as for item 161. ⚠ **Only the compilation group is searched today** (kb/Work PB1086): an interface compiled separately cannot be named, a specifier no source answers compiles clean, and an interface's literal-1 is only an in-group key — the emitted CLR interface carries the declared word. | `conformance:2002/pb974_repository_as_phrase` |
| DOC-A.1-164 | **RESERVE clause — the number of input-output areas when the clause is not specified**, §12.4.5.14.3 GR1, required + documented | **One input-output area per file connector, separate from its record area** (§12.4.6.4.4 GR1, *"The area being shared includes all storage areas assigned to the files"*, distinguishes the storage areas assigned to a file from the area for the current logical record). **An input-output area is 4,096 bytes** (`HostFile.InputOutputAreaBytes`) of the ONE buffer a connector keeps between its record area and the medium: a sequential connector's reader or writer (the host handle beneath it holds none, kb/Work PB753), a relative or indexed connector's store handle (through which its whole store is loaded at OPEN and persisted at CLOSE). **With the clause, the connector allocates exactly integer-1 areas** (GR1's first sentence; `FileConnector.InputOutputAreas`, sized once by `HostFile.InputOutputAreaBuffer`, kb/Work PB643), at every edition and for every organization, report files included; the areas are allocated by the OPEN, and a run unit that cannot supply the memory gets '30' on that OPEN (§9.1.13.6 1)). **Limit (§4.2.15):** integer-1 at most 524,287, the largest count whose areas one buffer can hold; a larger one is refused at compile time (COBOLNET2897), never silently reduced. **When the areas are emptied** depends on the connector's sharing mode: a connector holding the only writable handle releases its records to the medium as its areas fill, and at CLOSE; one whose sharing mode admits another writer (`FileLockPosture.AdmitsAnotherWriter`) releases each record during its own WRITE or REWRITE (§14.9.51.4 GR12), and discards what it has read ahead when a sibling releases one. The difference is visible to a program: `OPEN EXTEND` under `SHARING WITH READ ONLY`, 100 WRITEs of 64-byte records, then a second connector's `OPEN INPUT` and READs reach 64 records with one area (the first 4,096 bytes), none with `RESERVE 2 AREAS`, and all 100 after the writer's CLOSE or under `SHARING WITH ALL OTHER`. Same in every edition. | `conformance:2002/pb643_reserve_areas_visibility`; `conformance:85/pb643_reserve_areas_all_organizations` |
| DOC-A.1-167 | **Run unit — the relationship and interaction between COBOL and non-COBOL components**, §14.6.1, conditionally required (condition met: a .NET host can activate WiseOwl COBOL programs, items 65, 141) + documented | **The only non-COBOL component a run unit can contain is .NET code that ACTIVATES WiseOwl COBOL programs** (a "host"); COBOL never activates non-COBOL code — a CALL cannot reach it (item 16) and CANCEL does not touch it (item 19). **Beginning:** an executable built by `cobol` begins its run unit in its generated `Main` and contains no host. A host begins a run unit with `RunUnit.Run(body)`, which gives it a FRESH run unit — program table, EXTERNAL items, files, switches, locale (read from the process once, at that moment — item 120), FUNCTION RANDOM sequence, report state and exception status — then calls each compiled module's `__CobolModule.Register()` and activates programs by name (`ProgramRegistry.CallProgram`, item 65) or through `ICobolProgram.Call` (item 141). An activation outside `RunUnit.Run` joins the ambient run unit of the host's execution context (`RunUnit.Current`, one per async flow). **Storage:** the host and COBOL share only what an activation passes — the USING arguments and the RETURNING item (item 141); COBOL storage is otherwise invisible to the host, and each run unit's EXTERNAL items are its own. **Ending normally:** GOBACK or EXIT PROGRAM returns to the host at its call site (item 65); the run unit ends when `RunUnit.Run`'s body returns, or at process exit for the ambient run unit, and either way the §14.6.11 actions run (every open file of the run unit closed). **STOP RUN, and a fatal exception condition that terminates the run unit (§14.6.12), end the RUN UNIT ONLY — never the host process** (owner decision kb/Work R48, deliberately unlike GnuCOBOL's process exit): WiseOwl COBOL performs the §14.6.11 actions (every file of the run unit closed), resets the run unit's state (switches, SET LOCALE — item 168 —, report and exception state), and returns control to the host as ONE documented, catchable .NET exception type carrying the exit status (the item-192 STOP RUN status, or the abnormal-termination status) and whether termination was normal or abnormal; no other exception type escapes to the host for a run-unit ending, and `Environment.Exit` is never called. The host process continues and may begin another run unit, which inherits nothing. A standalone `cobol` executable IS its own host: its `Main` catches that ending and exits the process with the status. Every edition. ⚠ Diverges today: STOP RUN throws the internal `StopRun` signal into the host (out of `RunUnit.Run` too); a fatal EC reaches the abnormal-termination surface only on the `RunMain` path; and files are not closed for the ambient run unit (kb/Work PB1149, PB1253). A run unit's own state never carries over: the generated `Main`'s first statement begins a NEW run unit object (`RunUnit.Begin`), exactly as `RunUnit.Run` does, so switches, locale, report and exception state, FUNCTION RANDOM, and every class's factory object (and all factory data, §9.3.14.2) start fresh; only the host's clock seam and object-time debug switch carry over (`RunUnit.HostConfiguration`; witness `conformance-test:RunUnitScopeHostTests`, `unit:RunUnitStateDriftTests.EveryMember_IsFreshInTheNextRunUnit`). | — |
| DOC-A.1-168 | **Run unit termination — whether the locale is reset to the locale in effect when the run unit was activated**, §14.6.11 GR5, required + documented | **Yes — always, because a run unit's locale never leaves the run unit.** A run unit reads the process culture ONCE, when it begins, for its user and system default locales (items 118, 120); SET LOCALE then changes only that run unit's own per-category state (`RunUnit.Locale`, a `LocaleState`) and never the process's `CultureInfo`, its `LANG`/`LC_*` environment or any other run unit. So when the run unit ends, the locale in effect for the host and the process is exactly the one in effect when the run unit was activated, and the run unit's own locale state is discarded with it: the next run unit a host begins in the same process starts again from the process culture, not from any SET LOCALE the previous run unit executed (item 167). COBOL-2002 onward (the locale facility and SET LOCALE). Both ways a run unit begins give it a fresh `LocaleState`: the generated `Main`'s `RunUnit.Begin` and a host's `RunUnit.Run` each construct a NEW run unit object (kb/Work PB1069; witness `unit:RunUnitStateDriftTests.EveryMember_IsFreshInTheNextRunUnit`, which reflects over every run-unit member). | `unit:RunUnitStateDriftTests.EveryMember_IsFreshInTheNextRunUnit`; `conformance-test:RunUnitScopeHostTests.ASecondRunUnitInOneProcess_InheritsNoSwitchAndNoFactoryData` |
| DOC-A.1-192 | **STOP and GOBACK statements — constraints on the value of the STATUS literal or on the contents of the data item referenced by the STATUS identifier**, §14.9.42.4 GR5 · §14.9.18.4 GR10, required + documented | **No value is refused; the STATUS operand becomes the process exit code** (`Environment.ExitCode`, the one observable .NET gives both the value GR5 passes and the ERROR/NORMAL indication of GR2/GR3). The syntax rules still apply (§14.9.42.3 SR2–SR4: an integer data item or a display/national item; a numeric literal-1 an integer; no zero-length literal; `STATUS NULL` is COBOLNET1704). The mapping, shared by STOP and a main program's GOBACK (item 94): **(1)** a numeric operand's integer value (a data item's value truncated toward zero) is the exit code; **(2)** a non-numeric operand — an alphanumeric or national item, a non-numeric, national, hexadecimal or boolean literal, a figurative constant, `ALL literal` — is decoded as an unsigned integer from its digit characters, a non-digit position adding no digit (`CobolNum.FromAlphanumeric`), so `STATUS "007"` exits 7 and `STATUS "ABEND"` or `STATUS SPACE` exits 0; **(3)** the host keeps what it can report: the value is CLAMPED (never wrapped) to the 32-bit range, so `STATUS 4294967296` exits 2,147,483,647, and a POSIX host keeps only the low 8 bits, so `STATUS 300` exits 44 and `STATUS -3` exits 253. **A STATUS value, when present, wins over ERROR or NORMAL** — `STOP RUN WITH ERROR STATUS 0` exits 0 and `WITH NORMAL STATUS 7` exits 7; with no STATUS value `WITH ERROR` exits 1 and `WITH NORMAL` (or no phrase) exits 0. That precedence is GR5's implementor latitude settled by CLAUDE.md rule 1's order (the standard states none; GnuCOBOL 3.2 hands the one integer to `exit(status)` and substitutes 1/0 only when the operand is absent — surveyed kb/Work PB169; confirmed under the standing owner-decision protocol on kb/Work PB1178). The phrase is COBOL-2002+ on STOP and COBOL-2023 on GOBACK (COBOLNET0900 below). Full statement: §3 'STOP RUN / GOBACK termination status'. Implemented by `RunUnit.ExitStatus` and `NumericRenderer.ExitStatus`. | `conformance-test:StopGobackExitCodeTests.TerminationStatus_SetsProcessExitCode`; `conformance-test:StopGobackExitCodeTests.NonNumericStatusOperand_IsLegalAndInterpretedNumerically`; `conformance-test:StopGobackExitCodeTests.StatusBeyondTheHostExitCode_IsClampedNeverWrapped`; `conformance-test:StopGobackExitCodeTests.FigurativeAllAndBooleanStatusLiterals_RenderUnderTheGR5Mapping` |
| DOC-A.1-217 | **USAGE PROGRAM-POINTER clause — alignment, size, and representation of data; and allowable languages**, §13.18.60.4 GR24, required + documented | GR24 leaves four things to the implementor and this determination answers all four. **ALIGNMENT: none** (the item 205 rule). **SIZE: 8 character positions** — `FUNCTION BYTE-LENGTH` of the item is 8, the width shared with POINTER / FUNCTION-POINTER / OBJECT REFERENCE / INDEX (`PointerImage.Width`), and a strongly-typed group (§13.18.60.3 SR14) counts those 8 positions in its size. **REPRESENTATION:** the VALUE is the addressed program's externalized identity — the name of the OUTERMOST program identified by the externalized program-name in its PROGRAM-ID paragraph (§8.4.3.13.4 GR2) — held in the readonly record struct `ProgramPointer(string? Name)` (`src/Cobol.Net.Runtime/Control/ProgramPointer.cs`) and resolved and activated through the ONE run-unit `ProgramTable`, exactly as a CALL by name is; the predefined address NULL is the null name (§8.4.3.10.4 GR3, the NULL program address), and two program-pointers are equal when they name the same program, compared case-insensitively, or are both NULL. What its 8 STORAGE positions hold depends on who reads the bytes, each answer already pinned: as a BY CONTENT argument's record, the address token `PointerImage` defines (item 216); inside a strongly-typed group's character image, the D-SLOT placeholder — eight SPACES, whatever the value (item 56). **ALLOWABLE LANGUAGES: COBOL only.** GR24's second paragraph admits “another language for which the implementor has declared support for access by program pointers”, and this implementation declares none: a non-COBOL program has no run-unit registration, so `SET … TO ADDRESS OF PROGRAM` naming one takes §8.4.3.13.4 GR4 — EC-PROGRAM-NOT-FOUND and the predefined address NULL — never an undefined outcome. | `conformance:2023/pb61_length_byte_length_rule_branches`; `conformance:2002/program_pointer`; `conformance:2002/pb460_set_program_pointer_effect`; `conformance:2002/l1c23_program_address_outermost`; `conformance:2002/pb970_pointer_by_content_storage_image`; `conformance:2002/pb244_pointer_group_transfer`; `conformance:2002/w59h_non_cobol_names_not_found` |
| DOC-A.1-218 | **USE statement — action taken after the USE procedure when the I-O status value indicates a fatal exception condition**, §14.9.49.4 GR7 c) and GR12 c) → §9.1.13.1, required + documented | **WiseOwl COBOL CONTINUES the run unit.** §9.1.13.1 offers two actions (“The implementor may either continue or terminate the execution of the run unit”) and the standard does not choose; CLAUDE.md rule 1 takes GnuCOBOL's, which continues after an applicable error declarative. When the USE procedure for a fatal I-O status (first digit 3, 4 or 7, or an implementor-defined 9x value — '90' and '91', fatal per item 110) returns, control is transferred to the end of the input-output statement that produced the status, unless that statement's rules define other behaviour (§9.1.13.1) — a MERGE or SORT follows its own rules (GR7 a) / GR12 a); the §3 SORT/MERGE implicit-transfer determination) — and any NOT AT END or NOT INVALID KEY phrase of that statement is ignored. The answer is the same at every edition and whether or not a FILE STATUS clause is specified. **WiseOwl COBOL does NOT “specify otherwise” under §14.6.13.1.3 rule 3**, so when checking for the EC-I-O exception condition IS enabled (`>>TURN EC-I-O CHECKING ON`, 2002+) the fatal-exception rules that follow rule 3 apply after the §9.1.13 processing: once the declarative completes normally, the run unit is terminated abnormally (rule 5 for a format 3 USE that names the condition, rule 7 after a format 1 USE procedure), and a `RESUME` executed in the declarative continues instead (NOTE 1 / NOTE 2). The checked and unchecked outcomes are therefore the standard's own split on the checking state, both read from this one determination: the unchecked program's plain `__IoCheck` (`DispatchEmitter.EmitUseMachinery`) has no fatal arm of its own (a fatal status it did not handle ends the run unit only when the file also has no FILE STATUS clause, item 103), and the checked program's `EcEmitter.EmitIoCheckEc` ends in the §14.6.13.1.3 termination. | `conformance:2002/pb344_use_mode_read_exception`; `conformance:2023/pb344_use_mode_read_exception`; `conformance:2023/pb141_resume_io`; `conformance-test:DocA1Item218WitnessTests.CheckedFatalIo_Format1UseCompletesNormally_TerminatesAbnormally`; `conformance-test:DocA1Item218WitnessTests.CheckedFatalIo_Format3UseCompletesNormally_TerminatesAbnormally`; `conformance:85/pb344_use_mode_read_exception`; `conformance:2014/pb344_use_mode_read_exception` |
| DOC-A.1-219 | **User-defined words — whether extended letters may be specified in user-defined words externalized to the operating environment**, §8.3.2.2, required + documented | **Yes.** From COBOL 2002 on, an extended letter (§8.1.3.2 GR4: the Annex B repertoire, less the basic letters) may appear in ANY user-defined word, and an externalized one is no exception — a program-name, class-name, interface-name, method-name, property-name, function-name, prototype-name, or the name of an EXTERNAL data item or file. The externalized name keeps the extended letter as written, in the UTF-16 repertoire (item 213's national representation), and is matched at run time exactly as a basic-letter name is: case-insensitively, by the §8.1.3.2 GR4 b) uppercase/lowercase correspondence. The standard leaves the answer to the implementor, and CLAUDE.md rule 1's precedence takes GnuCOBOL's, which accepts extended characters in user-defined words, program-names included; the §5 A.4.6 Extended letters claim requires them in the words themselves. At COBOL-85 there are no extended letters, so such a character is a lexical error there. Implemented by kb/Work PB1402: the lexer's word class admits every extended letter, the edition's Annex B is asked of each word (COBOLNET2773), and the run-time match is the `CobolNames` fold, so `CALL "prüfung"` reaches `PROGRAM-ID. PRÜFUNG` (golden `2002/pb1402_extended_letters`). | `conformance:2002/pb1402_extended_letters` |

## 8. Rules with no observable obligation — owner-signed derivations (§4.4 / Annex A.2)

> **⛔ THIS SECTION IS THE ONE ALTERNATIVE TO A SPEC-DERIVED TEST, AND THE OWNER OPENED IT** — decision
> `kb/Work/PB386`, 2026-09-03, implementing `docs/rearchitecture/DESIGN-spec-conformance-review.md` **§1.1**.
> Some normative rules impose no obligation a conforming program can observe, and for those §1(c)'s covering
> test is not merely unwritten, it is **unmeetable**: any golden would pin an IMPLEMENTATION CHOICE as though it
> were the standard's answer, which is the exact thing the traceability inventory's `spec-derived` clause exists
> to prevent. A determination here stands in place of that test — **and in place of nothing else**. The verdict,
> its evidence, and (for an Annex A.1 row) the §7 determination and a greenfield implementing site are all still
> paid in full.
>
> ⚙ **The row key is `DRV-` plus the inventory `rule-id`, and the inventory row's `derivation` field carries
> `docs/CONFORMANCE.md#DRV-<rule-id>` COMPUTED from that same id** — never typed, for the reason §7's key is
> computed: a determination filed against a neighbouring rule cannot be *spelled*. Every cell below is machine
> read: `scripts/spec/audit_derivations.py` and `SpecTraceabilityInventoryDriftTests` both evaluate the arm, and
> `Schema.state_for` refuses to close a row whose determination does not stand.
>
> ⚙ **Four arms, and only one of them is MECHANICAL.**
> · **`undefined-A.2`** is MECHANICAL. Annex A.2 is *"a list of the COBOL language elements within this Working
> Draft International Standard that are explicitly undefined"* (§A.2), and §4.4 2) makes the point that matters
> here: *"A COBOL run unit that allows these situations to happen is a conforming run unit, although the
> resultant execution is not defined by standard COBOL."* Each A.2 item ends in the standard's own citation of
> the rule it is about, so `scripts/spec/extract_annex_a2.py` resolves item → `rule-id` and the checker requires
> the named item to cover **this** row. Naming a real item about a different rule is refused.
> · **`unpopulatable-antecedent`** and **`indistinguishable-consequent`** are reviewed ARGUMENTS. Their shape is
> checked — the closed set must be STATED, the named rule must EXIST and not be this one — and the `Signed`
> cell is the record that the argument was read. An unchecked argument would make the GAP metric cheaper to move
> than the work it stands for, which is the failure this whole register is built against.
> · **`definitional`** (owner decision `kb/Work/R43` item 4, 2026-09-24, extending `PB386` — answers `PB468`
> Q9) is a reviewed argument too: the rule DEFINES a property of the USER'S program and imposes no obligation on
> the implementation, so there is nothing an implementation can do that a program could observe as meeting or
> failing it. The `Names` cell must open `defines` and state the property and whose it is; a rule that says what
> an implementation or implementor SHALL do is never this arm.
>
> ⚖ **This does not reverse `kb/Work/PB280` Q2.** An Annex A.1 row asks the implementor to STATE a choice, so
> "there is nothing to observe" is unfalsifiable there and still cannot close a row: the A.1 row below is
> admitted because its determination **is** stated, in §7, and it names implementing code — only its WITNESS is
> impossible. `Schema.state_for` encodes that by ORDER, charging a DOC row's anchor and observability costs
> before it ever consults this register.

| Rule | Arm | Names | The derivation | Signed |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| DRV-GR-14.9.5.4-11 | undefined-A.2 | A.2 item 4 | **§14.9.5.4 GR11** — *"If a program-pointer has been set to point to the program to be canceled, the result of referencing the program-pointer in a subsequent CALL statement is undefined."* (`cite.py --check 14.9.5.4` → OK, rule 11). **Annex A.2 item 4 repeats it verbatim**, and §4.4 2) makes a run unit that reaches the situation a conforming one, so every behaviour conforms and there is no expected value to compute. WiseOwl COBOL's behaviour is nevertheless determinate: `ProgramTable.CancelNode` assigns `Node.Instance = null` and nothing structural, leaving the entry in `_byPath` and `_order`, so `CallPointer` re-resolves the canonical name the pointer holds and re-enters `CallProgram`, which re-instantiates the program in its initial state. That is one admissible realization of "undefined" and is stricter than the rule requires — specifically, not a dangling reference. | owner: 2026-09-03 |
| DRV-GR-14.9.30.4-3 | undefined-A.2 | A.2 item 41 | **§14.9.30.4 GR3** — *"the content of any data item, where any part of that data item lies beyond the range of the current record, is undefined"* (`cite.py --check 14.9.30.4` → OK, rule 3). **Annex A.2 item 41** carries it, and unlike Annex A.1 the A.2 list creates no documentation obligation, so there is not even a documented choice to assert. WiseOwl COBOL makes the content determinate: `FileConnector.Fit` pads or truncates the physical record to the record-area width with SPACES and `SequentialIoEmitter.EmitImageInto` stores that full-width image into the largest record description (§13.18.33.4 GR3), so every position past the current record reads as a space. | owner: 2026-09-03 |
| DRV-GR-14.9.34.4-2 | undefined-A.2 | A.2 item 46 | **§14.9.34.4 GR2** — *"the content of any data item, where any part of that data item lies outside the range of the current record, is undefined"* (`cite.py --check 14.9.34.4` → OK, rule 2). **Annex A.2 item 46** carries it. This is the RETURN twin of GR-14.9.30.4-3 and it is doubly unreachable: `SortBinder.BindReturn` resolves the record area through `file.AreaRecord` — the largest record's view — and `SortEmitter.EmitReturn` writes through `CobolString.Store`, which space-fills to the image width, so the bytes outside the returned record are deterministically SPACES. | owner: 2026-09-03 |
| DRV-GR-14.9.11.4-11 | unpopulatable-antecedent | the DISPLAY device set is closed at two members — System.Console and System.Console.Error, admitted by the output-capable device rows of ImplementorNames (read by AcceptDisplayBinder.MnemonicDevice) and emitted by a two-valued ternary in AcceptDisplayEmitter.EmitDisplay | **§14.9.11.4 GR11** — *"If vertical positioning is not applicable on the device, the vertical positioning shall be ignored."* (`cite.py --check 14.9.11.4` → OK, rule 11). The antecedent is FALSE for every device this implementation offers. `BindDisplayUpon` admits only the output-capable device-names of `ImplementorNames` and diagnoses anything else (COBOLNET0817); the emitter's sink is a two-valued ternary over the §14.9.11.4 GR8 standard display device (*"If the UPON phrase is not specified, the implementor's standard display device is used."*) and the SYSERR routing recorded at §7 item 59. Both are line-structured streams on which vertical positioning IS applicable, so there is never any positioning to ignore. ⚠ The vacuity is CONTINGENT: it is voided the moment `ImplementorNames` grows an output-capable device row — a printer or reel mnemonic — and this row must then be re-adjudicated rather than inherit its closure. | owner: 2026-09-03 |
| DRV-GR-14.9.30.4-20 | unpopulatable-antecedent | the physical-file set is closed at one host path per connector — FileConnector.HostPath resolves exactly one, on a mass-storage medium with no reel or unit model, and FileRegistry.CloseReelUnit is the matching no-op | **§14.9.30.4 GR20** — *"shall be a single reel/unit mass storage file"* (`cite.py --check 14.9.30.4` → OK, rule 20). This is a requirement on the SOURCE PROGRAM, and in this implementation every physical file a program can name already satisfies it: a connector binds exactly one resolved host path, there is no reel/unit or volume model, and `CLOSE … REEL/UNIT` is a successful no-op reporting `07` (§9.1.13.2 item 6). No program can name a multi-reel physical file, so there is no violating state to detect and neither a positive nor a negative witness is constructible. | owner: 2026-09-03 |
| DRV-GR-14.9.6.4-L2.1 | unpopulatable-antecedent | the physical-file category set is closed at the two `PhysicalFileCategory` values any connector can report — `SequentialConnector` answers (a) Non-unit and `RelativeConnector`/`IndexedConnector` answer (d), so no file is on unit media | **§14.9.6.4 GR3 symbol e, branch “Input files and input-output files (unit media)” item 1** — *“If the current unit is the last or only unit for the physical file, there is no unit swap, the current volume pointer remains unchanged, and the file position indicator is set to indicate that no next or previous logical record exists.”* (`cite.py --check 14.9.6.4` → OK, rule 3). Its antecedent needs a physical file **on unit media**, i.e. §14.9.6.4 GR2 category (b) or (c). No supported medium is in either: the placement is stated in §7's A.1 item 24 row, realized as `FileConnector.Category`, and FORCED by §9.1.13.2 item 6, which defines the `'07'` this compiler already reports for the REEL/UNIT and NO REWIND phrases as the status of a CLOSE that *“references a physical file on a non-reel/unit medium”* (`cite.py --check 9.1.13.2` → OK, rule 6). Every `CLOSE … UNIT` therefore takes symbol e's THIRD branch (non-unit media), which `GR-14.9.6.4-L2.2` covers, and no run unit can reach this one — there is neither a positive nor a negative witness to construct. ⚠ The vacuity is CONTINGENT on the placement: `CloseTable14Tests.Category_PlacesEverySupportedConnectorKind` and `ReachableCategories_NeedNoUnitStructuredSymbol` fail the moment a connector answers (b) or (c), and this row must then be re-adjudicated rather than inherit its closure. | owner: 2026-09-03 |
| DRV-RV-15.50.4-9 | indistinguishable-consequent | RV-15.50.4-5 | **§15.50.4 rule 9** — *"the returned value is rounded to the next larger integer value"* (`cite.py --check 15.50.4` → OK, rule 9) — cannot be told apart from **§15.50.4 rule 5**, *"The returned length shall include the number of implicit FILLER positions, if any, in argument-1."* (`cite.py --check 15.50.4` → OK, rule 5). §8.5.1.6.3 generates implicit filler bit positions to complete the last byte of a bit-bearing item (*"Implicit filler bit positions are generated"*, `cite.py --check 8.5.1.6.3` → OK), and rule 5 requires those positions to be COUNTED — which already yields the byte-rounded integer rule 9 would round up to. Confirmed in code: `BitLayout.ExtentBits` returns `RoundUpToByte` for every group without GROUP-USAGE BIT, so removing rule 9's ceiling would change none of the returned values. No conforming program can therefore distinguish an implementation that applies rule 9 from one that does not, and a golden written for rule 9 would be measuring rule 5. | owner: 2026-09-03 |
| DRV-DOC-A.1-19 | indistinguishable-consequent | GR-14.9.5.4-7 | **A.1 item 19** (result of canceling a non-COBOL program) documents **§14.9.5.4 GR10**, *"If the program to be canceled is other than a COBOL program, the effects of the CANCEL statement are implementor-defined."* (`cite.py --check 14.9.5.4` → OK, rule 10). GR10 governs a program the runtime system LOCATED per **GR2**, *"The program-name is used by the runtime system to locate the program"* (`cite.py --check 14.9.5.4` → OK, rule 2) — and `ProgramTable.Cancel` resolves every target with `probe: false` and returns on a null node, so no locate step runs and the named module's language is never consulted. The effect is therefore byte-identical to **§14.9.5.4 GR7**, *"No action is taken when a CANCEL statement is executed referencing a program that has not been called in this run unit"* (`cite.py --check 14.9.5.4` → OK, rule 7), which the corpus already pins. ⛔ The witness was WRITTEN, in golden-lane round 2, and deleted: its output was invariant under the determination it claimed to measure. The determination itself is stated in §7 and the row names implementing code, so only the witness is impossible — which is why this row is admissible where `kb/Work/PB280` Q2 refused an anchor-only one. | owner: 2026-09-03 |
| DRV-GR-14.9.27.4-12 | unpopulatable-antecedent | the physical-file category set is closed at the two `PhysicalFileCategory` values any connector can report — `SequentialConnector` answers (a) Non-unit and `RelativeConnector`/`IndexedConnector` answer (d), so no medium permits rewinding | **§14.9.27.4 GR12** — *"If the storage medium for the file permits rewinding, the following rules apply: a) When neither the EXTEND, nor the NO REWIND phrase is specified, execution of the OPEN statement causes the file to be positioned at its beginning. b) When the NO REWIND phrase is specified, execution of the OPEN statement does not cause the file to be repositioned …"* (`cite.py --check 14.9.27.4` → OK, rule 12). Its antecedent is FALSE for every supported medium, and that is forced rather than chosen: GR12 and **GR11** partition the media between them — GR11 governs a medium the NO REWIND phrase *"does not apply to"*, which §9.1.13.2 item 6 fixes as the non-reel/unit medium (`cite.py --check 9.1.13.2` → OK, item 6) — so the category (a) placement that makes this compiler's `'07'` correct for `CLOSE REEL/UNIT`, `CLOSE WITH NO REWIND` and `OPEN WITH NO REWIND` selects GR11 and empties GR12. Arm (a)'s observable — an OPEN INPUT positioned at the beginning — is **§14.9.27.4 GR14**'s (*"the file position indicator for that file connector is set to 1"*), which is unconditional, so a golden written for GR12 a) would be measuring GR14. Arm (b) is not merely unimplemented: `FileRegistry.NoRewindPhraseEffect` refuses any category but (a) LOUDLY, so a future rewinding medium cannot inherit this closure silently. ⚠ The vacuity is CONTINGENT on the placement: `CloseTable14Tests.OpenNoRewind_IsTheCloseArmsTwinOnTheSameMedium` fails the moment a connector answers (b) or (c), and this row must then be re-adjudicated. (Signed by the owner on 2026-09-09 — decision Q25, after a plain-language brief; DEVLOG 1577.) | owner: 2026-09-03 |
| DRV-GR-14.9.20.4-9 | undefined-A.2 | A.2 item 20 | **§14.9.20.4 GR9** — *"If identifier-1 occupies the same storage area as identifier-2, the result of the execution of this statement is undefined, even if they are defined by the same data description entry."* (`cite.py --check 14.9.20.4` → OK, rule 9). **Annex A.2 item 20 carries it** ("INITIALIZE statement. The result of the execution of the INITIALIZE statement is undefined if the receiving operand and the sending operand occupies the same storage area even if they are defined by the same data description entry. (14.9.20, INITIALIZE statement, General rule 9)"). The rule WIDENS undefinedness rather than requiring anything: it overrides §14.6.10 rule 2 ("When the data items are described by the same data description entry, the result of the statement is the same as if the data items shared no part of their respective storage areas." — `cite.py --check 14.6.10` → OK, rule 2) so that the overlap is undefined *even then*. Every behaviour conforms, so there is no expected value to compute and any golden would pin an implementation choice. WiseOwl COBOL's behaviour is nevertheless determinate and inside the envelope: `INITIALIZE G REPLACING ALPHANUMERIC DATA BY A1`, where `A1` is subordinate to `G`, compiles and runs, and §14.9.20.4 GR4's implicit MOVEs are executed per elementary item in the GR8 definition order, so a sender that is itself overwritten mid-statement is read as it then stands (measured: `A1`/`B1` both read `AAA`). (Signed by the owner on 2026-09-09 — decision Q26, after a plain-language brief; DEVLOG 1577.) | owner: 2026-09-03 |
| DRV-GR-4.4-1 | definitional | defines the conforming run unit — a property of the USER's run unit (that each of its runtime modules results from a successful compilation of a conforming compilation unit), not an obligation of the implementation | **§4.4 1)** — *"A conforming run unit is one that:"* … *"is composed of one or more runtime modules, each resulting from a successful compilation of a conforming compilation unit, and"* (`cite.py --check 4.4` → OK, 1)). The sentence DEFINES a term, and the term names the user's program: its subject is *a conforming run unit*, it contains no *shall*, and it asks nothing of the implementation. What an implementation owes a run unit is written elsewhere — §4.2's conforming implementation and every rule of Clauses 7–15, each a row of this inventory with its own verdict and witness — so a program cannot observe §4.4 1) apart from those rules, and a golden written "for" it would test them, not it. Owner decision `kb/Work/R43` item 4 (2026-09-24), extending `kb/Work/PB386`; answers `kb/Work/PB468` Q9. | owner: 2026-09-03 |
| DRV-GR-4.4-2 | definitional | defines the conforming run unit's runtime-behaviour half and the standing of undefined results — properties of the USER's run unit, not obligations of the implementation | **§4.4 2)** — *"complies with the explicitly stated provisions and specifications of standard COBOL with respect to the runtime behavior of that run unit"*, with the paragraph that follows it: *"The results of violating the formats or rules of standard COBOL are undefined unless otherwise specified"* and *"The inclusion of non-COBOL components in the run unit does not affect the conformance of the run unit."* (`cite.py --check 4.4` → OK, 2)). Every sentence classifies a USER'S run unit (conforming or not; what is predictable about it); none says what an implementation SHALL do. The runtime behaviour it refers to is specified — and verified row by row — by the rules it points at, and the undefined situations it names are exactly Annex A.2's, which this register's `undefined-A.2` arm already closes rule by rule. Owner decision `kb/Work/R43` item 4 (2026-09-24), extending `kb/Work/PB386`; answers `kb/Work/PB468` Q9. | owner: 2026-09-03 |
| DRV-GR-14.9.28.4-2 | undefined-A.2 | A.2 item 37 | **§14.9.28.4 GR2** — *"The results of executing the following sequence of PERFORM statements are undefined and no exception condition is set to exist when the sequence is executed: a) a PERFORM statement is executed and has not yet terminated, then b) within the range of that PERFORM statement another PERFORM statement is executed, then c) the execution of the second PERFORM statement passes through the exit of the first PERFORM statement."* (`cite.py --check 14.9.28.4` → OK, rule 2). **Annex A.2 item 37 carries it** ("PERFORM statement. The results of the execution of overlapping PERFORM statements are undefined. (14.9.28, PERFORM statement, General rule 2)"). ⛔ The rule's second clause — *no* exception condition is set — cannot be witnessed either, and that is not for want of trying: the only place a witness could observe it is after the sequence has run, and where control goes after (c) is precisely what the first clause leaves undefined, so no expected output line is derivable. NOTE 1 makes the point the standard's own way ("On some implementations it causes stack overflows, on some it causes returns to unlikely places"). WiseOwl COBOL's behaviour is determinate: `DispatchEmitter.EmitDispatchMethod` models no overlap and Table 13 defines no exception-name for it, so nothing can be raised; measured under `>>TURN EC-ALL CHECKING ON`, a `PERFORM PA THRU PB` whose range performs `PC`, which `GO TO PB`, runs PA/PC/PB, falls past PB and exits 0 with no EC and no diagnostic — one admissible realization, and the no-exception half is honoured. (Signed by the owner on 2026-09-09 — decision Q27, after a plain-language brief; DEVLOG 1577.) | owner: 2026-09-03 |
| DRV-RV-15.4.1-4 | unpopulatable-antecedent | the set item 4 governs is CLOSED by the standard itself and is EMPTY: the functions that have an equivalent arithmetic expression are exactly §15.7 ABS, §15.9 ANNUITY, §15.17 COMBINED-DATETIME, §15.23 DATE-TO-YYYYMMDD, §15.25 DAY-TO-YYYYDDD, §15.36 FACTORIAL, §15.60 MEAN, §15.74 PRESENT-VALUE, §15.79 SECONDS-FROM-FORMATTED-TIME, §15.81 SIGN, §15.86 STANDARD-DEVIATION, §15.88 SUM, §15.98 VARIANCE and §15.100 YEAR-TO-YYYY — the fourteen clauses containing “The equivalent arithmetic expression is as follows” — and NOT ONE of those fourteen function clauses contains the words “implementor-defined”, “implementor defines” or “defined by the implementor” anywhere in it | **§15.4.1 NOTE 1 item 4** — *“The result is explicitly implementor-defined”* (`cite.py --check 15.4.1 "The result is explicitly implementor-defined"` → OK, rule 4), the fourth disjunct of *“The result of an equivalent arithmetic expression is implementor-defined if any one or more of the following applies”*, whose normative twin under a standard mode is the same clause’s rule 2 c), *“The returned value is explicitly implementor-defined”* (`--check` → OK, rule 2). **Its antecedent is false for every equivalent arithmetic expression THE STANDARD DEFINES**, so it is unpopulatable before it is ever an implementation question. NOTE 1’s own lead-in scopes item 4 to *the result of an equivalent arithmetic expression*, so it reaches only functions that HAVE one; that set is closed and enumerable, and the enumeration in the Names cell was taken mechanically over §15 rather than recalled. Each of the fourteen was then searched for an explicit implementor-defined result and none carries one. The three functions this row’s own history named — §15.75 RANDOM, §15.80 SECONDS-PAST-MIDNIGHT and §15.38 FORMATTED-CURRENT-DATE — are not members of the set at all: they have no equivalent arithmetic expression, so their implementor-defined results are §4.2.5 Annex A.1 determinations (§7 items 144/145, 171, 112, 87) and instances of §15.4.1’s LAST paragraph (*“When a numeric or integer function does not have an equivalent arithmetic expression, its returned value is implementor-defined”*), never of item 4. §15.27 E and §15.73 PI do make a native returned value an implementor-defined approximation of a stated expression, but that is NOTE 1 item 1 (*“Native arithmetic is in effect”*) and those clauses print no equivalent arithmetic expression either. ⚠ The vacuity is the STANDARD’s, not this implementation’s, so unlike the device- and medium-set rows above it cannot be voided by a later implementation choice — only by a future edition that makes some EAE-bearing function’s returned value explicitly implementor-defined, at which point the rule-digest changes and the row is re-adjudicated by construction. The row’s companion `RV-15.4.1-1` (*“the returned value shall equal the value of the equivalent arithmetic expression”*) carries the observable half and keeps `conformance:2023/exp_standard_decimal_eae`; that test-ref was on THIS row and is cleared here, because a test about rule 1 is not evidence about rule 4. (kb/Work PB245.) | owner: 2026-09-03 |
